# The Book of Raziel the Angel

<margin>**Because all**</margin> who hold this book must keep themselves pure. One must not defile his flesh, nor approach a woman during her menstrual impurity <note>Hebrew: *Niddah*</note>, nor come near a corpse, nor be defiled by any uncircumcised person. He must sanctify his flesh in living water <note>original: *mayim chayim*; refers to a natural spring or ritual bath (mikveh)</note> and purify himself to be like the angels <note>original: *Elohim*; here likely referring to divine beings or angels</note>. He shall not eat anything impure, nor touch any ritual defilement, and he shall act in holiness according to all that is written herein; then he shall succeed.

<margin>**And all**</margin> who are cautioned regarding the letters of the Holy Name, yet do not sanctify themselves or purify their flesh, but touch it without purity, treating it with levity and failing to guard its way—if they willfully desecrate it, they shall be punished and perish from the world. Their portion shall be inherited by shame and become an eternal horror. Their benefit shall not be exchanged, their fire shall not be extinguished, and they shall be cast out from all their loved ones and pushed from light into darkness. But if one follows the fear of his Creator and handles this with purity and holiness, he will merit all its words and succeed in all his deeds. He will be granted favor, kindness, and mercy in the eyes of all who see him, and all his enemies will fear him and his presence, for the Holy Name shall be called upon him. Who is wise? Let him understand these things. Who is discerning? Let him know them. For the ways of the Lord are upright; the righteous walk in them, but the rebellious stumble in them.

<margin>**For success**</margin> in the study of Torah, for repentance, and for the observation of all things through truth: This is the law of the book—it must be used in secret. Whatever you do, do it at sunrise and its appearance. In every month, perform it during the first five days and the middle five days, and you will succeed in all your work.

<margin>**For**</margin> this book is great and praiseworthy. The Lord God revealed it to those who seek Him, so that whoever performs what is in it and guards it as it is written shall live and succeed in all his works.

<margin>**This**</margin> book is pure. Whoever wishes to use it should say the name of the Moon and the name of the Sun—five days during the new moon, five in the middle, and five at the end.

<margin>**The Season**</margin> <term>Season: *Tekufah*; one of the four turning points of the year (equinoxes or solstices)</term> of the first period, when the Sun is in Aries, Taurus, and Gemini, consists of 91 days and 3 hours, and one-fourth and one-third of an hour.
<margin>**The Season**</margin> of the second period—Cancer, Leo, and Virgo—consists of ninety-one days and six hours, and one-fourth of three hours.
<margin>**The Season**</margin> of the fourth period—Capricorn, Aquarius, and Pisces—consists of ninety-one days from the one and four to three hours. <note>The third season (Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius) appears to be skipped or conflated in this specific transcription's layout.</note>

<margin>**If**</margin> you seek to hide treasure in the earth, or if you act by the water: take water and pour it on the ground. Mention the name of the land in every season, and the land will "drink" from your hand, and you will act and succeed.

<margin>**If**</margin> in the first season your soul desires to perform a ritual for the earth's vegetation: say the name of the Sun to its uprightness: *YHV HV AY*. And on the first day of the week say: *Pigen'o Cheker*. The name of the first season is Samael and Anaiel and Ganshurash; the mistress is *AY D Mastir*. The name of the Sun is *Paniot Tehusham*. The zodiac sign is Aries <note>original: *Taleh*</note>, *Sha'atan*. The names of those who rule over the seeds of the earth are: *Yisurin, Churtah, Yuvah, Arkat, Dachriel,* and *Anaiel*. Say these seven names and say: "I request of you, Holy Ones of the worlds, that you do for me all that I desire," which are *Shadan* and *Asran*.

<margin>**If**</margin> in the second season your soul desires to work with trees: say the name of the earth, *Kol Achad*. The name of the mistress is *Eliyan*. On the second day of the week say: *Mochachi*. The names of the angels of the season are: *Nemal'el, Tzadkiel, Aniel*. The mistress is *Abir Abiram*. The name is *YHVH Abyin*. The zodiac sign is Cancer <note>original: *Sartan*</note>, *Kehadar*. The rulers over the trees are *Akaryah* and his great offspring: *Arvathu, Karvithu, Avchalat, Nehalba, Darchatiel*. Say these seven names and cast your mind toward the Master; request from them, and they will act, whether for good or for ill.

<margin>**If**</margin> in the third season your soul desires to bind <note>original: *lemisar*; to restrain or control through magic</note> reptiles: say the name of the reptile and the name of the earth, *Yishshiyah*. The mistress is *Yeham'el*. The name of the East Wind is *Aviyan*. The mistresses are Raphael, Gabriel, and Uriel. On the third day say: *Partan*. The name of the third season is *Shemael, Ashmadah, Gabriel*. The mistress is *Albara AY H*. The name is *YHVH Sufotinin*. The zodiac sign is Libra <note>original: *Moznayim*</note>, *Tishgun*. The rulers over the reptiles are: *Raphael, Rukiel, Gabriel, Tzuriel, YHV YHV, Michael, Shelitiel*. By the staff of the head and the covenant of Abraham, and in the place where Abraham offered sacrifice, and by the names of the two angels who serve—one on his right and one on his left—they fulfill all our commands even unto death. Thus his law is established in his hand, his words are heard, his awe is cast upon all creatures, his possessions prosper, and it causes good for him and all his family until the end of all generations. When God is angry with the world, He sends those on His right to sustain the world. These names are *Markafda* and *Markafael* and seven names of the upright. Lift your hand to heaven, take a vial and fill it with pleasant oil in a vessel of glass; by this, the reptiles of the earth are bound to us.

<margin>**If**</margin> in the fourth season your soul desires to work with animals: say on the fourth day of the week: *Ra'mi Shor Setar*. The name of the fourth season is *Agra, Gaduriel, Barchiel, Ravyu* of *Refa*. The name of the Sun is *Ablad, Ashkor, Kachdon, Shachron, Keldon*. The zodiac sign is Capricorn <note>original: *Gadi*</note>, *Shagad Luy*. The rulers over the animals are *Ahadrir, Machgadiel, Adiel, Zevna, Zarqu:* and seven names. [Perform this] on the day of the animal for [Name] son of [Name], by the holy word: *Hezekyo* and *Hezekyo* of the Sun.

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the practitioner, moving from the general requirements of purity to the specific ritual preparation required to receive a divine vision. It then provides a foundational history of the book’s lineage, beginning with Enoch and then focusing on how the angel Raphael delivered it to Noah to save him from the Flood. It concludes by tracing the book’s transmission through the Hebrew patriarchs and leaders.</meta>

# The Book of Raziel the Angel

When one is pure and clear, with a heart united with his inner being, God <note>literally "the white ones" or "the noble ones," likely referring to the pure of heart or the angels</note> will fill them and comfort them with many comforts. He shall go to the rivers before the rising of the dawn, wrapped in a white garment, standing and walking barefoot. He shall mention the names of the angels who serve in that specific month and burn a portion of the offerings three times each day. On the third day, he shall take all the ashes and scatter them on the ground inside his house and sleep upon them. He shall recite over himself the names of the mighty, ordained, heroic, holy, and ruling angels, and then go to sleep. He must not speak with any person. Then the angels will come to him in the night in a vision and a sight, not in riddles, and they will inform him and reveal to him every matter which he asks without fear.

<margin>**And after**</margin> four generations had passed, Enoch son of Jared <note>A biblical figure who "walked with God" and, according to tradition, was transformed into the angel Metatron</note> arose. He gained wisdom through the fear of God and conducted himself in purity. He would wash and sanctify himself in living water <note>original: *mayim chayim*; refers to a natural spring or ritual bath</note> and offer supplications before the Creator of all. The Place <note>original: *HaMakom*; a traditional Jewish title for God, suggesting He is the "place" where the world exists</note> was revealed to him in a dream, telling him of the Book, the way he should conduct himself, what his work should be, and the holiness of his purity. He woke up and went to a cave and remained secluded until noon. From the strength of the sun, his soul entered there so that the people of the place would not perceive him. Before God, blessed be He, he prayed; he ascended in purity and took hold of the Pure Name. In the hour that he understood it, his eyes were enlightened in all his ways. He conducted himself by it and walked until he resembled the holy ones of the heights <note>the angels</note>. He was separated from the life of the earth, and "he was not, for God took him" <note>quoting Genesis 5:24</note>.

<margin>**Because in this book**</margin> he became wise and cunning regarding the cycles <note>astronomical seasons or equinoxes/solstices</note> and the constellations, and all the luminaries that serve in each and every month; the names called for every season, and the angels that serve in the four seasons of the year. He understood the names of the earth and the names of the heavens, and also the names of the sun and the moon. He increased his honor with all his strength and understood all wisdom even more than Adam, the first man. He understood that the generations coming after him would not have the strength to withstand its power, for it is immense and honored. He hid it away until the time of Noah, son of Lamech, who was a righteous and wholehearted man in his generations. In the six-hundredth year of his life, the earth was corrupted by acts of violence and generations of sin, and "all flesh had corrupted its way upon the earth." The cry of the earth ascended to the heavens before the Throne of Glory of the Holy One, Blessed be He, and Noah found favor in the eyes of the Lord.

<margin>**And then**</margin> Raphael the holy Prince <note>the archangel Raphael, whose name means "God heals"</note> was sent to him. He said to him: "I have been sent by the word of God to you, to heal the earth, to inform you of what shall be and what shall be done, and to provide escape." Then he gave him this Holy Book and made him understand how he should conduct himself with it, what his work should be, and the holiness of his purity.

<margin>**And he said**</margin> to him: "Hear the words of the Lord, for you have been found to be a righteous and wholehearted man in your generations. Behold, I have given you this Holy Book, and I have informed you of all its secrets and mysteries, so that you may use it in holiness, purity, modesty, and humility. From it you shall learn how to make an Ark of gopher wood <note>a mysterious wood used for Noah's Ark</note>, and you shall enter it—you, your sons, your wife, and your sons' wives—to hide for a brief moment until the wrath has passed." Noah took the book from the hand of Raphael the holy Prince. At the moment he understood the engraved letters, the Spirit of the Lord rested upon him. He made the Ark in its length and breadth through the knowledge he gained from this Holy Name. Then it was heard by him: "Come, you and all your household, into the Ark, for I have seen you as righteous."

<margin>**And then**</margin> Noah son of Lamech hid the book before entering the Ark. Through its signs, he understood how to bring into the Ark two and seven <note>referring to the clean and unclean animals</note>, a male and his mate. The Lord shut the door behind him. The flood lasted forty days and forty nights. Then Noah opened his mouth with a spirit of wisdom and understanding and blessed the Lord God, the Great, Mighty, and Awesome King.

<margin>**And he said: Blessed be the Lord**</margin> who has given of His wisdom to those who fear His Name. Blessed is He who gives man dominion through his fear, who saves the souls of His pious ones, who hides those who take refuge in His shadow, and leaves a remnant to keep the vision alive. He gives His servants a spirit of intellect and understanding to speak with every man, beast, animal, bird, creeping thing, and fish, to make known Your strength and the greatness of Your power. From the great intellect he gained from this book, he knew whether it was day or night, and he knew the proper time for the food of every single animal. His prayer was heard before the Throne of Glory, and he and all who were with him in the Ark were remembered. God caused a wind to pass over the earth, the waters subsided, and the Ark rested in the Month of Radiance <note>original: *Chodesh HaZohar*; likely referring to the month of Iyar/Ziv, known for its brightness</note> upon the mountains of Ararat.

<margin>**In the wisdom**</margin> of this book, Noah conducted himself. He made it known to Shem, his son, for from it he gained understanding while in the Ark. Shem his son followed it and held fast to it in its holiness. After him, Shem delivered it to Abraham, Abraham to Isaac, Isaac to Jacob, Jacob to Levi, Levi to Moses, Moses to Aaron, Aaron to Phinehas, and Phinehas to his son, and so through all the generations, one after another.

<margin>**And anyone**</margin> who merits it—happy is he and it is good for him, for it has fallen to his portion and lot to know it and to gain wisdom from it. For it is not given to everyone, nor will all men understand or become wise through it, for wisdom comes from the fear of the Lord, and it remains with the wise man. A heart of understanding shall stand over its foundations. He who holds this book in purity shall not stumble, his face shall not grow pale, no enemy shall prevail over him, and workers of iniquity shall not confront him. He shall spend his days in goodness, and evil counsel shall be frustrated from him, for he has found a good life. Happy is he, for he has found life, and in his death, he finds rest. Demons and evil encounters shall not snatch him away, and he shall be saved from an evil person. Blessing shall come quickly to his house, and he shall tread upon the high places of his enemies. His name shall be remembered for good and blessing, for he was careful with the Holy Name. He shall find favor in the eyes of all who see him, and good success in the eyes of God and man. His soul shall be delivered from every evil death, for he practiced charity in his life and rebuked in secret. Fear shall depart from him because he gave his strength in secret before kings. He shall be praised for the uprightness of the Name of the King of kings, the Holy One, Blessed be He.

<summary>This page recounts the legendary history of the Book of Raziel, explaining how it was used by Enoch to transcend the physical world and later given by the angel Raphael to Noah to provide the wisdom needed to build the Ark and survive the Flood. It concludes by tracing the book's lineage through the biblical Patriarchs and Moses, emphasizing the divine protection and success granted to its righteous keepers.</summary>

# The Book of Raziel the Angel

And the zodiac sign of Pisces in wine-red <note>original: *be-chamra*; can mean wine or redness, referring to the color associated with the sign</note>, and these are their names: Y-H, Kachutiel, and Vehayin, and the seven names, and so on.

<margin>**And if**</margin> a day is to be cast <note>possibly referring to casting lots or determining an auspicious time</note>, one should remember seven days besides the first, and say the name of the first day of the week: *Ivriya*. Its eastern wind is *Kundar*.
Its ministers are Gabriel, Raphael, and Uriel. The name of the second season <term>Tekufah: one of the four seasons of the year, usually marked by an equinox or solstice</term> is *Nan'el* and *Tzadkiel*, and its ministers are *Abir* and *Abiram*.
And the name of YHWH is *Ankin*, and the zodiac sign is Cancer, *Kehaher*, and its seven kinds. And say: *H-H, Ya-H-H, Yadid Arucha, Yadar, Yehutzura, Yair, Abras'khem, Y-H, Y-H, Y-H, Y-H, Ehyeh Asher Ehyeh* <note>original: "I am that I am," the name revealed to Moses at the burning bush</note>, the sparks of YHWH, and the Merciful One answer me, the salvations of God. And I found the holy one who mentions to the people in rest and light and the seven names.

<margin>**And if**</margin> you desire to pass through during the day, say the name of the day in its season; and if at night, in the season of the night. And if on the earth, say the name of YHWH in the season of the day; and if at night, say the name of YHWH in the season of the night.
<margin>**And if**</margin> an animal is bound <note>likely meaning "captured" or "under a spell"</note>, say the name of the day; and if at night, say the name of the animal in the season of the night.
<margin>**And if**</margin> a creeping thing is bound, say its name in the season of the day; and if at night, say its name in the season of the night.
<margin>**And if**</margin> a man is bound, say his name in the season of the day; and if at night, say his name in the season of the night.
<margin>**And if**</margin> a man is delivered <note>original: *masur*; meaning betrayed or handed over to authorities</note>, say his name in the season of the day; and if at night, say his name in the season of the night. And be sure to say the name of the animal in the season of the night, and if it is the sun, in the season of the day.

<margin>**And these**</margin> are the angels who serve in the first season: Samael, Anael, and Neshorash; and the name of their ministers who are appointed over them is *Akassar*.
<margin>**And these**</margin> are the ones who serve in the four seasons; apart from these you shall not work, for apart from these you shall not succeed.
<margin>**And these**</margin> are the ones who serve in the <insert>second</insert> season: Samael, Tzadkiel, and Anael. The name of their ministers is *Abir* and *Abiram*. Apart from these you shall not work in the second season <unclear>Radad</unclear>, or you shall not succeed.

<margin>**And these**</margin> are the angels who serve in the third season: Barakael, Urmiel, and Gabriel; and their ministers are *El-Bara-Or*. Apart from these you shall not work, for you shall not succeed.
<margin>**And these**</margin> are the angels who serve in the fourth season: Uriel, Barael, and Kerubiel; and their ministers are *Rahahma*. Apart from these you shall not work, and if you do work, you shall not succeed.
<margin>**And these**</margin> are the angels who serve in the nights of the first season: Sha'anin, Charutz, Shargeman, and Shekhazman. The name of their minister is *Asael*, and the name of the moon is *Labarnit*. With these angels, perform your work in the nights of the first season.
<margin>**And these**</margin> are the ones appointed over the second nights: Ashasha, Atradman, and Shachnanan. Their minister is *Labarmak*, and the name of the moon is *Olar*.
<margin>**And these**</margin> are the angels appointed over the third nights: Plaim, Vedor-Gal, and Shat-Gasherin. Their minister is *Pania*, and the name of the moon is *Rat*.
<margin>**And these**</margin> are the angels appointed over the fourth nights: Ashandon, Radufael, and Shahar-Litz. Their ministers are *Gadiel* and *Sharin*.
<margin>**These**</margin> are the names that serve in the four seasons: In the first season, in Aries, Taurus, and Gemini—the name of the sun is called *Or Bamaftara*. In the second season, in Cancer, Leo, and Virgo—the name of the sun is called *Akhtamim*. In the third season, in Libra, Scorpio, and Sagittarius—the name of the sun is called *Avoya-Or*. In the fourth season, in Capricorn, Aquarius, and Pisces—the name of the sun is called *Askon*.

<margin>**And these**</margin> are the four names for the heavens in every season: In the first season, *Ason-Or*. In the second, *Height of the Firmament*. In the third, *Spreading Splendor*. In the fourth, *Cloud Fountain*.

<margin>**These are**</margin> the names of the heavens in their seasons; if you are to do any matter, mention the name of the heaven at the start of each season.
<margin>**These are**</margin> the names of the earth in the four turnings <note>seasons</note> of the year: In the first season, *Mamman*. In the second, *Dry Land*. In the third, *The World*. In the fourth, *The South*.
<margin>**These are**</margin> the names of the four winds of the world in their seasons: In the first season, *Akakron*. Second, *Kahrabakh*. Third, *Gavriya*. Fourth, *Artari*.
<margin>**And the name of the north wind**</margin> in its seasons: In the first season, *Amuna*. In the second, *Abron*. In the third, *Gulgaduda*. In the fourth, *Daspor*.
<margin>**And the name of the east wind**</margin> in the first season, *Avnon*. In the second, *Karbakh*. In the third, *Avdut*. In the fourth, *Begirtu*.
<margin>**And the name of the west wind**</margin> in the first season, *Katnas*. In the second, *Kangor*. In the third, *Zehior*. In the fourth, *Dri'or*.
<margin>**And the name of the south wind**</margin> in the first season, *Manshur*. In the second, *Alfron*. In the third, *Matniel*. In the fourth, *Tamhor*.

And these are the names of the ministers of the stars who serve in all the dwellings and serve in the seven dwellings of the heights:

-># The Book of Raziel the Angel<-

# And these
are the names of the stars of the zodiac for each and every season <term>Tekufah: one of the four seasons of the year</term>. In the first, the name of Aries is *Sha’atan*; Taurus is *Uvahan*; Gemini is *Shegadas*.

# In the second
season: Cancer is *Kehar*; Leo is *Shagnas*; Virgo is *Yihyah*. In the third season: Libra is *Tashgaz*; Scorpio is *Geiharan*; Sagittarius is *Akhtan*. In the fourth season: Capricorn is *Shegluy*; Aquarius is *Akhtarel*; Pisces is *Kahmira*. These are the names of the zodiac signs in their progression with the Sun. The Sun serves with all the signs, staying thirty days with each sign, and she completes the cycle of all the signs within twelve months.

# And these
are the names of the seasons: In the first, *Sha’atan*, *Toran*, *Shagram*, and *Vashyah*, Gabriel <note>The archangel Gabriel is often associated with the moon or water, but here governs a seasonal turning point</note>. And the name of the Moon is *Levanit*. In the second: *Yeshu’yah*, *Abrahin*, and *Shahakan*, and *Shahakna’el*, *Ba’el*, *Mana’el*. And the name of the olive trees <note>original: *zeiti*; this may refer to a specific agricultural timing or a mystical substitute name</note> is *Seltatlav*. On the second day, *Dothah*. In the third: *Pel’am*, *Katrona’el*, and *Sarah*. There are *Narin*, *Ranana’el*, *Shadran*, *Rani’el*, *Shahari’f*.

# And these
are the names of the stars of the zodiac for each season. [Season 1:] Aries is *Dum*; Taurus is *Davron*; Gemini is *Shagron*; Cancer is *Masrahim*; Leo is *Latzunkhar*; Virgo is *Anatz Kanan*; Libra is *Tzadki’el*; Scorpio is *Harfat*; Sagittarius is *Tzukar*; Capricorn is *Meshgadim*; Aquarius is *Mansagas*; Pisces is *She’afnu*. In the second [season]: Aries is *Bahami*; Taurus is *Mazkiran*; Gemini is *Biharnan*; Cancer is *Keharar*; Leo is *Shaghad*; Virgo is *Yihyah*.

# Libra
is *Shekatin*; Scorpio is *Kagazkar*; Sagittarius is *Dabtan*; Capricorn is *Yishah*; Aquarius is *Akhtaron*; Pisces is *Anisiyon*. In the third [season]: Aries is *Pelonah*; Taurus is *Tadni’el*; Gemini is *Yalkad*; Cancer is *Kadsiyon*; Leo is *Okad*; Virgo is *Kanehani*; Libra is *Tashganaz*; Scorpio is *Kohanan*; Sagittarius is *Avnor*; Capricorn is *Shavivan*; Aquarius is *Maspari*; Pisces is *Pahadi*. And in the fourth [season]: Aries and Taurus are *Kanush Amina*; Gemini is *Ashgadin*; Cancer is *Mahamtin*; Leo is *Aitavin*; Virgo is *Shaghon*; Libra is *Shaku*; Scorpio is *Badur Bashi*; and Sagittarius is *Tipna*; Capricorn is *Shegraloy*; Aquarius is *Antara*; Pisces is *Kahmahnagaz*. And she <note>referring to the Sun or the governing force</note> serves with each and every sign and completes all the signs for thirty days, which is two and a half <note>original: *trein u-falga*; possibly referring to the lunar cycle calculation of 2.5 days per sign per month</note>.

# And these
are the names of the days in the first season: the first day of the week <note>Sunday</note> is *Pignon*; the second is *Sanvin*; the third is *Kuftanin*; the fourth is *Ma’akal Had*. In the [second] season: the second day is *Ta’ashar*; the second <note>likely meaning the next period</note> is *Manhatur*; the third is *Kalantarin*; in the fourth, *Shagrin*. In the [third] season: the third day is *Sharu’ahan*; in the second, *Kehavrand*; in the third, *Pazdan*; in the fourth, *Haglumot*.

# In the [fourth]
season: the fourth day is *Peniz Le’or*; in the second, *Mishor*; in the third, *Vegadim*; in the fourth, *Betakra*. In the [first] season: the name of the fifth day <note>Thursday</note> is *Kadmanur*; in the second, *Azravin*; [third], *Bakufates*; in the fourth, *Ba’arivraro*. In the [first] season: the name of the sixth day <note>Friday</note> is *Kala’arzan*; in the second, *Doktokh*; [third], *Akhtanur*; in the fourth, *Aryeh*.

# And these
are the names of the Earth in their seasons: In the first season, the name of the Earth is *Mamgin*; in the second, *Yabashah* <note>original: "Dry Land"</note>; in the third, *Haval*; in the fourth, *Havah Rum*.

# And these
are the names of the spirits of the Earth in their seasons: In the first season, the name of the spirit is *Mahmad Laz*; and its minister is *YHV YHV Ayn*. In the second, *Kol Ehad* <note>original: "Each One"</note> and its minister is *Yahmaliyah Me’ot*. In the fourth, *Or Barukh Yavo’va’ot*.

# And these
are the names of the spirits of the Earth in the first season: the name of the East wind is *Akbarin*, and its minister is *Akbarin*, Gabriel, Raphael, Uriel. In the third: Uriel, and its minister is Gabriel. In the fourth: *Sidrahin*, and its minister is Gabriel, Raphael, Uriel. In the first season—

# The season
name of the West wind is *Kehalord*; in the second, *Tipufo*; in the third, *Sahmorbi*; in the fourth, *Mifaneh*; and their ministers are *Dari’el*, *Yo’el*, *Kemash*. In the first, the name of the North wind is *Amunim*; in the second, *Deslani*; in the third, *Areheh*; and in the fourth, *Mifani’el*; and their ministers are *Azri’el*, *Zakhri’el*, *Shema’i’el*. In the first season, the name of the South wind is *Mifnei Shor*; in the second, *Baldanin*; in the third, *Afusin*; in the fourth, *Yahaldark*; and their ministers are *Kokhavi’el*, *Shar’el*, *Micha’el*.

# And these
are the names of the angels for each and every season; these are the guardians of the four winds of the world in their seasons. In the first season, the name of the East wind is *Akbahon*; in the second, *Kehavz D’*; in the third, *Avrati*; in the fourth, *Berikhot*.

# And these
are the names of the North wind: in the first season, the name of the North wind is *Amarnah*; in the second, *Avran*; in the third, *Gazoro Kad*; in the fourth, *Kippur*. And these are the names of the West wind: in the first season, the name of the West wind is *Moznayim* <note>original: Libra</note>; in the second, *Sigur*; in the third, *Zahovor*; in the fourth, *Or*. And these are the names of the South wind: in the first season, the name of the South wind is *Manshor*; in the second, *Penimur*; in the third, *Tenufot*; in the fourth, *Tekakhor*.

# If
you wish to cause a person to wander <note>original: *she-yitaltel*; to uproot or displace</note> from his place, or to return a slave who has fled, or to divide the thoughts of your friend or your enemy—and it shall be effective for the four winds of the world against his will, and he shall be an exile in the world against his will—speak the names of each and every wind in its place <note>facing that direction at the correct time</note>, and you will receive him immediately. You shall do your desire and succeed in all your deeds, in every matter, but only in a state of purity.

# And these
are the names of the Sea in the four seasons of the year: In the first season, the name of the Sea is *Balid*; in the second, *Tehom* <note>original: "Abyss" or "The Deep"</note>; in the third, *Mardi’a*; in the fourth, *Shevil Gader*.

# And these
are the names of the Sea in the first season: *Argaz* and its minister is *Batmi’el*. And in the second, *Bahagfal* and its minister is *Pelip’el*. In the third, *Pisarpanon* and its minister is *Tatdali’el*. In the fourth, *Afadtan* and its minister is *Harvanol’el*.

# And if
you wish to cast something into the sea, or to perform a deed within the sea, write the name of the sea for each and every season, and give [it] into the sea
in His name <meta>Catchword: בשמו (By His Name)</meta>

# The Book of Raziel the Angel

...in His name, and He will receive it from your hand, and you will be answered and succeed in every single thing. Perform [these rituals] in purity and holiness:

<margin>And these</margin> are the names of the depths: In the first [level] is Tziktehu <note>original: "נתקתהו" - possibly a corruption of a name like Tziktehu</note> and the minister Tzafniel; in the second is Simpson and the minister Athiel; in the third is Asher Shen and the minister Ababaot; in the fourth is Ha-Itov and the minister Pniel.

<margin>And these</margin> are the names of the angels who serve in fire and flame: Malkiel, Raphadiel, Guriel, Amania, Araliel, Nuriel, Yuriel.

<margin>If</margin> you seek to perform a task and walk through fire, or enter into fire, or extinguish fire, recite the name of the season <term>Tekufah: An astrological season or turning point of the sun</term> and the names of the seven angels who are appointed over the rivers of fire. Whatever you desire they will do; they will receive [the request] from your hand and perform all your will.

<margin>And these</margin> are the angels who serve in the sea and all the creatures within it: Bukotya, Zabortya, Tarkiya. If you seek to cast something into the sea, write these names and "Katil Regyona" <note>likely a magical formula or angelic name</note> along with the names of the season and cast it into the sea. You may perform your desire, even if it is for harm <note>The text suggests these formulas can be used for both "good" and "bad" magic</note>, but it must be written on a plate of silver.

<margin>These</margin> are the angels who rule over the rain. If there is a drought and you seek to pray for rain, do this: pray and speak in the name of the constellations: Malkiel, Marmarmot, Tohtiel, Sagniel, Kakhtiel, Taniel, Gadiel, Karmiel, Yeshayah, Matriel, Lityah. Recite [the names of] these angels seven times. Take a white rooster <term>Sekhvi: A literary Hebrew term for a rooster</term> in purity and cleanliness, slaughter it, cover its blood, remove its limbs and take out its entrails. Fill it with myrrh, frankincense, saffron, good peppers, white pepper, honey, milk, and old wine. Stand facing the sun in the wind with focused intent and say: "I request of you, holy supreme ones, who bring down dew and rain, that you open the gates of the storehouses of blessing and bring down rain, and make the world flourish from the storehouse of blessing."

<margin>And if</margin> you are not answered by these, quickly fall on your face to the ground and mention the prayer, and afterward mention the honored, awesome, and holy Name. Libate the milk, the honey, and the wine seven times, and the rain will descend quickly, and the world will be renewed. Do all your deeds with caution, purity, and modesty, and you shall succeed.

<margin>These</margin> are the angels who rule over all evil beasts, such as bears, lions, and wolves. For one who mentions [these names] against them, the earth will shut and seal their mouths from you. These are: Machniel, Aphtiel, Bahiel, and Mechmiel. These beasts are appointed by the word <note>The text shifts briefly to Aramaic: "these animals are appointed by the word..."</note> of the Great Holy One, Kushiel and Shamshiel. Pass by and do not fear.

<margin>And if</margin> you are traveling on the road and you see the "wheels" <note>original: "Gilgalim" - could refer to celestial spheres, demonic manifestations, or whirlwind-like entities</note> coming toward you, place your hand on your right side and call the name of the spirit through which they come upon you, and afterward say the Name in reverse, and they will all turn back from you.

<margin>And if</margin> you fall into a gathering of evil people and understand that they have plotted to kill you and you have no savior, the honored and awesome Name shall be your savior. Place your hand on your side and say the name of the sun in its season, and the Name from its beginning exactly as it is written in its script and its letters, and you will benefit from it in uprightness and escape.

<margin>And if</margin> you write it on a tablet of gold, all evil thoughts and human schemes will be removed from you. If you are walking on the road and attackers <note>original: "Rofphim" - likely meaning bandits or those who cause trembling</note> fall upon you, and you see them before they see you, turn from the path <gloss>in the fields</gloss> and place both your hands on your head and call upon the sun in its season, and they will not see you.

<margin>And if</margin> they see you before you see them, say the Name three times and they will no longer see you. If they seize you and you fall into their hands, say the Name in reverse and you will escape.

<margin>And if</margin> you are in a house and it falls upon you, hurry and call the name of the earth in its season and the Name in reverse, and the stones and wood will be lifted from you, or whatever else might fall on you, and you will escape without blemish.

<margin>And if</margin> you are in a ship at sea and there is a great storm, and you see there is no place to flee or escape, call the name of the sea in its season and mention the honored and awesome Name over them; the storm will stand still in silence and you will be saved.

<margin>And if</margin> you stand in judgment over a matter and you see your enemy with your eyes, place <gloss>witnesses</gloss> your hands over your right ear and speak and adjure by the letters of the Name that they should rise into your heart, and you will be vindicated in the judgment.

<margin>And if</margin> your words are not heard, and you know that there is a transgression or sin in your hand, or a flaw of shame or another commandment [neglected], lie down and pray the prayer mentioned in this book and mention the Name that it should rise into your heart; your enemies will be broken before you and you will be vindicated.

<margin>And if</margin> you seek to see the light of the sun as it rises in the chariot <note>original: "Merkavah" - the celestial chariot of the sun</note>, say these: the name of the sun in its season, and mention the holy and pure Name against it. The sun will pass, and you will see it like a groom coming out of his canopy. Ask of it whatever you desire, and it will give to you. Afterward, say "He who looks at the earth and it trembles," and wake up and speak this Name in reverse, and release it to its path.

<margin>And if</margin> you desire to stand over a hidden treasure to bring up silver and gold hidden in the earth, take a plate of gold refined seven times and write upon it <gloss>in the field</gloss> a letter from this holy and pure Name. Tie it with a blue thread. Then bring a white pigeon, tie a blue thread around its neck, and release it into the air in the place where it will descend to search with its feet for a pit. If it is within the city, it will stand on the roof. Come and sweep the place seven times. If by day, say the name of the sun in its season; if by night, say the name of the moon in its season.

<meta>This page continues the mystical exploration of the Divine Names, shifting from the practical use of the Names to their symbolic and numerical significance in the structure of the human body, the lives of the Patriarchs, and the ritual objects of the Mezuzah and Tefillin.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>
<header>The Book of Raziel the Great</header>

# The Book of Raziel the Great

"May God Almighty <note>original: "Shaddai"</note> be at your hand, the God of your father, and may He help you; and may God Almighty bless you with fruitfulness and multiplication." "God Almighty appeared to me in Luz... behold, I will make you fruitful and multiply you." Why is this so? Because when you calculate the hidden letters of the name *Shaddai* <note>The letters used to spell out the name: Shin (S-Y-N), Dalet (D-L-T), Yod (Y-V-D). The "hidden" parts are the extra letters</note>, removing the revealed letters, the letters Y, N, L, T, Y, and D equal 500. A person is obligated to father a son and a daughter, who together possess 500 limbs: 248 for the male and 252 for the female. The female has four more than the male, located around the womb. Likewise, the phrase "Be fruitful and multiply" <note>original: "Pru U’rvu"</note> has a numerical value <term>Gematria: A system where Hebrew letters represent numbers</term> of 500, because that specific Name is over fruitfulness and multiplication. Therefore, when people pray ⬜ for children, they should pray using this Name. Similarly, the world is a journey of 500 years. 

The secrets hidden in the name *Shaddai* were revealed when the heavens were joined and expanded. God said to the heavens, "Enough!" <note>original: "Dai," a play on the name Shaddai</note>, for without the name *Shaddai*, there would be no limit to His service. *Shaddai* is mentioned by the prophets of the nations in "the vision of the Almighty" <note>original: "Machazeh Shaddai"</note>, "he who sees the vision of the Almighty," and "he who abandons the fear of the Almighty." The name *Shaddai* also guards against harmful spirits, as it is said, "He shall abide in the shadow of the Almighty" in the "Psalm of Mishaps" <note>Psalm 91, often used for protection</note>. For this reason, we write *Shaddai* on the Mezuzah, and on the outside of the Mezuzah case, we make an opening so that the name *Shaddai* can be read and recognized to drive away harmful spirits. This is because the name of the Lord <note>original: "YHVH"</note> in the Atbash cipher <term>Atbash: A substitution cipher where the first letter is replaced by the last, the second by the second-to-last, etc.</term> becomes *Mtzp"tz*. The letter *Shin* (300) from *Shaddai* corresponds to the *Shin* (300) found in the ciphered Holy Name. This is sufficient for the wise. It also corresponds to the 14 letters of the phrase *Kuzu B'muchsaz* <note>original: "כוזו במוכסז"</note>.

I will now explain to you the secret of these 14 letters of the Holy Name: *Kuzu* is the name *YHVH* (The Lord) in a "neighboring" letter cipher <note>where each letter is replaced by the one following it in the alphabet</note>. The *Kaf* follows *Yod*, the *Vav* follows *Heh*, the *Zayin* follows *Vav*, and the *Vav* follows *Heh*. Similarly, *Bmuchsaz* corresponds to "Eloheinu" (Our God): *Bet* follows *Aleph*, *Mem* follows *Lamed*, *Vav* follows *Heh*, *Kaf* follows *Yod*, *Samekh* follows *Nun*, and *Zayin* follows *Vav*. Thus, these 14 letters correspond to the three names "The Lord, Our God, The Lord" <note>original: "YHVH Eloheinu YHVH"</note>. This is why we write these 14 letters on the reverse side [of the Mezuzah parchment] corresponding to those names. We also place the name *Shaddai* in the Tefillin <term>Tefillin: Ritual leather boxes containing Torah verses, worn during prayer</term>: the letter *Shin* is on the head-piece, the *Dalet* is at the knot on the neck, and the *Yod* is on the hand. *Shaddai* also corresponds to the Patriarchs: Jacob, Isaac, and Abraham. The final letters of their names <note>original: "Yaakov, Yitzchak, Avraham"</note> in the Atbash cipher equal *Shaddai*. If you add the count of the Torah and the people of Israel (who are called "Jacob"), the total is 216. If you take the first and last letters of the Patriarchs—Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob—and Israel, the total is 216. Likewise, the 13 letters of the three Patriarchs' names correspond to the Thirteen Attributes of Mercy. There are also three *Yods* and one *Aleph*, corresponding to the Divine Name of 63 <note>A specific expansion of the Tetragrammaton associated with the Sephirah of Binah/Understanding</note>.

Therefore, it is said: "And I appeared to Abraham, to Isaac, and to Jacob as God Almighty." Furthermore, the sages said that the "hidden" numerical value of *Shaddai* is 500, and the years of the Patriarchs' lives also relate to 500: Abraham (175) + Isaac (180) + Jacob (147) = 502, corresponding to the Heavens, the Earth, and the 500-year journey. This is what is meant by "to give to your forefathers... as the days of the heavens above the earth." He explained that "the days of the heavens above the earth" refers to a journey of 500 years, which was the length of the Patriarchs' combined lives. 

The letter *Shin* on the head-Tefillin is equal to Jacob, as it is written: "Israel, in whom I will be glorified." Tefillin are called "Glory" <note>original: "Pe’er"</note>, as it is said, "Bind your glory upon you." During the destruction of the Temple, they were cast to the ground, as it is written: "He has cast down from heaven to earth the glory of Israel." The word "Tefillin" also contains the letters of "Glory." Because of this loss of glory, on the fast of the Ninth of Av, we place ashes in the spot where the Tefillin are worn, until the days of the Messiah, when we shall "give them glory instead of ashes." 

The *Sefer Yetzirah* <term>The Book of Creation: An ancient mystical text</term> says: "He made the letter *Shin* king, tied a crown to it, and formed the Heavens with it," for the Heavens are made of fire <note>original: "Esh"</note>. Thus, the image of Jacob is in the Heavens, and through the letter *Shin* (which starts the word for fire), He created fire. It is written: "The house of Jacob shall be a fire." Thus, the *Shin* corresponds to them. Jacob also wrestled with an angel of fire. Furthermore, the *Shin* has three branches, corresponding to his three names: Jacob, Israel, and Yeshurun. Moreover, the *Shin* is on the Tefillin cases because his likeness is engraved on the Throne of Glory; he is like the Cherub upon whom God rode when He came to Egypt, as it is written: "And He rode upon a Cherub and flew." This is the meaning of "And Israel saw the mighty hand." It is written "And Israel saw the great hand"—referring to Israel the Elder <note>the Patriarch Jacob</note>. The people of Israel saw the form of Jacob at the Sea and sang: "Then Israel sang... The Lord shall reign forever and ever." Why Jacob's form? Because he represented Truth, as it is written: "You give truth to Jacob." No spiritual "waste" <note>original: "Psulot" - meaning wicked descendants</note> came from him. Regarding his daughter Dinah, who was conceived by Shechem: she gave birth to Asenath, the daughter of Poti-Phera. The tribes wanted to kill her so that people would not say there was immorality in the house of Jacob. Jacob wrote the Great Name, engraved it on a golden plate <note>original: "Tzitz"</note>, and the angel Michael took her and brought her to the wife of Poti-Phera, who raised her as her daughter; later, Joseph married her. Around the *Shin* of the Tefillin, there are 12 stitches, corresponding to the twelve tribes, with three stitches on each side. Similarly, three tribes were at each foot of Jacob's bed when he was carried out of Egypt.

The letter *Dalet* corresponds to the neck, representing Isaac, who stretched out his neck to be slaughtered to sanctify the Name of the Holy One, Blessed be He. The *Dalet* is on the nape of the neck; just as the entire neck is valid for ritual slaughter, the entire nape is valid for the "pinching" of a bird sacrifice <note>original: "Melikah"</note>. Therefore, one shaves the hair on the neck so that the Tefillin strap sits directly on the skin; this is interpreted as a reference to Isaac. The verse "The hair <note>original: "Dalat"</note> of your head is like purple" also alludes to the *Dalet* corresponding to the neck. Furthermore, the *Dalet* extends upwards and downwards because "Zevul" <note>The fourth of the seven heavens</note> is the fourth firmament, where the Temple is built. Similarly, the Tabernacle had four coverings: 1) Blue hangings, 2) Goat hair, 3) Ram skins, and 4) Tachash skins, with the Divine Presence <note>original: "Shekhinah"</note> beneath them. Isaac was bound upon the altar, which corresponds to the Altar on High; therefore, the *Dalet* (the number 4) corresponds to him. The *Sefer Yetzirah* says: "He made the letter *Dalet* king, tied a knot for it, and created Mars in the fifth firmament." Mars is close to the Sun and receives from the Sun heat and dryness like fire. Thus, the *Dalet* corresponds to Isaac, who was an "ascent-offering to the fires." Mars is dry as fire and is appointed over heat, wrath, and anger. Therefore, when the Holy One, Blessed be He, is angry, He remembers Isaac as if he were being burned, as it is written: "May He turn your burnt offering to ashes, Selah"—this refers to the ashes of Isaac.

The letter *Yod* corresponds to Abraham, who was tested with ten <note>The value of Yod</note> trials. This is seen in the Tefillin of the hand, placed near the "house" <note>the body</note>, signifying that "Abraham was one" <note>singular in his faith</note>. The *Sefer Yetzirah* says: "He made the letter *Yod* king, tied a crown-knot to it, and created Virgo with it." Just as Abraham was as dear to his Creator as a groom to a bride who is a virgin. It was created with ⬜ the *Yod* as a kidney, for counsel and advice come from the kidneys, as it is written: "My kidneys instruct me," and "He shall remove it toward the counsel/kidneys." This corresponds to the kidneys which give advice. It is said in the *Midrash Shocher Tov* regarding Abraham: ⬜ "I have lifted my hand to the Lord." The *Yod* also corresponds to the heart, as it is written: "And You found his heart faithful before You." Furthermore, the name of the Lord <note>original: "Y-Y"</note> corresponds to Abraham because the Holy One, Blessed be He, foresaw...

<meta>This page serves as a formal introduction to the "Secrets of Raziel." It outlines the ethical and spiritual prerequisites for studying these mysteries and establishes a framework for understanding the Divine through three distinct lenses: Love, Petition, and Fear. It continues the previous page's focus on the name *Shaddai* by exploring how human intention in prayer connects to different aspects of God's power.</meta>

# This is the Great Book of Raziel

**Blessed** is the Wise One of secrets, who has shared of His wisdom with those who fear Him, and has given us a Torah of truth to make known to mankind His mighty deeds and the glory of the majesty of His kingdom, and the power of His works—the foundation of the upper and lower worlds. It is the glory of God to conceal a matter; keep honey and milk under your tongue <note>A metaphor for keeping mystical secrets hidden, based on Song of Songs 4:11</note>. Let these things be for you alone and not for strangers with you. This book, called the "Secrets of Raziel" <note>original: *Sodei Raziel*</note>, is not to be handed over except to one who is modest and middle-aged <note>Literally "standing in the half of his days," traditionally interpreted as age 40</note>, who does not succumb to anger or intoxication, who is self-controlled, who fears God and shuns evil, who walks in the ways of his Creator, and who pursues righteousness. "The secret of the Lord is for those who fear Him" <note>Psalm 25:14</note>, and "His secret is with the upright" <note>Proverbs 3:32</note>, and it is written elsewhere: "He reveals His secret to His servants the prophets" <note>Amos 3:7</note>. Thus, there are three "secrets" corresponding to the Torah, the Prophets, and the Writings, in which all secrets are contained.

They also correspond to the three "beginnings," as it is said: "The beginning of wisdom is the fear of the Lord" <note>Psalm 111:10</note>, and it is written: "The fear of the Lord is the beginning of knowledge" <note>Proverbs 1:7</note>, and it is written: "The start of wisdom is the fear of the Lord" <note>Proverbs 9:10</note>. They also correspond to the three types of wisdom mentioned in scripture: "Wisdom cries out in the street," "Wisdom has built her house," and "Every wise woman builds her house." The secret is not to be sold except to a wise person who is perceptive and understands from his heart, for the word "perceptive" <note>original: *Pekach*</note> in the ATBASH cipher <term>ATBASH: A substitution cipher where the first letter of the alphabet is replaced by the last, the second by the second-to-last, and so on</term> reveals a hidden secret.

There are three types of secrets: The Secret of the Chariot <term>original: *Ma'aseh Merkavah*; the study of the Divine Throne and the celestial hierarchy</term>, the Secret of the Work of Creation <term>original: *Ma'aseh Bereshit*; the study of how the cosmos was formed</term>, and the Secret of the Commandments, as we shall explain with the help of the Almighty <note>original: *Shaddai*</note>.

There are likewise three types of "Fear of Heaven": Fear of the Lord <note>original: *Yirah of YHVH*</note>, Fear of the Almighty <note>original: *Yirah of Shaddai*</note>, and Fear of God <note>original: *Yirah of Elohim*</note>, for there is a distinction between them.

One who fears the Lord <note>YHVH</note> is one who loves the name of the Holy One, Blessed be He, and who serves out of love. This is a high level, as it is written: "Happy is the man who fears the Lord." It does not say "fears God" here, but "fears the Lord," meaning he serves from love, as the verse continues: "who greatly delights in His commandments." Such a person thinks in his heart: "Who will give me the opportunity to perform a commandment?" for he delights greatly in them.

What is meant by "Fear of God" <note>Elohim</note>? This refers to one who is afraid before God lest he fall into temptation and fail, much like a person who fears a king lest he be unable to fulfill the king's decree. You can see this with Abraham; he was called "Abraham my friend <note>or "one who loves me"</note>," yet when the Holy One, Blessed be He, told him to offer up his son, it is written: "Now I know that you fear God." The explanation is: "You love Me and you respect My decree." This is the meaning of: "Happy is the man who does not walk in the counsel of the wicked." This man is the same one of whom it is written: "Happy is the man who fears the Lord," for the fear of Heaven is upon him all day long, and he contemplates the love of the fear of Heaven at all times in his heart. This is "Pure Fear of the Lord"—that fear which stems from love is pure and more precious than simple fear, just as light is superior to darkness. As it is said in the Talmudic tractate Sotah: "The fear of God mentioned regarding Abraham stemmed from love."

What is the difference between one who acts from love and one who acts from fear? One depends on "a thousand generations" and the other on "two thousand" for those who love Me. The Midrash also says there are three types of offerings: the Burnt Offering <note>original: *Olah*</note>, the Peace Offering <note>original: *Shelamim*</note>, and the Sin Offering <note>original: *Chatat*</note>. These correspond to three classes of righteous people:
1.  **Love:** Those who love the Holy One, Blessed be He, and give Him praise and glory. The **Burnt Offering** corresponds to this, as it is offered solely for the glory of the Holy One, Blessed be He.
2.  **Petition:** Those who serve because of a request. The **Peace Offering** corresponds to this, as it is brought regarding a request.
3.  **Fear:** Those who serve out of fear. The **Sin Offering** corresponds to this.

Love is more precious than petition, and petition is more precious than fear. Love is when the righteous serve the Holy One, Blessed be He, and give honor and glory to His kingdom because He alone preceded all things, and in His great goodness He created the world with a word and not with labor. He is God, Father, King, Mighty, Wise, Good, Merciful, and patient with all, filling all upper and lower worlds, sustaining His creatures, knowing the secrets of the universe, doing good for the wicked and the good, and being patient with the wicked so they might return and live. Because of all these attributes, the righteous love the Holy One, Blessed be He, and praise Him for His great works; for this, the Burnt Offering is brought.

What is "Petition"? This is when people serve the Omnipresent, Blessed be He, and offer their supplications and requests, asking Him for every good thing for their own benefit, such as wisdom, understanding, long life, healing, a prudent wife, upright children, success in their endeavors, wealth, honor, and property. Because these are good acquisitions, this is called "Petition," and for this, Peace Offerings are sacrificed.

What is "Fear"? This is when one serves the Holy One, Blessed be He, with great awe and much trembling to be saved from the curses written in the Torah for those who transgress His charges, both in this world and the world to come. In this world: to be saved from illnesses, plagues, and pains; so they do not need the help of others; so strangers do not rule over them; and so death or evil does not enter their homes or reach their daughters. This is so they may fulfill their days in goodness and blessing. In the world to come: to be saved from punishment, from the Destroyer, and from the burning fire of Hell <note>original: *Gehenna*</note> that is destined to burn the wicked on the day of Great Judgment. This is "Fear," and for this, the Sin Offering is sacrificed.

These three offerings—the Burnt Offering, the Peace Offering, and the Sin Offering—are all sacrificed in one House, by one Priest, for the sake of one God. All three are equal among the righteous, whether they serve in love, in petition, or in fear; they are all equal because they all take refuge in the shadow of the Holy One, Blessed be He. He loves the one who loves Him, He answers the one who requests of Him, and He watches over the one who fears Him.

This is "The beginning of wisdom is the fear of the Lord"—this is the Love of Heaven, where the heart of the righteous person burns like a flame for the Holy One, Blessed be He, to perform the commandments. This is what the verse means: "What does the Lord your God ask of you, but to fear?" I would not know if the reward is for fear or love, but because it is written "to fear and to love Him," the scripture is speaking of one who acts from love.

"Fear of the Almighty" <note>original: *Yirah of Shaddai*</note>: This is when one fears the Holy One, Blessed be He, because it is in His power to do all things <note>Another version: "That He alone has the power to do everything"</note>, and he offers his supplication before Him and cries out to Him with heart-felt intention, and He is entreated by him. When one asks a request of Him, He immediately performs it for him. This is the meaning of: "To him that is afflicted, kindness should be showed from his friend; but he forsaketh the fear of the Almighty" <note>Job 6:14</note>. The word for "afflicted" <note>original: *Lamas*</note> means "withholding," as in "melting away." One who withholds kindness from his fellow forsakes the fear of the Almighty—for God has "sufficiency" <note>A play on the name *Shaddai*, which contains the word *Dai*, meaning "Enough" or "Sufficient"</note> in His hand to fulfill his request, yet this person does not fear that the Almighty might not fulfill his own will. Or, it refers to one who harbors evil in his home so that a poor person is afraid to enter. We find in the Midrash that anyone who withholds kindness is as if he worships idols. "Fear of the Almighty" means serving before the Holy One, Blessed be He, fearing Him, arranging one's prayer to Him, and trusting in Him that He will save him from all evil by His hand. This is: "He who dwells in the secret place of the Most High... shall abide under the shadow of the Almighty," for "He shall deliver you from the snare of the fowler," because there is "sufficiency" in His hand. Why? Because you sought Him in your prayer and said to Him: "My refuge and my fortress," and in all your requests...

<summary>This page introduces the Book of Raziel, establishing strict ethical requirements for its study and defining three ways of relating to God—Love, Petition, and Fear—linking them to specific Divine Names and biblical sacrifices. It emphasizes that while Love is the highest motivation, all three forms of service are valid and protected by God.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the introduction to the "Book of Raziel the Angel," moving from the ethical requirements of the student to the mystical foundations of the Hebrew language and the nature of the soul. It establishes a link between the physical mechanics of speech, the elements of creation, and the divine spirit that animates the human body.</meta>

# The Book of Raziel the Angel

Two Yods <note>The Hebrew letter Yod (י)</note> are a *Shva* <note>A Hebrew vowel mark indicating a very short "e" or no vowel at all</note>; it is a *Hataf-Patah* <note>A composite vowel mark</note>. A line with a point below is a *Kamatz* <note>The "ah" vowel mark</note>; with a Yod above it is a *Holam* <note>The "oh" vowel mark</note>; and in the middle of the line it is a *Melafum* <note>Also known as Shuruk, the "oo" vowel mark</note>. If it is under the line, it is a *Hirik* <note>The "ee" vowel mark</note> without a line. A *Shva* or a *Tzere* <note>The "ay" vowel mark</note> with a line of three is a *Segol* <note>The "eh" vowel mark</note>. And the Letter Aleph (א) by itself is composed of a Yod and a Vav <note>The letters י and ו</note>, according to the law of the Name *Havayah* <note>The Tetragrammaton, Y-H-V-H</note>, to make known that the Lord is one and created everything: the upper, middle, and lower worlds and their fullness. There is no movement or connection without Him, blessed be He, for He is unique above the seven heavens and He rules over all.

He led the world as no other could, as the Sages said: "Two oversaw the beginning of the world" <note>This likely refers to the debate about whether the heavens or earth were created first, or the partnership of God and His Word</note>. In the worlds where you see majesty and splendor, He said in His heart: "There are no human beings to recognize the glory of My kingship; I shall create a world that passes and perishes, so that they might understand and take to heart the awe of Me, and serve Me in truth and with a perfect heart for their own good all the days."

When the Master of All spoke, He brought forth spirit from His holy spirit, and it is the head of all creatures. Just as a person speaks and brings spirit out of their mouth, so too the spirits are not restrained, as it says in the Tractate *Taanit* and in the *Book of Formation* <term>Sefer Yetzirah; the foundational text of Jewish mysticism, likely composed between the 2nd and 6th centuries</term>. From His word comes the spirit of fire and water. When a person speaks, they press their lips with strength to bring out a voice, and sparks emerge in the spirit. Therefore it is said: "Is not My word like fire... like a hammer that shatters a rock" <note>Jeremiah 23:29</note>, and sparks of stone and fire emerge from the power of the sparks coming from the mouth. Therefore it is written: "Behold, My words are like fire." They were like fire from the power of the human voice—spirit, water, and the heat of fire. How much more so the word of the "Consuming Fire" <note>A title for God</note>. Therefore, do not say there is no prophet of fire, water, and spirit, for "it is the glory of God to conceal a matter." Therefore we bless over water, saying "that all things exist through His word."

The **Ten Sefirot of Nothingness** <term>Eser Sefirot Belimah; the ten divine emanations through which God interacts with the universe</term>: One is the Spirit of the Living God. This is the spirit of life, the upper spirit to all spirits, and she is the Great Mother from whom all offspring emerged. She is the most praised of all roots and is attributed to the letter *He* (ה). She has the power of strength for all "fathers" <note>Roots or archetypes</note>, and her offspring and descendants are more praised than all other derived things.

The spirit of voice and speech is the spirit of life called the Holy Spirit (*Ruach HaKodesh*). It is written: "And they heard the voice of the Lord God walking in the garden in the spirit (*ruach*) of the day" <note>Genesis 3:8</note>. For are not all voices from the spirit? Like the hinge of a door, the axle of a wagon, or the axle of a wheel that is turned, or the sound of a Shofar. Yet, those voices are not like the voice of speech, as it is said: "And God called to the man." Thus: spirit, voice, and speech.

The voice came in the spirit of the day gently, so he could listen without fear, for He does not speak to a person except according to their strength to hear. He spoke to His prophets in every place gently, as a man speaks to his fellow, as it is written: "And the Lord spoke to Moses face to face." They hear the voice but do not see it. Therefore they call it in the Talmud a *Bat Kol* <term>literally "Daughter of a Voice"; a divine echo or heavenly resonance</term>. It is like a person walking between mountains in a valley, and the mountains above are full of hollows. When the person speaks, the speech enters with the spirit into the hollows—meaning those points in the mountain—and when he is silent, the word rises back to him and they come. If he speaks in a loud voice, it answers in a loud voice; if low, it answers low. There is nothing there but spirit, and in this way, they hear a *Bat Kol*.

When He revealed Himself to Elijah, He was revealed in the spirit in a low voice, as it is written: "Behold, the Lord passed by... a great wind... and after the wind, a still small voice" <note>1 Kings 19:11-12</note>. Daniel said: "And I heard a man's voice" <note>Daniel 8:16</note>. In the Torah, it was with a great voice, divided into ten voices, as it is written: "The voice of the Lord is with power" and "like the sound of many waters." It is written: "the voice of the Almighty (*Shaddai*) as they went," and "the sound of his words was like the sound of a multitude." These are all mighty deeds. It is written: "By the word of the Lord the heavens were made, and by the breath of His mouth all their host." Thus, there is speech and spirit. It is written: "By the blast of Your nostrils the waters were piled up," and "You blew with Your wind," and "It shall rush like a driving stream." From this, one recognizes the Creator, as it is said: "You have made him a little lower than the angels" <note>Psalm 8:5</note>.

The soul is called a "lamp," as it is written: "The soul of man is the lamp of the Lord" <note>Proverbs 20:27</note>, and "He breathed into his nostrils the breath of life." For cattle, it is not written "He breathed," but rather "Let the earth bring forth the living soul." Thus, man is superior to every beast and animal. For beasts and animals, the "living soul" is superior to plants and grasses, for which "living soul" is not said. The soul of man is superior to all life on earth; it is the soul of life, a pure spirit, the breath of life from the Lord; it is the lamp of the Lord. It knows knowledge, counsel, and imagination; it hears, sees, tastes, smells, and feels throughout the whole body. It seeks the matters of the body, for "the soul of all flesh is its blood."

The soul of all flesh is its blood, and it is the spirit. If a limb dries up—the hand or the foot—or if one becomes deaf or mute, it is because of that spirit. If one cuts off a limb, the person does not die, but the limb loses its portion of the soul. But if one is strangled, he dies immediately, and his blood remains in him. If he is killed, he does not die from the loss of blood, but because the spirit is drawn away from its connection. What is written "its blood is in its soul" means it is connected, meaning the spirit is drawn into its connection.

When one eats and drinks, the soul is illuminated. The veins open their mouths, the windpipe warms up, and from the great heat, the food is digested. The salt in the food enters the veins, and from them, the soul is sustained by the warmth of the blood; this is "blood in its soul."

The soul is divided into two parts: internal and external. The **Internal** is the heart, which receives all its desires. If it sees a beautiful object and craves it, if its master can fulfill its will, the heart will rejoice with full joy and the mouth will be filled with laughter. The heart changes based on the object and the fulfillment of its will. If its master cannot fulfill its will, the heart moans and becomes angry with wrathful heat, thinking of nothing but that object. The heart is the seat of love and hate, and all wisdom, knowledge, purpose, thought, and reflection come from it. Through this, you will understand that it sits in its "inn" within the brain <note>The text suggests the soul resides in the brain/mind while the heart acts as the requester</note>. The heart is quiet and asks it for knowledge and understanding and other deep matters, and its desire is filled by it. When a person is weary and tired, immediately a deep sleep falls upon him, and in a dream, it increases, and he does not know if he is awake or asleep.

The **External** is the tongue, and the external senses, and the voice and speech; these sleep and grow weary. But the Internal does not sleep; only the soul departs from the "sheath" <note>The body is often referred to as the *Naran* (an acronym for the levels of the soul) or a sheath/garment for the soul</note>.

However, when a person sleeps and is woken in a fright, and the body is startled and his heart flutters like a small bird—that is the soul returning. But if she returns of her own will, waking without fright, such as close to morning, then the soul is in its "inn." Because all the realization of wisdom and understanding is in the brains, the heart is called the "Internal." The "External" is the tongue, which tells the secrets of the internal heart, whether good or evil, prayer or petition. For were it not for the tongue telling one person to another through speech, one would not know good from evil. The heart thinks, as it is said: "My heart overflows with a goodly matter," and the tongue tells it. And they are the established internal parts.

<summary>This page explores the mystical mechanics of Hebrew vowels as divine structures and explains the relationship between human speech, the elements, and the three-part structure of the soul. It details how the soul (the "Internal") remains active during sleep while the physical senses (the "External") rest.</summary>
<keywords>vowel points, Tetragrammaton, Sefer Yetzirah, Sefirot, Ruach HaKodesh, Bat Kol, human soul, heart vs. brain, dream state, anatomy of spirit</keywords>

--- Page 26 ---
<meta>This page continues the introductory moral and mystical exhortations found at the beginning of the "Book of Raziel," focusing on the virtues of modesty and the esoteric significance of the Hebrew letter Aleph in relation to the Divine and the history of the Holy Temple.</meta>

# The Book of Raziel the Great

...that one should praise another and not praise himself, as it is written: "Let a stranger praise you, and not your own mouth" <note>Proverbs 27:2</note>. However, one may speak to a companion or a student so that they may learn from his words, and in that context, he may praise himself <note>The text suggests that self-praise is only permitted for educational purposes to inspire others</note>, as it is said: "And I shall be whole with Him, for I have kept the ways of the Lord." The man was raised up on high <note>2 Samuel 23:1</note>, and similarly, "How I have loved Your days." He told them how he lived a long life so that they too might do the same. All positive commandments should be performed in secret, as it says, "and walk humbly with your God" <note>Micah 6:8</note>. But if you perform them so that others may learn from you, do so in front of everyone—such as wearing *Tefillin* <note>phylacteries</note>, *Tzitzit* <note>ritual fringes</note>, and the study of Torah—for "the envy of scribes increases wisdom" and the performance of commandments. 

Whatever you can do, perform the positive commandments for the sake of Heaven, as it is said: "If you are righteous, what do you give to Him?" <note>Job 35:7</note>. Any commandment that has no one else to perform it, occupy yourself with it. Place the love of your Creator alone and the fear of your needs before your face. Be quick to perform the commandments with joy. Do not speak idle words in any place, and certainly not in the synagogue where the Divine Presence <note>The *Shekhinah*</note> is before you; sit in awe before Him and direct your heart to thank Him. It is not enough to merely avoid transgressions because of the shame people feel before others; even if no person sees you, one should fear lest it become known to people and he be ashamed. Rather, regarding any transgression that others do and you do not do, your reward is doubled. For every man may look upon the face of a King, but before a woman [it is different]. It is certainly a commandment to see the face of the King, for "your eyes shall behold the King in his beauty" <note>Isaiah 33:17</note>, but it is forbidden to gaze at women, as we say in Chapter 190, 7 <note>Likely a reference to a specific legal code or ethical text</note>, that it is forbidden to look even at an unmarried woman, and how much more so a married woman. If you restrain your spirit and do not look at the faces of women, your reward is doubled and redoubled. Know that anyone who increases words brings about sin. Calculate the "loss" of a commandment against its reward. Let all your deeds be for the sake of Heaven. It is good that you take hold of this, and more than these, be careful. If you do so, happy are you in this world, and it shall be well with you in the World to Come. "My help comes from the Lord, Maker of heaven and earth" <note>Psalms 121:2</note>.

The **Secret** of the Lord is for those who fear Him <note>Psalms 25:14</note>. This is the secret of the Work of Creation. Rabbi Isaac said: The Torah should not have begun but from "This month shall be for you" <note>Exodus 12:2, the first commandment given to the nation</note>. Why then did it open with "In the beginning"? Because it is said: "He has declared to His people the power of His works" <note>Psalms 111:6</note>. All this was done so that they might know, understand, and contemplate His great and awesome deeds, and testify that there is none like Him and none beside Him. I shall write this book, called "Secret" (*Sod*), to make known the might of the Creator of the universe. Happy are those who know it, for anyone who knows the secret and fears the Lord is assured that he is a child of the World to Come. I will write for you orally the Gates—all that exists above—to know the unity of the Glory. Set your heart upon His awe and prostrate yourself to Him, for He is one and there is no second to Him, blessed be He.

<image-desc>The word "Aleph" (אלף) is written in a large, ornate square script, enclosed within a rectangular border featuring decorative flourishes.</image-desc>

# Aleph

This represents the Holy One, Blessed be He, for He is first and He is last. He is King over the whole world and there is none like Him. Just as the letter Aleph (א) is the head of the letters, so the Holy One, Blessed be He, is the head of all the angels. Aleph (א) is one, which is the smallest count, for there is nothing less than it. *Revavah* <note>10,000</note> is a large count. When Aleph is at the beginning of a word, it is "one" (1); when Aleph is at the end of a word, it is "ten thousand" (10,000) <note>This refers to a mystical interpretation where the word *Eleph* (thousand/one) changes meaning based on context or positioning</note>. It says that he returns from "one" and from "ten thousand," as it is written in Ezekiel, "one thing to one" <note>Ezekiel 37:16-17</note>, and in Daniel it is written, "ten thousand times ten thousand stood before Him" <note>Daniel 7:10</note>. The Aleph at the beginning of the count is "one," as in "one ten-thousand" (*had revu*), and the Aleph at the end is "ten-thousand" (*rebu*). This makes known to mankind that He is one and there is no second to Him; He is first and He is last, like the Aleph which is at the beginning of the count and at the end of the count. Furthermore, Aleph is at the start of the alphabet (A, B, C, D) and also at the end in the reversed alphabet (*Tashrak*). Its pronunciation involves three letters: A-L-P (*Aleph*).

The *Aleph* (א) is among the guttural letters (from the throat); the *Lamed* (ל) is among the letters of the tip of the tongue and the middle of the palate; the *Pe* (פ) is among the letters of the lips. Thus, the word "Aleph" begins at the house of swallowing (the throat), is in the middle of the palate and the tongue, and its pronunciation ends at the lips. This is to inform you that He is one: the first, the middle, and the last. The letter Aleph turns its face away from the other letters to tell you that the One in His world, when He created the world, turned His face away from the world, as it is said, "He saw there was nothing and did not look," because He foresaw the destruction of the Temple. He said, "I have no pleasure in My world; I will hide My face from it." 

Know that at the very beginning of the creation of the world, there was a hint of the destruction of the Temple. The *Beit* (ב) of *Bereshit* ("In the beginning") is large to say that the "Beginning" refers to the Temple, of which it is written, "A glorious throne, on high from the beginning, is the place of our sanctuary" <note>Jeremiah 17:12</note>. Then it opens with "And the earth was chaos and void" (*Tohu va-Vohu*). The middle word <term>in Gematria</term> <note>Gematria is the Jewish system of assigning numerical values to Hebrew letters</note> hints that the First Temple would stand for 410 years. And the "H" and "T" <note>numerical values</note> hint that the Second Temple would stand for 420 years. *Tohu va-Vohu* refers to the two Sanctuaries, both of which stood for roughly 400 years; this is "And I shall dwell (*ve-shakhanti*) among them" <note>Exodus 25:8</note>, where the word "dwell" (*shakhan*) contains the number 410. In many places, the destruction of the Temple is hinted at. And here, in "In the beginning" (*Bereshit*), it makes known that at the start of the world the destruction of the House was mentioned, for everything He created shall be destroyed, and "the Lord alone shall be exalted" <note>Isaiah 2:11</note>. 

The letter *Beit* (ב) also means "house" (*Bayit*). He created everything in "twos" (*Beit* equals 2), for everything has a partner: two worlds (this world and the world to come), heaven and earth, mountains and hills, seas and rivers, Hell and the Garden of Eden, the Leviathan and the Piercing Serpent, the sun and the moon, male and female, the wicked and the righteous. Man has two eyes, two ears, two nostrils, a mouth and a tongue, two hands, two legs, and ribs on this side and that side. Even the fat tail of a sheep is divided into two by a single sinew. All this is to inform you that everything has a pair; therefore, the Torah opens with the letter *Beit* (2), except for Him, who is One in His kingship, blessed be He.

Therefore, the name *Aleph* is at the head of the letters, and Aleph is at the start of the name *Adonai* (Lord). But the letter *Yod* (י) is at the head of the Pre-eminent Name, *Y-H-V-H*. This is the sound of the breath from the *Melafum* <note>vowel sound</note> to inform us that He created His world with ten utterances, filling everything. Furthermore, the pronunciation of "El" is above... <note>The text describes the mystical pronunciation and vowel points of divine names</note> to inform us that He is unique above the seven heavens and rules over all. (Another version: The tongue of His name is Aleph, and the name *Ehyeh* ["I Shall Be"], which is the beginning of the ten <term>Sefirot</term> <note>The ten divine attributes or emanations in Kabbalah</note> from top to bottom, [begins with Aleph]. And *Adonai*, which is the end of the ten *Sefirot* at the bottom, begins with Aleph). 

The Aleph is the head of all letters and the end of the letters in the reversed alphabet, like the "material of a seal," to show that everything is One. Within these letters is the pronunciation of all the Holy Names. The reason *Yod* (י) is at the head of the Pre-eminent Name *Y-H-V-H* is because one cannot write an Aleph without first starting with a Yod <note>In Hebrew calligraphy, the letter Aleph is composed of a diagonal Vav and two Yods</note>. Also, no movement or vowel pronunciation can exist without the *Yod*, *Vav*, or *Yod*—whether it is a *Holam*, a *Melafum*, or a *Hirik*. This is to inform us that with the *Yod*, He created the ten *Sefirot*, the middle world, and the lower world. The Aleph itself contains the *Shva*, *Tzere*, *Patah*, *Hirik*, *Holam*, *Melafum*, and *Hataf-Patah*...
