# The Book of Raziel the Great

(From [heat], moisture becomes fire, and when the heat becomes fire, light remains. This light is itself warm and good. This is the meaning of "You shall know this day and take it to your heart" <note>Deuteronomy 4:39</note>—for He performed all these things. God is the Actor; there is none like Him. He acted, He created everything, and He fills everything.) The spirit of man is in the heart, for all thoughts are in the heart. Without the heart, there is no wisdom, as it is said: "For the matter is very close to you, in your mouth and in your heart" <note>Deuteronomy 30:14</note>. Therefore, it is called *Lev* <term>Heart; in Hebrew, LB</term>—meaning one should think from the *Beit* <note>the first letter of the Torah, in the word *Bereshit*</note> to the *Lamed* <note>the last letter of the Torah, in the word *Yisrael*</note>, which together spell *Lev*. Therefore, the *Sefer Yetzirah* <term>The Book of Formation; an ancient foundational text of Jewish mysticism</term> begins with the thirty-two <note>The numerical value of *Lev* is 32</note> paths of wisdom through which the world was created. The heart thinks: "The Holy One, Blessed be He, created me, and He is with me in all my states and bears witness to me." 

From a drop from my father and mother, He created a form <term>Golem; an unformed physical mass</term> containing ten things. These are: skin, flesh, sinews, bones, marrow, blood, hair, nails, and the black and white parts of the eyes. Correspondingly, the Holy One, Blessed be He, gives him ten things of His own. These are: the soul (*Neshamah*), the features of the face, the hearing of the ear, the sight of the eye, the scent of the nose, the speech of the lips, the habits of the tongue, the use of the hands, the walking of the feet, and wisdom and understanding. The spirit of man immerses the whole body just as the wind/spirit <term>Ruach; the Hebrew word means both "spirit" and "wind"</term> surrounds the whole world. This is to say that man is a "small world" <note>The concept of the Microcosm</note>, and there is no air without spirit. Therefore, a person should think: "How fortunate am I to have been created in the image and likeness, and that He gave me speech to serve the King of Glory, and gave me a spirit that is deposited in His hand every time I sleep. If He wishes, He could take my breath away—so how could I sin?" I shall pass before Him in joy and gladness and nullify the pleasures of this world for the pleasures of the World to Come.

There are six vocal sounds: *A, E, I, O, U* <note>The text lists five vowel sounds represented by Hebrew points: Kamatz, Tzere, Chirik, Cholam, Shuruk</note> corresponding to the six constellations embedded in the celestial sphere. Six rise by day and night; however, they shine but are not seen during the day because of the power of the sun. Sometimes they sink and sometimes they rise. So too are the six "Kings" among the musical accents <term>Cantillation marks used to chant the Bible</term> that serve above: *Zarka, Revi'i, Gerishim*, etc.; and from below six: *Atnach, Revi'a*, etc. 

He made the letter *Aleph* (א) king over the wind, bound a crown to it, permuted them one with another, and formed with them: Man in the world, and saturation in the year, and the body in the soul, male and female. The male with *AMSh* <note>אמש - the three "Mother Letters" of the Hebrew alphabet according to the *Sefer Yetzirah*: Aleph (Air), Mem (Water), Shin (Fire)</note> and the female with *AShM* <note>אשם - a permutation of the same letters</note>. The explanation is: He made *Aleph* king at the head of speech and permuted the letters with one another—all of them with *Aleph*—making them into two formations. He built the first as *AMSh* and the second as *AShM*. He formed a spirit from His spirit and formed with it the air in the world, saturation in the year, and the body in the soul. Every thing that is a mediator: for according to the three <note>the mother letters</note> they were created from the spirit from the chest and the heart, which is the body cast between the belly and the head. He birthed the soul of the spirit in every existing thing: male in *AMSh* and female in *AShM*. There is a quality in the air of the world that grows, births, and makes females fruitful among creatures, among plants, and among fruits. And there is saturation that births and grows females among creatures and plants in everything. For everything that has heat and a body, there is *AMSh* and *AShM*. 

The spirit of God fills everything. He turned the air of the world to be a space so that the world might live by the breathing of the spirit. For without space and air, no creature could live; if it were blocked at its source, it would have no air, and where there is no air, there is no spirit, and death is immediate. Therefore, from His holy spirit, He fills every space in all four directions. This is the meaning of "And by the breath of His mouth, all their host" <note>Psalm 33:6</note>—the spirit is the breath of life. Therefore, it was created with *Aleph*, the Spirit: for a person cannot say "A" except by opening his mouth and letting out breath. The spirit comes from the heart in the middle of the belly, where the spirit serves. Likewise, the spirit comes from the nose, which is the mediator between the eyes. And the spirit comes from the heart, which is in the middle between the trachea and the lungs. For the Holy Spirit comes from the Throne, which is the mediator between the camps <note>the angelic hosts</note>; this is the Spirit of God. Likewise, the Ark is the mediator between the camps, from which the spirit goes out to the prophet. Beneath the Throne of Glory, there is a space like a hollowed-out pit <note>Original: *agur*</note>, and there are the spirits, as it is said: "The soul of my lord shall be bound in the bundle of life with the Lord your God" <note>1 Samuel 25:29</note>. It is prepared in the space of the mouth—the palate and the tongue—for there is a height in the palate like a ship, and in it is the spirit. There is a spring under the tongue like the rivers around the Throne. As for the space like a pit under the Throne of Glory, this is "I went down to the garden of nuts" <note>Song of Songs 6:11; a play on the word *Egoz* (nut) and *Agur* (pit/hollow). A marginal gloss here notes: This refers to the depths of the Chariot/Merkavah</note>. Therefore: "He was seen upon the wings of the wind," for they are light. For the spirit is the head of all creatures, and the spirit only rules in a place of spirit—therefore it is called *Ruach* <term>Spirit/Wind</term>. 

The letter *Resh* (ר) is at the beginning of the word *Ruach* because the spirit of man is in his head, and from there it exits where it was breathed in, as it is said: "And He breathed into his nostrils the breath of life" <note>Genesis 2:7</note>. It is also written: "The Spirit of God hovered over the face of the waters" <note>Genesis 1:2</note>—this is the spirit of man, for he lived 930 years. Like the word *Ruach* (ר-ו-ח) spelled out: *Reish, Vav, Chet*—the numerical value <term>Gematria</term> is 930. <note>The text calculates the full spelling of the letters of *Ruach* to equal the years of Adam's life</note>. Wherever it is written "Spirit of God," it refers to a strong spirit that the prophet feels the Holy One, Blessed be He, walking within. *Ruach* spelled backward is *Chur* <note>meaning a hole or opening</note> (this is His messenger from the "hole"). *Ruach* in the *Atbash* <term>A cipher where the first letter is replaced by the last, second by second-to-last, etc.</term> system is *SPG* (ספ"ג), for there are tablets with small holes like a sponge (*Sfog*). When the wind blows through the spirit, rain descends little by little to the earth; this is the meaning of "He causes His wind to blow, and the waters flow" <note>Psalm 147:18</note>.

---

## Concerning the Spirit and its Power

The word *Ruach* (Spirit) has three letters. Three times the spirit went out without measure in the days of Job, Elijah, and Jonah. The spirit performs three actions: it breaks rocks, shatters mountains, and brings forth fire. As it is written of Elijah: "A great and strong wind (*Ruach*) rent the mountains and broke the rocks in pieces before the Lord" <note>1 Kings 19:11</note>. And it is written in Ezekiel: "And behold, a stormy wind... and a fire infolding itself" <note>Ezekiel 1:4</note>. The letter *Reish* is at the head of *Ruach*, and *Reish* in small gematria is 2, for the spirit orbits for two hundred years, as it says: "The wind goes toward the south... and the wind returns on its circuits" <note>Ecclesiastes 1:6</note>. The letter *Yod* (י) <note>Perhaps referring to the 10 aspects of spirit</note> is in the spirit, for ten matters of spirits were said regarding the Messiah: 1. Spirit of wisdom, 2. Spirit of understanding, 3. Counsel, 4. Might, 5. Knowledge, 6. Fear of the Lord... 8. Regarding the spirit, there are eight types of spirits: 1. A spirit of vengeance ("My spirit shall not judge"), 2. The spirit that blows in the world ("The spirit of God hovered"), 3. A spirit of angels, as it is written "not in the wind was the Lord," and the Targum Yonatan translates "as the angels of the spirit, the Presence of the Lord," for He "makes His angels spirits," 4. The spirit of human beings, as it says "The God of the spirits [of all flesh]," 5. A broad spirit to execute judgment—this is the "spirit of judgment," 6. A spirit that sees visions and is terrified—this is "the spirit of God terrified him," 7. A spirit of tyranny/boldness that fears nothing, even if he is weak, he has a spirit of tyranny, and this is...

# The Book of Raziel the Great

...external wisdoms, which are spirit, voice, and speech. For a voice without speech is like the call of beasts or birds; it has no utility because they do not understand, but rather they only recognize each other—husband and wife, every bird according to its kind, and every beast according to its species. Not every voice is speech, but every speech is a voice, and every voice comes from the spirit. Every 
<margin>If seen without a voice</margin> 
spirit does not come from a voice, yet there is no creature without a spirit. And all who possess a spirit have a voice. 
Regarding fish, scorpions, and crabs, which have no voice—one cannot say this is because they are in the water, for frogs in the water have a voice. However, the frog 
<margin>And there is no creature without spirit:</margin> 
has a throat and thus has a voice, but fish, scorpions, and crabs are without voice because they have no throat.

But this spirit is praised and unique; it was given only to the man of understanding to speak clear words, as it is said: "You have made him a little lower than the angels" <note>Psalm 8:5</note>, for he has an image and likeness, knowledge and plotting, the thoughts of the heart, the habit of the tongue, and the use of power in his external limbs. This is the superiority of man over the beast, and the superiority of the beast over plants. For the beast recognizes its master, knows of eating and drinking, satiety, joy, and hunger, and recognizes its food and has the power to walk. However, it does not have the contemplation and thoughts of a man regarding the affairs of the world; it does not even have contemplations regarding eating and drinking. It lacks the "pearls of the tongue" <note>An idiomatic way to describe articulate speech</note>. 
The beast is superior to the plants of the field, for the plants of the field have no spirit, only growth. The plant lacks the man's ability to join and use tools, and it lacks the beast's ability to walk and its spirit. 

From the spirit comes the spirit of prophecy, which is called the "Spirit of God," for it is a gift from God as an addition of wisdom, just as Pharaoh said: "Can we find such a one as this, a man in whom is the Spirit of God?" <note>Genesis 41:38</note>. Behold, Jacob, due to his great grief over Joseph, the spirit of prophecy departed from him, and he was like a stricken man, saying, "My way is hidden from the Lord" <note>Isaiah 40:27</note>. But when he heard that Joseph was in Egypt, his heart was transformed from its contemplations, and "the spirit of Jacob lived" <note>Genesis 45:27</note>. Wherever it is written "The Spirit of God was upon him," it refers to the spirit of prophecy or the spirit of might. Wherever it says "The word of the Lord," it refers to speech, as in "And the Lord spoke to Moses." There is no speech without spirit, and there is no existence without speech. 

The "spirit of might" is like that of Othniel son of Kenaz: "And the spirit of the Lord came upon him" <note>Judges 3:10</note>; and regarding Jephthah, Samson, and David, the "spirit of the Lord" refers to a spirit of might <note>courage/strength</note>. A spirit of prophecy is for specific times; a prophet does not always know what he is speaking, but those who heard Isaiah speak would understand. When one prepares the soul with musical instruments and melody, and contemplates the fear of God, a spirit of truth will come upon him. But in sadness, an evil spirit terrifies him. Therefore, the heart should rejoice in the love of its Creator. There is a spirit of prophecy like that of Elijah, Elisha, Moses, Joshua, and the elders; a spirit of wisdom like that of Bezalel; a ministering spirit of the Holy One, Blessed be He; and a spirit that travels from the Lord: "And the Lord turned a very strong west wind" <note>Exodus 10:19</note>, and "Behold, a great wind." These are messengers, as it is said: "He makes His angels spirits" <note>Psalm 104:4</note>. 

However, in the matter of Ezekiel, who said, "And a spirit lifted me," and "The spirit of the Lord carried me"—that spirit is what carries the high and exalted Throne along with the Living Creatures (*Chayyot*), as it is said: "Wherever the spirit was to go, they went... for the spirit of the Living Creature was in the wheels" <note>Ezekiel 1:20-21</note>. For every time the Holy One, Blessed be He, is revealed in the power of the might of His Chariot (*Merkavah*), the Living Creatures, and the Wheels, Ezekiel is lifted by that power. We do not find that the spirit lifted him before the Holy One, Blessed be He, was revealed to him, as it says: "And a spirit lifted me, and [I heard] the sound of the wings of the Living Creatures touching each other." This was the first time and the second time. And as for the "likeness of their faces"—these are the faces [of the Chariot]. And the third time: "And the glory of the Lord went up from the midst of the city... and a spirit lifted me." And finally: "And the glory of the Lord came into the house." 

All learning is through the voice, and the voice of a human being rises from his spirit. Every letter has six sounds <note>referring to vowel vocalizations</note>. For example, the letter *Aleph* (א) has: *Kamatz* (אָ), *Patach* (אַ), *Tzere* (אֵ), *Sheva* (אְ), *Shurek* (אוּ), and *Cholam* (אֹ). These are found in the words for "Light" (*Or*), "Lights" (*Me'orot*), "North" (*Tzefoni*), "Man" (*Adam*), "If he washed" (*Im rachatz*), "If the way" (*Im haderech*), "I am light" (*Ani or*). 

Thus, the letter *Aleph* has six sounds. Among the "simple" letters, there are fifteen; for if you remove the seven "doubled" letters from the alphabet—which are *Bet, Gimel, Dalet, Kaph, Pe, Resh, Tav* <term>Begadkephrat; letters that have both a hard and soft pronunciation</term>—there remain fifteen letters out of the twenty-two. Each of these letters has six sounds. From the simple ones, there are fifteen letters, each with six sounds, totaling 90 sounds. For the seven doubled letters, in their emphasized and clear speech, there are 84 sounds; for the seven letters of *Begadkephrat* possess six sounds for the hard version and six sounds for the soft version, totaling twelve for each of the seven, which equals 84. The total for the 22 letters is 174 sounds. 

The spirit of man should not be raised in pride, but he should contemplate his Creator, who is the First of all firsts and the Last of all lasts, and who is unique—who can turn Him back? He "turns man back to dust" <note>Psalm 90:3</note> and to a humble spirit. When a man looks at himself, he sees that his body was formed from a fetid drop of seed, and then became a piece of flesh, and into it was blown a spirit of seventy [faculties/languages] <note>original: "seventy Greeks/Yevanim," likely a mystical reference to the 70 nations or 70 languages/faculties of the soul</note>. In him were formed bones, and the sinews were stretched, and the spirit was joined in the blood, and the bones were filled with marrow and clothed in skin. 

How was the features of the face formed? The height of the nose and the nostrils—how are they pierced inward to the throat for breathing and smelling? And his ears for hearing? The whites of the eyes and the pupils of the eyes—from how many colors? And the iris of the eye, and the eyelashes, and the eyelid? And the throat, the palate, and the tongue—how is it drawn out? And the lips, to open and close, to rise? And the cheeks, and the two "doors" of the mouth? The place of the teeth, and the two lips like a curtain over them? And the rest of the joints of the hands and feet, the cavities of the body, the fingers and their knuckles, and the nails—how they differ from one another? No one is tied to another, even though they were silent in the womb. He comes out naked and blind, and a "gaping mute" <note>meaning an infant who cannot yet speak</note>. 

Let a man say to his heart: Who made you see? Who opened your ears? Who put speech in your mouth? Who gave you the power to walk and the use of your hands and feet, and wisdom and understanding? In the days of your youth, who blackened the hair of your head? For if you wash it with all kinds of lye and soap, you cannot blacken a single hair, nor can you whiten even a single hair. But in your old age, all your hairs will whiten. 

Behold, fire, air, water, and earth were all created together at the beginning of his formation. His flesh, bones, skin, sinews, organs, and membranes grew—all that was of him—all at once. Not this part by itself and that part by itself, but all together as one nature. Just as when fire is kindled, one sees light, flowing heat, redness, and flame rising from the fire. The "air" is the spirit, and the water and the earth—for by the word of the Creator, the moisture was gathered and collected from some of them and became water, and the wetness of the water was separated from it and became earth, and the air which was separated...

<meta>This page continues the exploration of the elements and the spiritual mechanics of creation, moving from the nature of the spirit and voice into the specific mystical properties of the Hebrew letters, the role of the Torah as a blueprint for the universe, and the numerical significance of the word "Bereshit" (In the Beginning).</meta>

# The Book of Raziel the Great

And in the letter *Shin* <note>The letter ש (Shin) often symbolizes fire (Esh) in Kabbalistic thought</note> of the fire, there is the root of the word "change," for the fire of heaven changes in that it consumes water, as it is written: "and [it] licked up the water that was in the trench" <note>1 Kings 18:38, referring to Elijah's sacrifice</note>. Because it emerged from His holy bitterness <note>referring to a divine attribute of judgment or heat</note>, the appearance of the fire changes. It is written: "everything that may abide the fire, you shall make it go through the fire, and it shall be clean" <note>Numbers 31:23</note>. Above, its purification is by fire in the River Dinur <note>a river of fire in Jewish angelology</note>, and for those below, purification is through water. Thus, by the Word, the world was filled with spirit, water, and fire. This is the secret of *AMSh* <note>original: אמש (Aleph, Mem, Shin), the three "mother letters" representing Air, Water, and Fire</note>—Air, Water, and Fire—for the fire took the water and made them shine. For everything that seeks water, you shall abandon it above, and it shall descend below. The flame ascends upward to the place from which it came. "And the spirit of God hovered over the face of the waters" <note>Genesis 1:2</note>—this is His holy spirit. And He took the fire with Him into His partition; this is the meaning of "and the awesome one is with Him" <note>Job 37:22</note>. Now the world was water and spirit upon the water, and fire for them all, from eternity, from all of them.

And the Lord said in His heart: "There is no glory for a king without an army. I shall create worlds to make known the glory of My kingdom." And the King took counsel with His professional tool, which is the Torah, as it is stated in *Bereshit Rabbah*: "'Then I was by Him, as a nursling [Amon]' <note>Proverbs 8:30</note>—the Torah said: 'I was the professional tool <note>A pun on 'Amon' (nursling) and 'Uman' (artisan/tool)</note> of the Holy One, Blessed be He.'" And it says in the Midrash: "The Lord by wisdom founded the earth" <note>Proverbs 3:19</note>—there is no wisdom but the Torah, as it is written: "for this is your wisdom and your understanding" <note>Deuteronomy 4:6</note>. This teaches that the Holy One, Blessed be He, created His world with the Torah. You can know this through the "fullness" <note>Term: milui — the practice of spelling out the names of letters to find hidden numerical values</note> of Wisdom <note>original: חכמה (Chokhmah)</note>: *Het, Kaf, Mem, He* equals 603 in Gematria <note>numerical mysticism</note>. And it is written: "In the beginning [Bereshit] God created"—there is no "beginning" but the Torah, as it is written: "The Lord possessed me in the beginning of His way" <note>Proverbs 8:22</note>. *Bereshit* in Gematria is equal to "Torah-Yatzar" <note>Torah-Formed</note>, to teach you that He formed His world through the Torah.

Know that if you add the letters: *Aleph* is one; *Bet* is two, making three; *Gimel* is three, making six; *Dalet* is four, making ten; *He* is five, making fifteen; *Vav* is six, making twenty-one; *Zayin* is seven, making twenty-eight; *Het* is eight, making thirty-six; *Tet* is nine, making forty-five. Set that aside. Now calculate: *Yod* and *Tzadi* make 100; *Kaf* and *Pe* make 100; *Mem* and *Mem* <note>Final Mem</note> make 100; [another calculation] makes 400. There remains *Nun*. Put *Nun* with *Mem* and it makes 90. Add the 95 to 400 and it makes 495. Thus, it lacks 5 from 500, which represents the Five Books of the Torah. Thus, the Torah completes the count to 500. And the world was created with a journey of 500 years; therefore, He formed the world through the Torah. This is what is written: "If My covenant be not with day and night, I have not appointed the ordinances of heaven and earth" <note>Jeremiah 33:25</note>—the verse speaks of the Torah, as it says: "you shall meditate therein day and night" <note>Joshua 1:8</note>. 

Know that all day long one should meditate on the Torah. When you calculate the verse "The Torah that Moses commanded us" <note>Deuteronomy 33:4</note> using the *Atbash* <note>a cipher where the first letter is swapped with the last, the second with the second-to-last, etc.</note> method, the number is 13,000 minus 40. That is to say: 1 face is 400; 6 times 80 is 480; 3 times 200 is 600; 5 times 90 is 450. Thus, "Torah" is 1,830. <note>The author continues various complex permutations of the letters in "Torah," "Moses," and "Commanded" to reach specific cosmic totals</note>. These equal 500, 5, 20, 30—13,000 minus 40. Likewise, in a day there are 12 hours, and each hour has 1,080 "parts" <note>Term: chalakim — traditional units of time in the Hebrew calendar; 1,080 chalakim = 1 hour</note>. Thus, 12 times 1,080 is 12,960 (which is 13,000 minus 40). The same applies to the night. This is the meaning of "meditate on it day and night." There are 24 Books <note>the Hebrew Bible/Tanakh</note> corresponding to the 24 hours of the day and night. There are 6 orders [of Mishnah] corresponding to the 6 days of the week. There are 4 orders that have Gemara <note>referring to the Talmudic commentary</note> corresponding to the 4 days of the week without a Torah reading, and these 4 orders sustain them. And in the Torah was the counsel of the King of Kings of Kings, the Holy One, Blessed be He. Blessed be the Lord forever, Amen and Amen.

---

### In the Beginning

"God created"—He opened with the letter *Bet* <note>the second letter of the alphabet</note> to inform human beings that with the 22 letters of His Name, He created two worlds: This World and the World to Come. As it is said: "For in Yah [YH], the Lord, is an everlasting rock" <note>Isaiah 26:4</note>—This World was created with the letter *He* and the World to Come with the letter *Yod*. Because the World to Come arose in thought to be created before [this] world, He placed the *Yod* before the *He*. And if you ask why the Holy One, Blessed be He, preceded the *Yod* with the *Bet* in the alphabet? It was to join the letters of the Name: *Aleph* (1) plus *Bet* (2) equals 3; [plus *Gimel* (3)] equals 6; plus *Dalet* (4) equals 10. And next to 10 is *He* (5). This forms the Name *Yah* [YH], the holy Name, as we shall explain in the Gate of Names. 

The letter *Bet* represents This World, which has three walls:
->( Behold )<-
just like the shape of the letter *Bet* (ב). For it was created with Ten Utterances corresponding to the Ten Commandments. The letter *Het* (ח) represents the Resurrection of the Dead. Therefore, He began creating His world [with *Bet*]. Furthermore, the *Bet* has a corner pointing upward to signify the Unity [of God]. Furthermore, this is the rule: *A-B-G-D-H-V* sums up to "Surely" [Ak] and "your two heads" <note>mystical references to letter sums</note>. Always turn your face toward your world. 

The *Bet* is written near the destruction of the Temples, and He commanded that we increase [the population], which is... and with the addition of 7, it reaches 28. Remember "Then Moses sang" <note>Exodus 15:1</note>. Regarding the Temple, "I saw the earth, and behold, it was without form and void [tohu vavohu], and the heavens, and they had no light" <note>Jeremiah 4:23</note>. Thus He hinted at the power of His works. Furthermore, behold *A-B-G-D* is the secret of "Surely" [Ak]... at the beginning of the world, "the earth was without form and void, and darkness." "Tohu" (Formless) means the firmament was separated from the earth. The totality of *A-B-G-D* is ten... *A-D* with *N* in the middle... from every good... a firmament like "in the waste [tohu] howling wilderness" <note>Deuteronomy 32:10</note>, a desolate place where there is no heat. *He* and *He* make 10, like the small count, making the name *Adonai*. From every good, like "they went after vain things [ha-tohu] which cannot profit or deliver" <note>1 Samuel 12:21</note>. And so it was the secret of the holy garments... a hint of the holy garments of linen. And with the *Bet*, "I saw the earth and behold it was without form and void," as I wrote nearby, "I saw and behold there was no man." In the middle, there is "against," which in Gematria is *Yah* (15). "Against" itself is nine. Thus "the earth was formless" of all the world's needs. "And void" (Bohu)—without buildings. Therefore, "and the spirit..." 15. "And the man and his wife hid among the trees of the garden" <note>Genesis 3:8</note> because there was no house in the world. And "darkness"—these are the thick clouds, as in "darkness of waters, thick clouds of the skies" <note>Psalm 18:11</note>. He called the clouds darkness because they prevent light and darken the appearance, as in "Lo, I come to you in a thick cloud" <note>Exodus 19:9</note>. And here the clouds were created. But actual darkness—it did not exist. He does not say "darkness" as if it were a partner to the light that existed before and then became dark; rather, light had not yet been created in the world. And as he said...

<header>The Book of Raziel the Great</header>

# The Book of Raziel the Great

"Then the spirit clothed Amasai" <note>1 Chronicles 12:19</note>: God is the spirit of the prophets, as it is said, "And I will put My spirit upon him," "the spirit of Elijah," "and the spirit of the Lord shall rest upon him," and "I will pour out My spirit upon all flesh." These constitute the eight types of spirits. And "The King" <note>original: והמלך (Ha-Melekh)</note> is a term related to *Aleph* <note>The first letter of the Hebrew alphabet, symbolizing God as the 'Aluph' or Master of the universe</note>, for the Holy One, blessed be He, made a "weight for the spirit." The knowledge of human beings is found in Scripture, in the Mishnah, in the Talmud, in the Aggadah <note>homiletic or narrative teachings</note>, in worldly conduct, and in the secrets—and He did this "to make a weight for the spirit" <note>Job 28:25</note>. The masculine aspect is remembered through *AMSh* <note>original: אמ"ש (Aleph, Mem, Shin), the three "Mother Letters" representing Air, Water, and Fire</note>, because the letter *Mem* is the middle between *Aleph* and *Shin*. It transforms through the *Atbash* cipher <note>A system where the first letter is swapped with the last, the second with the second-to-last, etc.</note> into *Vav*, *Yod*, and *Dalet*, which together with *AMSh* create "Fire" <note>original: אש (Esh)</note>. The feminine aspect is in *ASHM* <note>original: אשם</note>, because *Mem* transforms into *Heh*, and *Heh* into *Aleph*—through the *Aleph-Beit* order of *A-P, B-Ay, G-S, D-N, H-M*—thus "Woman" <note>original: אשה (Ishah)</note> comes from *ASHM*. Just as the letters stood out and shone in the *Urim and Thummim* <note>The high priest's breastplate that provided divine oracular answers</note>, so before the creation of the world, the letters stood out in the Torah.

When the letters *ASHM* were prominent, a strong air was created that grows beings of power and males more than females, and large, fat fruits. And when *ASHM* was prominent <note>The text repeats the term, likely referring to a different permutation or state</note>, a weak air was created that grows weaklings and females more than males, and lean fruits. Therefore, there is air that grows weaklings and females and lean fruits, and there is air that grows mighty ones and males and fat fruits. Because the spirit emerges from His holy spirit, all human vitality is a "breath of spirit." Therefore, God is mentioned in relation to "spirit" more than anything else, as it is said: "And the spirit of God hovered" <note>Genesis 1:2</note>. From the dampness of the spirit comes water, and from the power of water comes fire. They have an example in the world: if a person blows into their palms, they become damp. It is also known that if water is heated in a clean glass vessel and placed in the sun during the summer solstice in hot climates, one can ignite a burning flame from it. Similarly, from the "fire of stones": if one heats water in certain places and washes in it for many days, one will find at the bottom of the vessel something like pieces of stone. This is a matter of logical reason. Furthermore, the spirit is stronger than them all, for they all depend on the spirit, and the spirit depends on the word of the Holy One, blessed be He. Regarding all of them, the word "creation" is written: "He who forms the spirit," and it is written of the water that is above the firmament that they were created in the wasteland. And regarding darkness and light: "He forms light and creates darkness." Why does it say "And the spirit of God hovered over the face of the waters"? To let it be known that the spirit is above the water. Therefore, do not be amazed that in the days of Elijah <note>original: ימי א' (Days of A.)</note>, a cloud "like a man's hand" rose from the sea, for the spirit was there. Furthermore, "the spirit of God" refers to His holy spirit; this is the "Air of Life" which He brought forth from the Spirit of Life that He created for His glory—the Air in which the Holiness resides. From that spirit, all spirits were created. Thus, the spirit is the head of all creatures below and above. In the four directions of the world, there is a spirit in each one, for in every corner of the world, a literal spirit blows within it every day. This is what is said in the *Sefer Yetzirah* <note>The Book of Formation, an early mystical text</note>: "Spirit from Spirit; He engraved and hewed into it." With the breath of the mouth, it is complete and mixed with the three. In it is literal spirit, breadth, and wholeness.

For the spirit emerged from His holy spirit, and from the spirit came water, and from the water came fire. This is "water from the spirit," and in every place where there is water, the spirit is there, as it is written: "The Lord drove the sea back by a strong spirit." At the beginning of the work, the waters were formed by the spirit, for through the spirit, they became damp and moist. The light gathered the moist waters to one side and hewed from them *Tohu* <note>Chaos</note>, the "green line" that encompasses the whole world like the green scum on the surface of water. And *Bohu* <note>Void</note> refers to the "slimy stones" <note>original: אבנים המפולמות</note> sunken in the abyss. The waters emerge from the letter *Mem*. *Mem-Mem-Mem*—water was created. *Mem* is water. It is turned toward the earth where the water is. The letter *Mem* is silent; when a person pronounces it, they use their lips—a sign that waters are silent. *Mem* is the first letter of the words for water, spring, source, fountain, and ritual bath <note>original: מים, מבוע, מקור, מעין, מקוה (Mayim, Mabbua, Makor, Mayan, Mikvah)</note>. Because the waters emerged from His holy word, all purification is in a *mikvah* (ritual bath) of water. The Holy One, blessed be He, is called *Mikvah* three times: twice "The Lord is the Hope (*Mikvah*) of Israel" and once "The Hope (*Mikvah*) of your fathers is the Lord." Thus, a *mikvah* has three names. The numerical value <note>term: Gematria (Hebrew numerology)</note> of *Mikvah* <note>מקוה = 151</note> is equivalent to forty *seahs* <note>The minimum volume of water required for a ritual bath, though the Gematria of 'Mikvah' (151) is often linked to other concepts, while the letter 'Mem' equals 40</note>. And it says in *Sefer Yetzirah*: He made the letter *Mem* king, tied a crown to it, and combined one with another, and formed with them Earth in the world, Cold in the year, and the Belly in the soul, both male and female. This explains that *Mem* is at the head of speech. By joining the letters together, He made them into two utterances; He called one "from fire" and the second "from fire," and He formed Earth in the world, which contains water and cold. The "Belly in the soul" generates cold because of the food it consumes. The male is from fire and the female is from *masha* <note>perhaps a permutation of Mem-Shin</note>. There is a land that gives birth to things, whether among creatures or grasses, for there is a female aspect to everything. Just as when a person speaks in the winter, which is cold, their breath is warm and contains moisture—by the vapor of Your mouth, Exalted One, His speech is a "consuming fire" that emerged from the impression of the *Mem* utterances. Therefore, it is not said how they were created; rather it is written: "The voice of the Lord is over the waters." They were the first to receive the King with praise, saying: "The Lord on high is mighty."

The Torah is compared to water, as it is said: "Ho, everyone who thirsts, come to the waters" <note>Isaiah 55:1</note>. It was His counselor in His work and it purifies those who study it. Therefore, for forty days Moses received the Ten Commandments. And if so, there were another forty days—twice forty <note>original: מ' (Mem/40)</note>—and the Ten Commandments, which equals *Mayim* <note>Water: Mem(40)+Yod(10)+Mem(40) = 90. The translator notes the text's specific arithmetic: 40+40+10=90</note>. By the goodness of the Word, the whole world was filled with water and the spirit hovered over them. From water comes fire, and water comes from the spirit of His holy word. From the power of the heat comes fire, and from it, the Throne was hewn, as we shall explain. The letter *Shin* is open at the top; this is fire, which is the opposite of "Heaven" <note>original: שמים (Shamayim)</note>, which is made of fire. It makes a hissing sound when it tears, making a noise—a sign for fire, which makes a sound when it burns. The *Shin* points upward like a candle; when it is extinguished, the flame rises upward to the place it came from. It is called *Shin* because of "change" <note>original: שינוי (Shinui)</note>. These are the Heavens. It says in *Sefer Yetzirah*: He made the letter *Shin* king, tied a crown to it, and combined one with another, and formed with them Heaven in the world, Heat in the year, and the Head in the soul, both male and female. This explains that He made *Shin* king over fire, engraved it at the head of speech, combined the letters and made them into two utterances. He designated the male with *Shema* and the female with *Sha'am*. He formed Heaven in the world from fire, Heat in the year, and the Head in the body, for heat is generated from the brain. There is heat in the year, and it fears from the heavens to give birth and make things grow, whether male or female creatures. *Shin* is a language of change. *Shin* has three branches, and it is the first letter of "Heaven" <note>Shamayim</note>, in which there is the Sun (fire), the Moon (snow/ice), and the firmament (black). Thus there are three aspects to fire: the sun is like a red coal, the moon is like a white flame, and the firmament is like the dimness of a coal. *Shin* has three branches because fire was created with *Shin*, containing flame, smoke, and coal. There is no fire without the spirit that blows upon it. Therefore, He joined *Aleph* with *Shin*—Fire <note>Esh</note>; with *Aleph*, the spirit was created.

# The Book of Raziel the Great

<insert>
->The Light of Raglan<- <note>original: "אור רגלאן" (Or Raglan); possibly a mystical name for a specific celestial light or an angelic reference</note>
->The Land Above<-
->Water<-
</insert>

In the beginning, the world was like an egg <note>a common medieval metaphor for the cosmos</note> within a law that rises without a shell, white like a celestial sphere. Above and below, the world is surrounded by the firmament. Between all the "shells" <note>original: "קליפות" (qlipot); the outer layers or husks of creation</note> there is light. Light was created from the moisture of the "Sword" <note>original: "חרב" (cherev); likely a reference to the "flaming sword" at the entrance of Eden or a primordial division</note>. He created water from the storehouses of water, rain, and lightning; there are also storehouses of snow. There is a **place** where the waters turn red and there is no darkness, and likewise with snow. Rain comes through lightning, and lightning results from the heat of the lights. He called it "Light" <note>original: "אור" (Or)</note> based on the word for "**Air**" <note>original: "אויר" (Avir)</note> when it is pierced by darkness. Light is necessary for every creative action; therefore, regarding all other things it is written, "and it was so," but regarding light it is written, "and there was light," because everything else requires light. Regarding everything else it is written, "and God saw that it was good," but regarding light it is written, "and God saw the light, that it was good," for everything needs both the light and the action. He said "good" regarding the light but not regarding the heavens, the earth, or the water, because His work was not yet finished. He had only said "**Let there be light**," and not yet the heavens, nor that the earth should be, nor the water. Therefore, it is not written here "and it was so," but rather "and there was light." "And there was light" refers to the circle of the song: **He was** <note>original: "היה" (Hayah)</note> shining between the shells, for the light emerged from His word. This preceded everything, as it is written: "The opening of Your words gives light" <note>Psalm 119:130</note>. That light is sweet and hidden for the righteous, so they may rejoice, as it is written: "The light of the righteous rejoices" <note>Proverbs 13:9</note>.

<margin>The World</margin> One third is water, one third is desert, and one third is inhabited. Additionally, there is earth, heaven, and seas. This corresponds to the **seventy nations, and this is the secret of the seventy** in the **Days of the Messiah**, who performs acts of kindness for all. There is the "Upper Jerusalem," whose boundary is three firmaments on this side and fifty on that side. It is written that the laws were created as solid things; if so, why does one not see the light from the other side? It is because they are like a lead mirror on one side; therefore, they cannot see through. It is written, "and the heavens He meted out with a span" <note>Isaiah 40:12</note>—three handbreadths. The Holy One, blessed be He, took pure fire and pure water, mixed them, and created the heavens (Sham-mayim) <note>a play on "Esh" (fire) and "Mayim" (water) forming "Shamayim" (heavens)</note>. In the beginning, God created the heavens and the earth, and He established seven dwellings above and seven below. He stretched out the heavens above like a great canopy. The "Villon" <note>original: "וילון" (the Curtain); the first of the seven heavens</note> serves no purpose other than as a covering, like a curtain placed over the entrance of a palace when the king enters and is covered, and when he leaves, it is pulled back. So too with the Villon: when the morning star rises and the sun goes out to serve, the Villon rises and covers the face of the first firmament because of the sun's orb. When the sun enters, the Villon is moved so that the moon, stars, and **constellations** <note>original: "מזלות" (Mazalot)</note> can serve the world. The House of Hillel says the earth was created first; if so, why does the text begin with the heavens? Because of the order of the constellations.

Nisan <note>March/April</note> is the first of the months of the year, associated with Fire, and fire is the head of all things, just as light was first for all. Nisan is the head of the year's heat, and its sign is Aries (the Ram), which is Fire. This is the mnemonic T-A-Q <note>Taleh (Aries), Aryeh (Leo), Qeshet (Sagittarius) — the three Fire signs</note>. After Fire comes Earth: Taurus (the Bull) is the sign for Iyar <note>April/May</note>, which is Earth. This is Sh-B-G <note>Shor (Taurus), Betulah (Virgo), Gidi (Capricorn) — the three Earth signs</note>. After that comes Air: Gemini (the Twins) is the sign for Sivan <note>May/June</note>, which is Air. After that comes Water: Cancer (the Crab) is the sign for Tammuz <note>June/July</note>, which is Water. This is S-P-D <note>Sartan (Cancer), Akrav (Scorpio), Dagim (Pisces) — the three Water signs</note>. The cycle repeats: Leo (the Lion) is the sign for Av, Fire; Virgo (the Virgin) is the sign for Elul, Earth; Libra (the Scales) is the sign for Tishrei, Air; Scorpio (the Scorpion) is the sign for Marcheshvan, Water; Sagittarius (the Archer) is the sign for Kislev, Fire; Capricorn (the Goat) is the sign for Tevet, Earth; Aquarius (the Water-Bearer) is the sign for Shevat, Air; and Pisces (the Fish) is the sign for Adar, Water. Your signs are: Sh-Sh-T, P-A-B, M-A-Q, G-D-R <note>these are mnemonics for the months and their elemental qualities</note>. Therefore, in the verse, the order is Heaven, Earth, Dust, and Spirit: **"And the Spirit of God hovered over the face of the waters."**

Just as light was first, so Aries (Fire) is called the first. In Nisan, the chamber of the Temple was contributed to for the daily offerings, to buy lambs (Aries) that must be inspected. When God called to the Light to rule over the Darkness, He said: "Depart, for I wish to create My world with light." The Darkness said to Him: "In what form did You create me before it?" He replied: "If you do not recognize Me, I shall rebuke you and destroy you from the world, for I am creating it with its light, which is fire, the sign of Nisan." Darkness asked: "What will You create after the light?" He said: "You, Darkness, similar to the Bull (Taurus), the sign of Iyar." "And after that?" He said: "Gemini (the Twins), the sign of Sivan—a human who uses both light and darkness, and to whom I will give the two Tablets of the Law." "And after that?" He said: "Cancer (the Crab), which is Water, the sign of Tammuz, for all need water." "And after that?" He said: "Leo (the Lion), the sign of Av, for when a man grows strong like a man, like a lion." "And after that?" He said: "Virgo (the Virgin), the sign of Elul, for in the future, man will delve deep." "And after that?" He said: "Libra (the Scales), the sign of Tishrei, for when a man is as fat as a virgin, they weigh him in the scales." "And after that?" He said: "Scorpio (the Scorpion), the sign of Marcheshvan; just as one is weighed in the scales, if he has sins, they lower him to Gehenna <note>Purgatory/Hell</note>, a place of snakes and scorpions." "And after that?" He said: "Sagittarius (the Archer), the sign of Kislev; once he descends to Gehenna, he has no ascent unless mercy is sought for him, then I shoot him out of there like an arrow from a bow." "And after that?" He said: "Capricorn (the Goat), the sign of Tevet; lest you say that once he descends and rises he has no strength and his face is blackened—rather, he skips and rises like a young goat." "And after that?" He said: "Aquarius (the Water-Bearer), the sign of Shevat, for I pour pure water to purify them from their sins." "And after that?" He said: "Pisces (the Fish), the sign of Adar; once he rises from Gehenna, he inherits the World to Come, where the [evil] eye has no power over him. **Thus we have found in the Midrash.**"

---

<margin>And now</margin> let us return to our previous topic regarding the **Fire** in Nisan: the chamber is contributed to for the needs [of the Temple]. The Bull (Taurus) in Iyar is Earth, for the depths emerge from beneath in the form of a calf that crouches beneath the abyss, as we say in the tractate Taanit; therefore Solomon made the form of a calf with the abyss beneath its feet. Gemini (the Twins) in Sivan corresponds to the Spirit (Air), for no spirit goes out except through the word of death <note>unclear, perhaps a reference to the breath of life and its cessation</note> or through the flowering tree. Cancer (the Crab) in Tammuz corresponds to Water, for there is its lot, and all the offspring of the earth depend on the constellations. Therefore, He put the heavens before the earth, although the earth was created first. Thus far we have explained the workings of the earth. The Holy One, blessed be He, created twelve constellations (Mazalot) as shining lights by day and night, though they are not seen by day because of the **power of the sun's orb**. All are seen at night except those upon which the sun sits at the time of its rising. The measure of one constellation is joined with the next; some are covered and some are revealed. It rises at its dawn and sets, star by star, from one to the other, until this one fully sets and that one rises. This is the order of their rising: Aries and Libra—when Aries stands in the East, Libra sets in the West. Aries is a star...

<meta>This page continues the cosmological narrative, moving from the general structure of the celestial "shells" and the primordial light into the specific mechanics of how the elements—Water, Fire, and Light—were derived from the Divine Names to form the heavens, the Earth, and the atmosphere.</meta>

# The Book of Raziel the Great

He spoke, and there was darkness over the face of the deep—the darkness of the clouds, a thickness of water that dwells beside the water. From the beginning, the world's "skin" was a darkness that possessed physical substance <note>original: "mamashut"; implying the primordial darkness was a tangible element, not just an absence of light</note>. This substantial darkness existed for many thousands of myriads of years—it has no beginning. Thus His world stands, while the Holy One, Blessed be He, exists in other worlds that "no eye has seen, O God, besides You" <note>quoting Isaiah 64:3</note>. Before the exit of the Sabbath, close to evening, He shone light for a brief moment, like a command, only to let it darken again like the darkness of night. Then the Holy One, Blessed be He, consulted with His Torah on how to create His world. The Torah was written upon His arm in white fire over black fire, and seventy-two Names of the Holy One, Blessed be He, were engraved upon His right hand.

He was solitary in His world. He looked "this way and that" and saw that "there was no man," and He desired to walk alone, as it is said: "But He is one, and who can turn Him? And what His soul desires, even that He does." Immediately, He stretched out His hand and took one Name, and from it, He produced three drops of water. The entire world was filled with water, and the Divine Presence (*Shekhinah*) was over the waters, as it is written: "And the spirit of God hovered over the face of the waters." He divided the water into three parts: one third suspended by His word, one third in the springs, and one third in the sea.

He took a second Name and produced from it three drops of light. He divided it into three: one for this world, one for the World to Come, and one for the days of the Messiah.

He took a third Name and produced from it three drops of fire. He divided it into three: from one, the angels were created; from one, the Holy Creatures (*Hayot HaKodesh*); and from one, a "fire that is not blown" <note>a mystical, eternal fire</note>. Thus, the primordial matter <term>Hyle: the basic, unformed substance of the universe</term> was filled with water, light, and fire. Fire was to His right, light was to His left, and water was beneath Him. He took them and spread them into one another: He mixed fire and water, and the heavens (*Shamayim*) were made <note>a classic Hebrew pun: *Esh* (fire) + *Mayim* (water) = *Shamayim* (heavens)</note>. He took water and light and created His Throne of Glory. He took fire and light and created from them the Holy Creatures and the ministering angels. As it is written: "The Lord by wisdom founded the earth." Where does this "Wisdom" come from? From these seventy-two Names of the Holy One, Blessed be He, which are engraved upon His arm. Seventy Names remained, each of which is capable of creating another world.

So we find in the Midrash: "The Lord by wisdom founded the earth." But I received it as it is written in the *Book of Formation* <note>original: "Sefer Yetzirah," a foundational text of Jewish mysticism</note>:
1. One: The Spirit of the Living God.
2. Two: The will of His spirit and His desire. Two is His Holy Spirit, derived from the spirit of the air and the Holy Spirit, so that the spirit should not go out and depart to the seven winds—a tangible spirit. And from the many, the spirit went forth.
3. Three: Water from the spirit; this is the moisture of the many dampnesses.
4. Four: Fire from water; this is the essence of heat found in speech.
5. Five: Height and Depth.
6. Six: East.
7. Seven: West.
8. Eight: South.
9. Nine: North. <note>The text counts ten "Sefirot" or emanations, though the numbering here aligns with the directions and elements</note>

From the speech—at the time when the world was only spirit, water, and fire—He took fire and water and mixed them together and created the heavens. He called out their skin: "Let there be light!" Not because He required illumination, for "light dwells with Him," and it is written, "He wraps Himself in light as with a garment." Rather, He was like a man who builds a dwelling and lights a lamp to illuminate the house; so He decreed upon the waters to shine for the sake of the clarity of the atmospheric spirit, which resembles a mist. And when He speaks from within the fire—
<gloss>The fire of the spirit is what resembles the release of very fine smoke. It is what encompasses the space between heaven and earth. It was divided: first, then second in the wilderness, third in the mountains, and fourth in the inhabited world. It rules in the inhabited world, yet no eye can perceive it. One who wishes to see it should be in a house that has three windows; when the sunbeam emerges through the windows into the house, then one will see the "bitterness" of the beam, tied and standing from the windows—that is the air.</gloss>
—and in the winter days, one can see with a fine eye that which rises from the air. I shall raise it up, for if it enters the springs, the water, and the warmth of the earth—which does not accept it—it is seen as it exits outside. Furthermore, in the winter, when the air is cold to the body, a breath <note>original: "nihor"</note> comes out of a person's mouth like fine smoke. But in the summer, when the air is hot and the body is hot, they do not differ from one another.

The heavens are suspended in the air, and the earth is suspended from the air, and the sphere rolls, so to speak, yet the earth does not move from its place. This is like a glass vessel that is wide with a narrow mouth; if one places cumin, or a dry leaf, or a bird’s wing, or a garlic skin inside and blows into the mouth of the vessel with force until the vessel is filled with air, then whatever is in the vessel will rise into the air in the middle as long as the spirit is in it. So the earth is suspended in the air. The nature of the air is moist, like the power of the sun on a hot day. But at night, if you lie under the open air and cover yourself with a woolen garment or leather, the next morning you will find it moist with dew. This moisture comes from the mouth, reacting against the "light-breath" in the air that exits the mouth—this is the air of the spirit, water, and light.

The Holy One, Blessed be He, cannot be truly compared to anything, but we use comparisons so the listener may understand. Thus, the "breath" <note>original: "gehor"</note> came from the mouth of the Master, and from the moisture of its flow, the waters were formed. From the power of that breath surrounding the mist that covers the rivers, the "unformed and void" (*Tohu wa-Bohu*) was made. This is the "Green Line" <note>original: "Kav ha-Yarok"; a mystical line that encompasses the world</note> which resembles the heights and the arc of the rainbow. When a person is at sea in a ship and a wave is over them, they can see the Green Line above, reddish at the edges of the heavens, and black below near the deep—which is the place of darkness. In the vastness of the sea, one can look from end to end. The glory of the world exists from heaven to earth, for the depths of the sea are fifteen cubits higher than all the high mountains. Indeed, in their movement in the sea, a man may climb to the top of the mast to look at the curvature of the earth; because of the height of the sea, he will see that when a man is at sea far from the land, the whole land appears before him like a pot.

From the foam of the waters, they froze along the engravings of the water, becoming like snow in the cold and heat, carved out like the rows of spice-beds where we see types of "garments" <note>strata or layers</note>. They have a boundary, so to speak, so that the "spices" do not mix—like "his cheeks are as a bed of spices." This is "established as a wall," for "the waters were a wall unto them," like a ceiling in a deep storm to place the rains within. The clouds existed around the water as is their custom.

There was darkness, and one cannot speak of the light and how it emerged from His word. Yet we see fire and light and their colors in many forms. Sometimes its voice is heard, but we cannot stand upon its essence. How then can we understand that which is hidden and secret? Only as our ancestors received it in our hands shall we tell: how He commanded the water to shine by His holy word.

Let me tell you a parable: They said it is like a man who engraved letters from the likeness of those angels who were secret. The Holy One, Blessed be He, rebuked them and cast them down from the high heavens. Adam the First <note>Adam HaRishon</note> found them, and he returned and engraved their likeness from Aleph to Tav <note>the Hebrew alphabet, A to Z</note>. Thus the waters were shining from the angels within them. Furthermore, just as a man lights a lamp in his house to give light, so when He created His world through speech, the Holy One, Blessed be He, commanded the clarity of the water to bring forth light to illuminate His world. "And there was light"—this is a wondrous formation in our hands: that light was created from water. For in many places, water is called "light," as it is said: "He spreads the cloud of His light," and "He covers the light with His hands," like the "eye of light" <note>source of light</note> and within it a spring of water. Know that all of these are related to "tears" <note>moisture</note>. Therefore, light is called "Day" (*Yom*), which is related to the language of "Sea" (*Yam*) and "Water" (*Mayim*). This is "Let there be light"—let the darkness of the clouds depart, and the spirit that binds the water and the light between the shells. For the heavens...

It uses physical analogies, such as sunbeams in a window and air pressure in a glass vessel, to explain the suspension of the Earth and the nature of the atmosphere.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the cosmological exposition from the previous section, moving from the primordial elements into the specific correspondence between the macrocosm (the universe) and the microcosm (the human body). # The Book of Raziel the Great

And the world was created from fire, dust, spirit <note>original: "ruach"; can mean wind, spirit, or air</note>, and water. Red bile corresponds to fire, black bile corresponds to dust, phlegm <note>original: "halacha"; the moist humor</note> corresponds to water, and blood corresponds to the spirit, for "the blood is the soul" <note>quoting Deuteronomy 12:23</note> and the soul is the brain. Furthermore, I shall teach you the secret of the creation of the world's elements:

Initially, spirit was created from the Spirit of the Creator of all. From the spirit that was created, water and light were formed. From the water, fire and snow were created. From that fire which was created from the water, the heavens were made. From the snow which was created from the water, the earth was made. After the earth and heavens were created, the spirit of life, the heavens, and the light remained. From the heavens, heat and dryness were born—which correspond to fire. From the light, spirit <note>air</note> was born. From the earth, dryness and cold were born. From the water, moisture and cold were born.

Thus are the four elements of the body created: his spirit of life comes from the "father and mother" <note>referring to the biological parents or the spiritual emanations</note> from the Spirit of the Creator of all. From the spirit of life of the father and mother, the seed is created. The blood corresponds to water, and the blood also corresponds to the air. From the "evil" <note>likely referring to the physical waste or lower drives</note> that the father and mother expel, red bile is created; and the phlegm, which is like snow, is created from this.

By the word of the Lord, the world was established in water and spirit. Fire was beside Him like a throne upon the waters, and the King of Glory sat upon it. The Torah was written in **white fire upon black fire** upon His arm. The Torah was placed in His lap, hidden and as a source of delight. The Throne stands by the word of His mouth, for the living creatures <note>original: "hayot"; the high-ranking angels who carry the Throne</note> had not yet been created. From the Garden <note>Eden</note> according to its kind, and in the center, a curtain of light is spread, and within it is engraved every image of creatures and souls hidden in a bundle beneath the Throne.

And here is His request: within it are seven dwellings. Repentance stands between the Garden and Eden. The Holy Temple is built before Him, and the Presence (*Shekhinah*) of His glory is in the center. There, the name of the King Messiah is engraved upon a precious stone on the Altar. The Spirit of God bore it until He created the world. 

He consulted with the Torah, whose name is "Wisdom" <note>original: "tushiyah"</note>, regarding the creation of the world. The Torah replied: "Create the angels who do not sin." [The Torah continued:] "Behold, the Lord is in Sodom! Do not deliver me into the hand of a man who 'drinks iniquity like water' <note>quoting Job 15:16</note>." 

The Holy One, Blessed be He, replied: "I have already created **Repentance** as a remedy for those who sin in their souls, so that their souls may be healed. And the **Temple** to offer sacrifices for atonement; and the **Garden of Eden** for the souls of the righteous; and **Gehenna** for those who transgress the commandments; and the **Torah** to occupy themselves with; and the **Messiah** to gather the exiles." 

Immediately, the Torah was appeased. She returned and said: "Master of the Universe! You are the God of gods. Who shall tell You what to do? If a king has no army, over whom does he rule?" He spoke pleasing words of Torah to her, and she listened to Him. He then struck the heavens, and His majesty and strength became the garments for the firmaments and the seven lands of the nations.

<margin>5</margin>
"Behold, to the Lord your God belong the heavens and the highest heavens, the earth and all that is in it" <note>quoting Deuteronomy 10:14</note>. This verse begins with the letter *He* <note>the 5th letter of the Hebrew alphabet, noted in the margin</note> and ends with *He*. Between the earth and the firmament is a journey of 500 years; so too is the thickness of each firmament, and so too from one firmament to the next, and from one end of the world to the other. 

Now, "incline your ear like a hopper" <note>a traditional idiom for listening intently</note> and hear the sound of my words. Put the fear of Heaven in your heart, and you shall contemplate and understand the matter of this world: 

He took snow from beneath the Throne of Glory and threw it upon the waters. The waters froze and became the dust of the earth, for "He says to the snow: 'Become earth'" <note>quoting Job 37:6</note>. From where did that snow come? By the word of the Great King, the waters froze. The whiteness within them became the treasuries of snow. He took snow from under the Throne to say: "The earth is my footstool." Just as snow is white but its melted waters are murky, so too are all creatures white and good in their appearance, but in the end, they return to the dust. From this snow come seven "horns" <note>power sources or directions</note>; thus are all the works of the earth: six days, and for the earth there are six statutes—summer and cold and heat, and summer and winter. 

It says in the *Book of Formation* <note>original: "Sefer Yetzirah," a foundational Kabbalistic text</note>: He carved and hewed water from spirit. He made them into *Tohu* (Chaos) and *Bohu* (Void), mud and clay. He arranged them like a garden bed, set them up like a wall, and covered them like a ceiling. He placed snow upon them and it became dust, as it is said: "For He says to the snow: 'Become earth'." 

*Tohu* is the **green line** <note>original: "kav yarok"</note> that encompasses the entire world. From it come the **moist stones** <note>original: "avanim mefulhamot"</note> that are sunken in the abyss, from which the waters flow. The explanation of "water from spirit" is this: the moisture is set to one side, and He hewed *Tohu* from them. This is the green line that surrounds the world like the green film on the surface of water. Within it are the moist stones from which water emerges, for they were created from water. 

Should a person not believe this—that a stone can be created from water—let him take common copper, make a "stone" from copper, take clear water and boil it over a fire. Every time the water diminishes, he should add more water over many years. Eventually, he will break the copper vessel and find a stone inside it. So too in man and beast, you find small stones formed from the moisture of the body; through the heat of the liver, a stone is made in the liver, or the kidneys, or elsewhere. 

When the Holy One, Blessed be He—who is a "consuming fire"—spoke His words, the waters became mountains, rocks, and dust, as He separated the moisture and brought forth water from it. The dust and the *Tohu* that surrounds it became visible to the observer. In the vision, the "pillars of heaven" (which are the offspring of fire) stood with the "pillars of the earth" (which came from the water). When the waters were gathered together, they made mud, clay, and soot. He arranged them like a garden bed in the depths, set them up like a wall, and surrounded them like a ceiling. From the power of that mud and clay—a piece of ceiling—He placed snow upon them, and they froze and became dust. This is what is meant in *Sefer Yetzirah*: "He made substance from *Tohu*." That which was *Tohu* He made into substance, and the mud, clay, and earth He fixed so they would have substance. 

Thus, He made the earth and created the **Rock** over the abyss (*Tehom*). He engraved upon it the **Explicit Name of forty-two letters** and fixed it over the face of the abyss to restrain its waters. He marked it with two letters. Every matter of the abyss is governed by His Name. From the heavens to the earth, [the Name] *Ehyeh Asher Ehyeh* <note>I Am That I Am</note>. *Ehyeh* is below, and *Yah* is above, corresponding to the Crown (*Keter*). 

The Throne is sealed with *Yah*. The Name *Y-H-V-H* is comprised of three letters of fire and flame. With *Y-H-V-H*, He created heaven and earth. Therefore, its beginning is *Yod* and *He*. "Let the heavens rejoice and the earth be glad" <note>the first letters of this phrase in Hebrew spell the Tetragrammaton Y-H-V-H</note>. For by His Great Name, everything is sustained. "I am the Lord, who makes all things." 

The Rock upon which the forty-two letters were engraved is placed over the abyss so the waters do not rise. But when the generation of the Flood sinned, He removed the "pipe" <note>the stopper or channel</note> and immediately the waters burst forth—"all the fountains of the great deep." When the generation [of David] dug the drainage channels <note>original: "shittin"; the foundation shafts of the Temple</note>, they found the pipe. David said: "Woe is me, have I labored for this in vain?" When he sought to found the Temple, the pipe was rolled away and the waters surged and filled the whole world. David fell upon his face and said: "A Song of Ascents: Out of the depths I have called You, O Lord" <note>Psalm 130</note>. He descended into the channels very deep and raised it with fifteen...

<meta>This page continues the cosmological discussion of the elements, moving from the internal composition of the body and the primary elements into the celestial mechanics of the zodiac. It establishes the rigid order of the stars and their correspondence to the seasons, the Hebrew calendar, and the 12-fold structure of the Torah and the human body. It maintains the "as above, so below" philosophy established on the previous page.</meta>

# The Book of Raziel the Great

In the season of heat, it sets in the west. The star <note>original: "kochav"; often refers specifically to Mercury in medieval astronomy, or a general celestial body</note> of Libra rises; Aries fully sets, Libra fully rises. Taurus and Scorpio, Gemini and Sagittarius, Cancer and Capricorn, Leo and Aquarius, Virgo and Pisces—all of them depart and enter according to a number, as if in a book. How so? They rise from the east by number and set in the west by number, and not one of them changes its order, nor does any enter the territory of its companion. The constellation <note>term: "Mazal" (constellation/zodiac sign)</note> that serves during the time of sowing does not serve during the time of harvest; the one that serves during the time of harvest does not serve in the winter; the one that serves in winter does not serve in summer; the one that serves in summer does not serve in the cold; and the one that serves in the cold does not serve in the time of heat.

**This** is the order of their service: half of Libra, Scorpio, and half of Sagittarius—these serve during the sowing season for 60 days. Half of Sagittarius, Capricorn, and half of Aquarius serve during the days of winter for 61 days. Half of Aquarius, Pisces, and half of Aries—these serve during the days of the cold for 61 days. Half of Aries, Taurus, and half of Gemini—these serve during the harvest days for 61 days. Half of Gemini, Cancer, and half of Leo—these serve during the summer days for 61 days. Half of Leo, Virgo, and half of Libra serve during the days of heat for 61 days. The Sages of Israel say: the firmament is made like a dome <note>original: "kuba"; a vaulted tent or pavilion</note>; the sphere is fixed and the constellations revolve. But the sages of the nations say: the sphere revolves and the constellations are fixed within it. The [Jewish] Sages replied to their words: we never find Scorpio in the south or the Great Bear <note>original: "Agala"; literally "the Wagon," referring to the Big Dipper/Ursa Major</note> in the north—the Great Bear does not move from its place and does not revolve like a sphere. The signs are: T-A-Q <note>Taleh (Aries), Aryeh (Leo), Qeshet (Sagittarius)</note> for Fire; Sh-K-G <note>Shor (Taurus), Betulah (Virgo), Gidi (Capricorn)</note> for Earth; T-M-R <note>Teomim (Gemini), Moznayim (Libra), Dli (Aquarius)</note> for Air; S-I-D <note>Sartan (Cancer), Aqrav (Scorpio), Dagim (Pisces)</note> for Water. And there is no human who knows the essential root of the constellations except for the Holy One, Blessed be He, who created them. One cannot replicate His deeds, as it is written: "Which God created, to do" <note>Genesis 2:3</note>. Everything that the Holy One, Blessed be He, created in this world, He created its counterpart through the breath of His mouth. Everything He created in heaven and on earth—who can do according to Your works and Your mighty deeds? He ordered the constellations to make His works known to His children and so they might bless Him for them. It appears that the constellations revolve, for a constellation is called *Mazal* because it "flows" <note>original: "mazal" connected to "ozel" (to go/flow)</note> and they prostrate themselves to His glory, as it is written: "and the host of heaven prostrates to You" <note>Nehemiah 9:6</note>, for by the breath of His mouth He created all their host. Four [elements] above and below correspond to the four divisions. Four above and below correspond to the twelve divisions. Four above and below are T-A-Q Fire, Sh-K-G Earth, T-M-R Air, S-I-D Water.

Corresponding to the twelve constellations is the letter *Gimel* <note>The third letter of the Hebrew alphabet</note> with which they begin, for they are... <note>The text here contains cryptic alphabetical permutations regarding the letters Zayin and Mem</note>... it is written "month" <note>original: "Chodesh"</note>, and there are twelve large joints in the hands and feet, and twelve tribes, and twelve kings surrounding the Throne of Glory, and twelve blessings added in every prayer, and twelve "spheres" of Torah. These are: (1) The Torah of the Lord is perfect, (2) restoring the soul, (3) sure, (4) making wise, (5) right, (6) rejoicing the heart, (7) pure <note>original: "bara"</note>, (8) enlightening, (9) clean, (10) enduring, (11) truth, (12) righteous altogether <note>These are twelve descriptors of the Torah found in Psalm 19</note>. Therefore, there are twelve hours in the day so that you may occupy yourself with these twelve spheres of Torah, and then bread will be provided for you to eat; for there were twelve loaves of the Showbread <note>The "Lechem HaPanim" kept in the Temple</note> containing twenty-four tenths of flour, given to the priests who teach Torah twelve hours by day and twelve by night—twenty-four books <note>The 24 books of the Hebrew Bible</note>. Therefore, it is juxtaposed to: "The Lord possessed me in the beginning of His way" <note>Proverbs 8:22</note>, "she has slaughtered her meat, she has mixed her wine, she has sent forth her maidens, she cries out" <note>Proverbs 9:2-3</note>—these are the priests. "Whoso is simple, let him turn in hither"—if he does not wish to occupy himself with Torah, let him have this: "Come, eat of my bread" <note>Proverbs 9:5</note>, for the Torah is bread, and the Showbread is twelve, and there are twelve names for the Torah: (1) Beginning, (2) Ancient, (3) From then, (4) From everlasting, (5) From the head, (6) From the earliest, (7) When there were no [depths], (8) When there were no [fountains], (9) When there were no..., (10) Before, (11) Before, (12) While as yet [He had not made] <note>These 12 terms are derived from the description of Wisdom/Torah in Proverbs 8:22-26</note>. And another twelve: (1) His way, (2) His works, (3) He makes, (4) In our mouth, (5) In His bosom, (6) In the midst, (7) In His strength, (8) When He established, (9) In His decrees, (10) Beside Him, (11) Before Him, (12) His earth—to say that He created twelve constellations for the sake of the Torah. And just as the constellations are divided into four, so is the Torah: to "watch at my doors daily"—(1) when you sit, (2) when you walk, (3) when you rise, (4) when you lie down. And the year is divided into four seasons. A man is obligated to serve/study three seasons by day and night... <note>unclear reference to the months of Av and Yair</note>. Therefore it is juxtaposed: "And you will listen to His commandments and keep all His statutes" <note>Exodus 15:26</note>... on the fifteenth day of the second month... if he studies, God will provide all his needs. The fetus is covered in its mother's womb for three parts of the year... and is circumcised at eight days...

It is written: "And you forgot the Torah of your God, I also will forget your children" <note>Hosea 4:6</note>. And further, it juxtaposes "your fathers" with "the Rock that begot you, you unmindful" <note>Deuteronomy 32:18</note>, a hint to Abraham: "Look to the rock from which you were hewn" <note>Isaiah 51:1</note>, for Abraham occupied himself with Torah and was an expert in the constellations, and [God] "brought him outside"—meaning, "Go out from your astrology!" <note>Reference to a Talmudic teaching that God told Abraham to look above his astrological fate</note>. Furthermore, four are drawn above and below corresponding to the divided constellations. T-A-Q is Fire—corresponding to the "above" which is entirely fire. Sh-K-G is Earth—corresponding to the "below" which is earth. T-M-R is Air—behold, it moves above and below. S-A-R <note>Sartan, Aqrav, Dagim</note> is Water—it also moves above and below. Behold, they are Air and Water, just as when a person breathes during the rainy days, the breath of his mouth resembles a cloud, and it is water. During the rainy days, no sprout is found in any color except green... because the rain is the husband of the earth and the earth longs for it, therefore it sprouts what resembles it: green. For if one puts rainwater in a stone vessel and it remains for many days, it becomes greenish. But in the summer days, when the constellations serve with the sun... all kinds of rains/liquids... therefore the blossoms turn into all kinds of colors. And why is T-A-Q, the first ones, Fire? Because the heavens are the primary part of creation and they are above. One might say the heavens should have been T-M-R Air, for the air/wind is above all flying things; however, the heavens are bound together, and so is the earth, and both were made by His hand, as it is said: "My hand also has laid the foundation of the earth" <note>Isaiah 48:13</note>. And the Air T-M-R took hold of the Earth, because the air/wind shakes the earth when it rouses it. And the fourth, S-A-R, is Water, because the water descends below to a low place like the sea... and the dew descends, as it is written: "The poor from the dust" <note>Psalm 113:7</note>. There are four seasons in the year:
The Season of Nissan <note>Spring</note>: Hot and Moist.
The Season of Tammuz <note>Summer</note>: Hot and Dry.
The Season of Tishrei <note>Autumn</note>: Cold and Dry.
The Season of Tevet <note>Winter</note>: Cold and Moist.
The four elements of man: Blood, Phlegm, Red Bile, Black Bile. Cold, Heat, Summer, Winter. There is Earth, Water, Air—four are the foundations of the body: Blood, Phlegm, Red Bile, and Black Bile, and the seasons correspond to them: Cold, Heat, Summer, and Winter.

<summary>This page details the astronomical and elemental correspondences of the twelve zodiac signs, aligning them with the four seasons, the four human humors, and the twelve-fold structure of the Torah. It emphasizes the divine order of the universe and the Jewish philosophical view that the stars are fixed paths ordained by God, meant to be understood through the lens of Torah study.</summary>
