# The Great Book of Raziel

The King who reigns over all, and the double heavens <note>Hebrew: *ha-shamayim ha-kepulim*; likely referring to the dual nature of the heavens or the concept of 'Heavens of Heavens'</note> which appear like a Throne; this is the meaning of "The heavens declare the glory of God" <note>Psalm 19:1</note>. It stands thus: above the heavens is a sea, and above the sea is water, and for the sea, there are lightning and thunders hovering over all of them. Strong walls of fire surround Him, and a great ladder is set in the heavens with its top reaching the firmament. Holy angels and honored princes are appointed over it.

Angels are appointed over the **Gates of the North**: they are five.
And over the **Gates of the South**: they are five. <note>The text uses a shorthand symbol ':' likely indicating the count.</note>
Over the **Gates of the East**: they are six.
Over the **Gates of the West**: they are six.

The angels dwell within the **round calf's foot** <note>original: *kaf regel egel agul*; a reference to the 'straight feet' of the living creatures in Ezekiel 1:7, which were like the sole of a calf's foot</note>. From their wings outward, they are divided to fulfill that which is said: "each one went straight forward; they did not turn as they went" <note>Ezekiel 1:9</note>. Because the circle is outside the protrusions in the round calf's foot—the translation of which is "round legs"—it is a sole of a foot. The reason their feet are round is that darkness is the beginning of His creation, and its likeness is as an ox; therefore, Solomon made twelve oxen under the Sea <note>The 'Brazen Sea' in the Temple, 1 Kings 7:25</note>. And why is it a "straight foot"? So that the foot of the ox is not larger than that of the lion, nor that of the man larger than that of the eagle. "How manifold are Your works, O Lord!" He knows that just as we have drawn it, so it is.

There are three protrusions for the Throne: one from here and one from there, and it is round. Behold, the levels are so great and so small; I shall explain both. Why are there three protrusions? There are steps like the letter *Bet* (ב); such is the form of the Throne. And three in the appearance of **Chashmal** <term>Chashmal; often translated as 'glowing amber' or 'electrum', the mystical substance seen in Ezekiel’s vision</term>. It is in the appearance of the *Chashmal* and the "likeness of a sapphire stone" and so on. One is from above in the calling, and they made it like a *Bet*. Therefore, in the *Alphabet of Rabbi Akiva* <note>A mystical midrashic text where the letters of the alphabet argue before God</note>, it says that when the letter *Bet* descended from the Throne of Glory, the spheres trembled. The Holy One, Blessed be He, said to them: "Why do you tremble?" They replied: "The *Bet* that was upon our heads has descended, and all our glory is within it." "Exalted from the beginning" refers to "they shall speak of the glory of Your kingdom." And it is written: "And the top of the throne was round," and under His feet were steps, and one small one corresponding to it.

Solomon, peace be upon him, said: "Hear in Heaven, Your dwelling place" <note>1 Kings 8:39</note>. From the heavens, the *Bet* is His throne and the earth is His footstool. A house of rest for the Master and for His footstool, the house of our God. And we equate it to His footstool—"Holy is He." We equate it to His footstool, yet He did not mention His footstool. There are seven "fifths" <note>likely referring to the five books of the Torah multiplied or categorized</note> and one *Mishneh*—the Book of Deuteronomy <note>original: *Mishneh Torah*</note>. Therefore, one bows to the Torah, for it was His footstool within the Ark. So too were seven thrones made like the heavens, and His throne is not just one. The heavens are bent and seen within it, and over it is the appointed **Ox**.

The main matter we shall explain: the Throne of Glory in the creation of the King of Glory, and the **stature** <note>original: *kumah*; referring to the mystical dimensions</note> of the Prince of the Host, **Sadriel**, who is appointed over his angels. Among them is the Officer appointed over twelve thousand. There are *Chokhelah* angels standing at the gates of heaven. There are many therein who "declare the glory of God," as it is written: "The heavens declare the glory of God."

### 9
Corresponding to the **Firmament** that divides, for the word *Rakia* (Firmament) is found nine times in the Book of Genesis until the "waters" that praise Him above the heavens:
1. In the heights.
2. His angels.
3. His hosts.
4. The sun.
5. His moon.
6. Stars.
7. Heavens of heavens.
8. And the waters that are above the heavens.
9. So too, "a place for Your dwelling" <note>original: *makhon le-shivtekha*</note>.

The dwelling place is the heavens, and there are nine kinds of fire in the **Work of the Chariot** <term>Ma'aseh Merkavah; the highest level of Jewish mysticism concerning the Divine Chariot</term> corresponding to nine visions in the work of the firmament. Above the heavens is a firmament in which are fixed the Sun, Moon, Pleiades, Orion, stars, and all the ruling constellations, and every deep. A great ladder is set in the firmament, and its top reaches the clouds <note>original: *shachakim*; the third heaven</note>. Angels of fire and honored princes are appointed over it; they are seven, and they are appointed over the gates of the firmament and the gates of the "work of the firmament."

At the gates of the **North Wind**, six princes are appointed.
At the gates of the **South Wind**, seven princes.
At the **East**, six princes.
At the **West**, six princes.

In the **Second Firmament**, there is frost, incense, storehouses of snow, storehouses of hail, angels of fire, angels of judgment, spirits of terror, and spirits of fear. That firmament is full of dread, for within it stand angels without end, formed into hosts. Over them are honored princes and officers. In the firmament, there are twelve degrees/ascents, and in every single degree stand angels in their glory.

**On the First Degree** stand nine princes and officers, their standing is in heat <note>or "sun-darkened" / *ahama*</note>, wrapped in awe, girded with fear, entwined with trembling. Their likeness is the form of fire, their faces like the appearance of lightning, and their mouths are never silent from speaking many things, yet their voice is not heard, for their work is to serve, to terrify, and to dismay all who stand over them.

**On the Second Degree** stand eleven princes. They stand with strength, filled with the power of affection and love. Opposite them a fire burns, and they run and bring the constellations of humans close to one another.

**On the Third Degree** stand fourteen princes. They agitate, cause trembling, and provoke humans. They are stumbling blocks to humans and frustrate their thoughts. They are mighty in strength; terror is before them and trembling behind them. They roar and cause quaking, walking and making noise with a sound of crashing and thunder. In their hands are rods of fire, and their faces are like sparks of fire coming out of their eyes. All of them are ready to nullify and to break.

**On the Fourth Degree** stand sixteen princes. Seven are girded with the storm. The sound of their distress is like the sound of flying copper <note>or "hissing serpents" / *nachash hash*</note> from the East, and they turn to the North. Their voice is like lightning and fire is around them. They take away sleep from humans; they have the power to do good or evil.

**On the Fifth Degree** stand twelve princes. They hold shields and spears, with helmets on their heads and scale-armor as their clothing. To their right and left are stones of hail. They run with trembling and walk upon fire, holding torches. They hasten and whisper from the corner <note>or "from zenah"</note>, and their mouths are never silent from roaring. Their breath is like burning fire, and a flaming fire kindles from the wind of their nostrils, for their works are in the storehouses of fire; from the fire they came, and in fire is their station.

**On the Sixth Degree** stand seventeen princes. They conduct themselves with humility and are full of glory. They are clothed in white garments and are awesome. Their seat is upon many thrones. They are faithful to the Truth and to the true, perfect healing.

**On the Seventh Degree** stand eleven princes. They are girded with the strength of their might in terror. Half of them resemble fire and half of them resemble water. They are wondrous in their works, and one cannot understand their form, for fire is in their stature and sparks are in their eyelids. They stand in awe. Those who stand below them cannot look upon their appearance, and those who stand above them are terrified by their sight. They walk to the three directions of the world.

**On the Eighth Degree** stand nineteen princes...

# The Great Book of Raziel

At night, one may speak with the stars to ask questions and speak with the spirits; for all of them, one must call upon the Name in purity. Under the **second shofar**, there are 37 <note>The Hebrew letters ל"ז (Lamed-Zayin) represent the number 37.</note> angels of Might, the luminaries who have the power and strength to run and fly to all corners of the world; for all of them, one must call upon the Name in purity. Under the **seventh shofar**, there are 46 <note>The Hebrew letters מ"ו (Mem-Vav) represent the number 46.</note> who tell of every matter in a dream; for all of them, one must call upon the Name in purity, and he shall succeed.

# **Amual**
The angel appointed over the **Chulin** <note>original: *Chulin*; likely referring to a specific level of the heavens or the 'profane' firmament in this cosmological system</note> firmament, who is in charge of twelve thousand angels. There are many paths in the heavens; when a path lengthens, it is composed of black fire, and at the point where it ends, there is a great mountain of white fire that separates it—a pure fire without visibility. From there onward, it spreads to another path. At the break in the path, there is pure white fire, and so on. The paths wind about, and the angels have their own routes; one goes this way according to his duty, and some travel in circuits until they ascend the ladder from firmament to firmament, reaching the **Chariot** <note>original: *Merkavah*; the Divine Chariot or the highest realm of God's presence</note>. However, there are angels who do not merit to join <note>The text notes an alternate reading: "to be commanded"</note> the body of the Chariot. Wherever it is written "the heavens" or "in the heavens," it implies the space of the dome or up to the body of the firmament. But "the heavens above the heavens" is like a room within a room. Know that all the heavens are made like a vault, "and He stretched them out like a tent to dwell in" <note>Isaiah 40:22</note> upon pillars.

->**Cosmic Diagram**<-
| **I Shall Be** <note>original: *Ehyeh*; the divine name associated with Keter and pure existence</note> | **The Heavens as a Vault** | **I Shall Be** |
| :--- | :---: | ---: |
| | **To the Firmament of the Earth** | |
| | **Upon the Waters** | |
->And here is the Splendor of His Glory, as it is above.<-

The celestial rings <note>original: *karkasi*; likely related to the Greek *kirkos*, meaning circles or rings</note> and the sea are equal; they are held within the waters of **Oceanus** <note>The great world-encircling water in ancient cosmology</note>. The waters of Oceanus are between the ends of the heavens and the ends of the earth. The ends of the earth and the ends of the heavens are spread out upon the waters of Oceanus, as it is said: "Who lays the beams of His upper chambers in the waters" <note>Psalm 104:3</note>. The interior of the water rises upward like a spread tent, and human beings stand beneath it for burial below, and its width is below. These are the heavens, narrowed below and wide in the middle above. All creatures sit beneath them as in a tent to dwell. He sealed the heavens and the earth with the seal of **I Shall Be That I Shall Be** <note>original: *Ehyeh Asher Ehyeh*; the name revealed to Moses at the burning bush</note>. The letters *Aleph-Heh* are from below, and *Vav-Heh* are from above. There are three seals of *Ehyeh Asher Ehyeh*: "I Shall Be That I Shall Be has sent me" <note>Exodus 3:14</note>. The twelve letters correspond to the twelve hours of the day and the twelve hours of the night, the twelve months of the year, and the twelve signs of the zodiac.

->**Diagram of the Throne**<-
| **I Shall Be** | **Eagle** | **Here is the place of Honor in awe** |
| :--- | :---: | :--- |
| | **Eagle** | **The feet of the Throne—not literally, but double heavens, thus it is explained.** |
| | **Eagle** | |
->And under His feet<-

<margin>
(Note)
These twelve letters refer to the three-fold *Ehyeh Asher Ehyeh*. That is to say, if you look at the diagram before you, you will find three instances of *Ehyeh*: twice as *Ehyeh Asher Ehyeh* and once as *Ehyeh*. Thus heaven and earth are sealed with three *Ehyeh* names, etc., as in the chapter.
</margin>

The twelve tribes are also sealed: four for each direction. Three letters above and two letters below: *Aleph-Heh* from below, *Yod-Heh* from above for three directions—three *Ehyeh* names. The fourth direction is flying and standing, and it is not sealed. There is a time when it is sealed to Him by *Yod-Heh, Yah, Yod-Heh* from above and *Vav-Heh* from below. The sign for this is: "Let the heavens be glad, and let the earth rejoice" <note>Psalm 96:11</note>. "Let the heavens be glad" (*Yishmechu*) begins with *Yod-Heh* above, and "and let the earth rejoice" (*Vetagel*) ends with *Vav-Heh* below. At the beginning of the letters, the Name consists of 54 letters. As for the opening of the North: in it, the Divine Presence <note>original: *Shekhinah*</note> descended to confuse the languages: "And the Lord came down to see the city..." <note>Genesis 11:5</note>. And He descended to Sinai: "And the Lord came down upon Mount Sinai..." <note>Exodus 19:20</note>. And He shall descend to the New Jerusalem: "And His feet shall stand in that day upon the Mount of Olives" <note>Zechariah 14:4</note>. The letters of the Seal are like the appearance of a lightning flash and flames of light, and they are visible for millions of miles in height. Because they are engraved by His finger, they are bound in strings of pure, glowing sparks. *Ehyeh* is millions of miles in the height of the other, a measure of 79 flames, with which He writes three thousand. Why are the heavens like a ring <note>An alternate reading suggests "sealed"</note>? Because if we were to engage with them and use them, the world would be burned with fire, for many hosts of fiery heavens, massive arrays, and walls of fire desire to descend and burn the world. But once they see the wings of the heavens bound to the wings of the earth, and the wings of the earth to the wings of the heavens by the seal of *Ehyeh*, they stand back from their anger and conduct themselves with the attribute of mercy.

## **In the Book of Formation**
The height is sealed with the letter *Yod* for the six directions <note>original: *peshutot*; literally 'simple' letters or directions</note> by fixing them in His great name **Y-H-V** <note>The first three letters of the Tetragrammaton, used as a seal in the *Sefer Yetzirah*</note>. He sealed the six ends with them. He turned upward and sealed it with *Y-H*. The number 6 calls to 32 <note>An alternate reading suggests 1 to 32</note> which are not doubled, *H-V, V-H*, 15... He took *Y-H-V* as we will explain in the permutation of the letters of His name; He permuted them one with another. The Holy One, Blessed be He, made a likeness of a Throne in the heavens—not a literal throne, but only to inform all the angels.

It references the Sefer Yetzirah and identifies the angel Amual as the overseer of the Chulin firmament.</summary>

# The Book of the Great Raziel

The Wagon <note>The "Wagon" (Agalah) refers to the constellation Ursa Major, often called the Big Dipper</note> and there is that which follows the Pleiades. Some say that the Wagon is the constellation Ash <note>Arcturus or the Great Bear</note>, and Orion is the one who follows two stars of the Wagon; they were called "Orion" <note>Hebrew: Kesil</note>.
The five are called the "bands of Orion," and for the Pleiades there are seven stars. This is the beginning of the "dominion of the day" and the "dominion of the night." And during the weekday, for the dominion by day, for the dominion by day and by night, there are five. On the second day, the Teli <note>The "Teli" is a mythical celestial dragon or axis that was believed to support and rotate the heavenly spheres</note> rolls in the firmament. The Teli moves and turns with all the constellations, as if to say He "turns wise men backward," for the dominion of the constellations is nothing but the will of the King of Kings, the Holy One, Blessed be He, who created them. There is no end to the wisdom of the constellations; therefore, every man who has the "breath of the spirit of life in his nostrils" should understand in his heart that the Holy One, Blessed be He, knows what a day will bring and what will be. Behold, even a man wise in the constellations can understand all that will happen; how much more so He who created them <insert>and knows</insert> that He knows all thoughts even before they are formed. All the great luminaries He placed on high, except for the Wagon, which is placed in the North. The "harmers" <note>Spiritual entities or demons</note> who walk in the firmament, and the angels who fell from their holy place from heaven, go up to hear a word from "behind the Curtain" <note>The *Pargod*, the veil separating the Divine Presence from the lower realms</note>; they are weakened by a man’s staff, and immediately they return to their place. All the stars and all the constellations were created on the eve of the fourth day at their start, and one did not precede the other except by two-thirds of an hour. Therefore, all the actions of the Sun are done with partitions, and all the actions of the Moon are done with haste. The Sun and the Moon are the two great luminaries. This firmament is stretched out over us like the dome of a tent, and stretched like a cloth, as it is written: "He who stretches out the heavens like a curtain," and Isaiah said: "and spreads them out like a tent to dwell in." The firmament covers all creatures like a tent that covers all that is beneath it. The sphere of the luminaries, the stars, and the constellations [moves] from East to West and from West to East, leading from Aries to Taurus, and from Taurus to Gemini, and so on until the Fishes <note>Pisces</note>; it starts and finishes until it completes generation after generation. It raises and lowers the two great luminaries and the stars from South to North and from North to South. The Earth is spread over the waters and dwells within the waters of the depths like a ship in the heart of the sea. <insert>And the Ocean River</insert> surrounds it from all sides; this is called the Great Sea. There is no one who can cross it, or go out and swim in it. All other waters in the world emerge from it. Like that which is beyond the Ocean, there is "tohu and bohu" <note>Original: "unformed and void" (Genesis 1:2)</note>. Thus the ends of the firmament are joined, as it is written in the Book of Job: "Who laid its cornerstone?" and Isaiah said: "The Creator of the ends of the earth." This earth was created as a dwelling place. Towards the North, the earth is high, up to the dome, and the firmament is attached to it on every side. The Teli is in the middle of the firmament and rolls the constellations. The Sun is the Head and the Moon is the Tail. One who wishes to know the location of the Head of the Teli <note>The ascending lunar node</note> should add two years to the [date of] the creation of the world, and divide them by 19, [then] six years, and look at what remains in its cycle; see what corresponds to the year and what corresponds to the month for the position of the Head of the Teli and the Tail. Its movement is backward, yet it moves forward and sometimes backward. So too the stars; when they move forward, everyone born under them moves toward the good; [but if] backward and not forward from the Head of the Teli, [it is different]. It stands in a constellation for 18 months; until its Tail, there are seven constellations. When its head is humbled—and just as that which is opposite its head—as the constellation in which a sickness begins, and the sickness ends [after] two years.
Twelve constellations: some are male and some are female. We shall explain: T.A.K. <note>Aries, Leo, Sagittarius</note> are in the East—why are their names male? Because the Eastern side is "youthful" and is the beginning of the constellations, and everything that is an ascent at first is male. S.K.G. <note>Taurus, Virgo, Capricorn</note>—why are they female? Because the Southern side descends, and everything that is in descent is female, like a female who is "below." S.A.R. <note>Cancer, Scorpio, Pisces</note> in the North are leaning toward "tohu" and are placed low like females. Ch.M.R. <note>Gemini, Libra, Aquarius</note> are in the West, and they are male because the Western side [is where] the Divine Presence <note>The *Shekhinah*</note> dwells in the West, and the honor of the dwelling is only for males. T.A.K. are Fire, and their names are male because their appointment is over fire, and fire [rises] upward like a male who is fit for the "upper" position. S.K.G. are Earth, and their names are female because their appointment is over earth, and the world is "below" like a female who is fit for the "lower" position. Ch.M.R. are Air/Wind, and their names are male because their appointment is over wind, and the wind is in between, between the upper and the lower, and the deciding third is male. S.A.R. are Water, and their names are female because their appointment is over water, and water is "below," and the deciding third is female who is fit for the "lower" position. And these are they:

-> <insert>Fire, Air, Water, Earth</insert> <-

## Twelve

Constellations the Holy One, Blessed be He, created in the world and arranged in the firmament, they and their hosts. These are they: Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius, and Pisces. He ordered them to serve during the twelve months of the year, and their light <insert>shines forth</insert> by day and by night, each in its place. They are not visible to the eye [by day] because the light of the Sun's sphere obscures them. All of them are visible at night except for the constellation in which the Sun sits. Before the Sun, six are covered and six are revealed. This is the order of their rising: Aries. When Aries begins to sink, Libra begins to rise. If all of Aries has set, all of Libra has risen. Thus they all go out according to the number of their hosts; they all have names, and none enters the territory of his neighbor, nor does any change his custom. The one who serves at the time of sowing does not serve at the time of harvest, and the one who serves at the time of harvest does not serve at the time of sowing. This is the order of their service: half of Libra, Scorpio, and half of Sagittarius serve during the time of sowing for 61 days and its hosts. Half of Sagittarius, Capricorn, and half of Aquarius serve during the winter days for 61 days, <insert>even though</insert> there is no [scriptural] proof for the matter. Half of Aquarius, Pisces, and half of Aries serve during the time of the cold for 61 days. Half of Aries, Taurus, and half of Gemini serve during the time of harvest for 61 days. And so it says: "You shall bring the Omer <note>The first sheaf of the barley harvest</note> of the firstfruits of your harvest to the priest." When do you bring the Omer? In the month of Nisan. And so it says: "And the Jordan overflows all its banks all the time of harvest." Half of Gemini, Cancer, and half of Leo [serve] during the summer time for 61 days. Thus you find that from the time of sowing until the harvest, the Sun travels through seven constellations, from the middle of Libra until the middle of Aries, which are 180 parts. So too from the time of harvest until the time of sowing, seven constellations from the middle of [Aries] until the middle of Libra, which are 180 parts. Those that divide the constellation—the firmament is made like a dome for the constellations. Therefore they are called *Mazalot* <note>Constellations/Signs of the Zodiac</note>, from the language of *Azal* <note>meaning "to go" or "to flow"</note> because they move. Therefore, from of old, the Wagon was called in the South—

-> and the Wagon in the North, and the Holy One, Blessed be He, guides them in His wisdom. <-

<summary>This page details the Jewish mystical understanding of astronomy, explaining the movement of the zodiac signs (Mazalot) and the celestial dragon (Teli) through the firmament. It categorizes the twelve signs by gender and element (Fire, Air, Water, Earth) and links them to the agricultural seasons of the Land of Israel.</summary>

--- Page 47 ---
<meta>This page continues the astronomical and astrological discourse from the previous page, focusing on the specific mechanics of the Zodiac signs—specifically the relationship between the Pleiades and Orion—and the solar cycles through the "windows" of the firmament.</meta>

# The Book of the Great Raziel

->The Book of the Great Raziel<-

To its left and opposite it, they are gathered by day and by night—**Gemini** <note>Hebrew: Teumim</note> at the end of the seventh and **Scorpio** <note>Hebrew: Akrab</note> at the end of the fifth. From Gemini to **Sagittarius** <note>Hebrew: Keshet</note> between sunset and the twilight hours; Scorpio to **Cancer** <note>Hebrew: Sartan</note>; between the **Wagon** <note>The Big Dipper/Ursa Major</note> and Scorpio. The Wagon is to the North and Scorpio is to the South; the rising of the circle is toward the South and the setting of the circle is toward the North. In the North, the Wagon returns and serves the **Teli** <note>The "Teli" is the celestial dragon or axis upon which the heavens were thought to rotate</note>, and the Teli serves the constellations, and the constellations serve the sphere, and the sphere rotates within the sphere of **Aquarius** <note>Hebrew: Deli</note>, the Water-Bearer. The constellations are in Cancer, and the Teli is like a king, and the Wagon is like a leader, and all of them are in the wind that stands in the wind of heaven.
<margin>By His spirit the heavens are beauty; His hand has pierced the crooked serpent <note>Original: "nachash bariach." This is a quote from Job 26:13</note>, and this is the Teli. And the luminaries return and are gathered like kings and like stars.</margin>
The hosts and the constellations are arranged like a military camp, as it is said: "He who brings out their host by number" <note>Isaiah 40:26</note>. The calculation of the twelve constellations finds that the **Pleiades** <note>Hebrew: Kimah</note> and **Orion** <note>Hebrew: Kesil</note> are above them, for they are the seven. The constellations are found from the Pleiades and Orion because they are forty systems. "Can you bind the chains of the Pleiades or loose the cords of Orion?" <note>Job 38:31</note>. It does not say "will you bind" but "can you bind," to teach you that from those five and from the calculation of seven, you learn that the five systems are the Pleiades and the seven cords are Orion. Rather, the Holy One, Blessed be He, reversed the calculation and said to Job: "Can you bind the five delights <note>Hebrew: Ma'adanot</note> of the Pleiades, which are actually seven? And can you open the seven seats of Orion, which are actually five?" Together they make twelve. The Pleiades is the constellation of **Taurus** <note>Hebrew: Shor</note>, and from the tail of the Lamb <note>Aries</note> until the Pleiades, they are counted with Taurus. As for Orion, some say it is the Wagon, and some say it follows the Pleiades. Some say the Wagon is Ash <note>Arcturus or the Great Bear</note>, and some say the Wagon is Orion. Five stars are called the "cords of Orion" because two are considered the face of the Wagon and five are its seats and its rotations. For the Pleiades, there are seven stars, and they are the "delights of the Pleiades." When the Holy One, Blessed be He, brought the **Flood** upon the world, He took two stars from the Pleiades and the Flood occurred. When He wished to make it pass, He took two stars from Ash <note>the Great Bear</note> and stopped the openings. Because of this, Ash always follows the Pleiades to seek its children <note>This is a famous Talmudic legend from Tractate Berakhot 58b</note>. Some say it is a serpent, and this is Ash mourning for her children forever. Whenever one constellation begins to shine in the East, the one that is seventh from it begins to set in the West, even though they are not all visible. Among the constellations together, some of the rising ones are visible and some of the setting ones—which are the seventh—are not visible. For example: **Aries** and **Libra**. When Aries begins to rise, Libra begins to set. **Taurus** and **Scorpio**: when Taurus begins to rise, Scorpio begins to set. And so it is with **Gemini** and **Sagittarius**, **Cancer** and **Capricorn**, **Leo** and **Aquarius**, **Virgo** and **Pisces**. Always, six constellations rise from the North of the sea, which is the South side, and are visible in the inhabited world. They go toward the North side behind the height of the earth and are not visible to the eye, unlike the other constellations. Therefore, **Samuel the Sage** <note>Samuel of Nehardea, a 3rd-century Jewish scholar and astronomer</note> called them "the hidden ones of the bellows," using the language of "girding," like a key; meaning that the seven are called "delights" because they are bound from within the inhabited world and are not visible to the eyes of the inhabitants. The verse did not label all the constellations and stars this way. Ash is Orion <note>A variation in tradition</note>, and Orion is the constellation of Gemini. In the sea is the strength of Ash, the head of the constellation of Taurus, because they are more recognizable and known than all other stars and constellations. Because the Pleiades and Orion are always together, close to one another and following one after the other, and are so visible to the eye, the Holy One, Blessed be He, said to Job: "Can you bind the delights of the Pleiades or loose the seats of Orion?" This is to teach you to look at the firmament: when the Pleiades (which is Taurus) rises in the East, you will see the constellation of Scorpio setting in the West. And when Scorpio rises in the East, Taurus sets in the West. Orion is with Taurus. <insert>According to another version: with other constellations</insert>. These constellations are seated from West to East, from the North side behind the height of the earth; therefore they are called "seats," for they seat the "delights." And Taurus is called so because the Pleiades rises in the East; it is more Northerly than all the constellations. Therefore these seven were called "delights" and no others. And its rising begins with the Pleiades and completes. As for Scorpio, it is because the Pleiades rises from the North and Scorpio rises from the South. The rest of the constellations rise from the East of the Pleiades to the East of the West. Thus you have learned of the seven systems, and they are the "cords." Regarding this, it is said "cords," and they are considered systems with Orion. There are five cords anchored from West to East; when Orion is in the East, they are cords to the system, and Scorpio sets in the West at the end of five constellations that are on the North side. They become cords with Orion from West to East toward the South side, opposite Scorpio. Thus Gemini is found at the end of the seven in the East after the Pleiades, and the cords are counted from East to West on the North side opposite Gemini, which is Orion at the end of the seven. Thus Scorpio is found at the end of the five in the West... from Gemini until the beginning of Sagittarius is twelve constellations. Thus you have learned that the Pleiades and Orion are two degrees, no less and no more. In **Genesis Rabbah** <note>A classic collection of ancient Jewish homilies on the Book of Genesis</note>, the "delights of the Pleiades" refers to the ripening of fruits. It explains that the Pleiades serves during the rainy season and the world cools, killing the worms that destroy the fruits. Orion "pulls" between Sagittarius and the "knot"; this is the explanation that Orion serves during the summer days. Before the summer days, the fruit is at its stem, and Orion warms it and grows it, pulling the body from the arrow to the stem. These are but a few of His ways.

->Blessed is He and blessed is His name, Amen.<-

***

### And Now
I will enlighten you on the matters of the Sun. There are 365 windows, which are the degrees. For 183 days it ascends in the Eastern wind, and for 183 days it descends in the Western wind, according to the days of the solar year. It goes out in the East and enters in the West, for the Divine Presence <note>Hebrew: Shekhinah</note> is in the West. It enters and prostrates before the King,

->the King of Kings, the Holy One, Blessed be He, and says: "Master of the Universe, I have done as You commanded me."<-

***

### In the Season
of **Tishrei** <note>Autumn</note> and **Tevet** <note>Winter</note>, it circles in the Southern corner in the waters of the Ocean between the ends of heaven and the ends of the earth. It sets because the night is long and the way is very great until it reaches the window in the East through which it wishes to exit. And in the season of **Nisan** <note>Spring</note> and **Tammuz** <note>Summer</note>, it circles in the Northern corner in the waters of the Ocean between the southern ends and circles to the North. It goes to the North in the season of Tishrei and Tevet and circles to the North in the season of Nisan... there are "deaf ones" <note>likely referring to specific winds or angelic classes</note> in the Southern corner and "plowmen" in the Northern corner. And upon its circuits in the windows... the wind of the Sun returns. Three letters from the Divine Name are written upon its heart, and the angels lead it. Those who lead it by day are those who lead it by night. The Sun rises in a chariot and rides, adorned like a bridegroom, as it is said, "as a bridegroom [coming out of his chamber]" <note>Psalm 19:5</note>.

# The Book of the Great Raziel

->The Book of the Great Raziel<-

He makes **Arcturus**, **Orion**, and the **Pleiades** <note>Hebrew: *Ash, Kesil, and Kimah*</note>, through which He leads and establishes each one in its proper time. Through them, He changes the times and alternates the seasons, passing the **Arcturus** constellation along. He breaks **Orion** <note>The text uses "breaks" (shover) to describe the transition of power between celestial bodies</note>; He breaks those who pass through the days of heat. And so says the Master: "The creator of all breaks" <note>A play on words regarding "shover" (breaks) and "sepher" (scribe/book)</note>, a term of the weaver and the seal, like the one who makes a "breach" in the book of Isaiah. **Orion** breaks the passerby, and its interpretation is that the Master of the Universe, who creates everything, when He closes the constellation of Orion, He closes off those who come and go, for the winds strengthen during the rainy season and there is no way for the camp of the sea <note>meaning: maritime travel ceases</note>. In the "Book of Shabbat," which explains the Covenant of Samuel, it is said that this firmament is made like a dome, resembling a tent spread over the earth, as it is said: "He stretches them out like a tent to dwell in" <note>Isaiah 40:22</note>. Its edges are below and its interior is above in a dome, like a tent, and all the earth is beneath it. And there are those who say the **Oceanus** <note>The Great Ocean</note> surrounds it, and surrounding that is a land of "chaos and void" <note>Hebrew: *Tohu va-Vohu*</note>, as it is written in the book of Isaiah: "Have you not known? Have you not heard? The everlasting God, the Lord, the Creator of the ends of the earth" <note>Isaiah 40:28</note>. It is not inhabited. And in that land, some are attached to the firmament all around, as it is found in the books of the wise of the nations, for the firmament is to the earth from every side.

But in the books of the wise of Israel, it is said that in the North side, the firmament’s edges are not attached to the earth and it is not fixed, for "He stretches out the north over the empty place, and hangs the earth upon nothing" <note>Job 26:7</note>. From the South, its corner is closed like a sealed chamber, round, as it is written: "Who does great things past finding out... who makes Arcturus, Orion, and the Pleiades, and the chambers of the South" <note>Job 9:9</note>. From the East and the West, the firmament is founded upon the earth, as it is written in the words of Amos: "He who builds His upper chambers in the heavens, and has founded His vault upon the earth" <note>Amos 9:6</note>. The **Pleiades** and the **Chambers of the South** have their winds hidden within them during the rainy days and they serve one hundred and twenty-one [days?]. He said to Job: "Does the hawk fly by your wisdom, and spread its wings?" <note>Job 39:26</note>—this is so that the wing coming from the heat <note>possibly meaning the southern winds</note> is under its wings to sustain it against the sun's scorching; the same applies to other birds, but because the hawk has large wings and many feathers, its name was mentioned above the others. And so we learned in the tractate *Rosh Hashanah* that the **Pleiades** <note>Kimah</note> serves during the rainy days. 

The "crooked serpent" <note>Hebrew: *Nachash Bariach*</note> is the constellation of the Dragon, called in the language of the Sages the **Teli** <note>The *Teli* is the celestial axis or "curled dragon" upon which the ancient mystics believed the spheres of the heavens rotated</note>, because the twelve constellations are hung upon it—six on one side and six on the other. Internally, it forces the heavens from end to end; therefore it is called the "Bar-Serpent" <note>Bariach means both "crooked" and "a bar/bolt"</note>. At times it twists and puts its tail into its mouth like a "coiling serpent" <note>Hebrew: *Nachash Akalaton*</note>. The six internal constellations serve and lead the world at that time, and when they rise to serve, it returns and twists also from the other side so that it may bring the external constellations inside to serve the world, while the six middle ones now serve outside. At the hour when the "Bar" stretches the firmament from end to end, the firmament is spread over the whole world from edge to edge, except for a portion of the Northern corner which was not finished. This world is made and divided into three parts: one third water, one third desert, and one third inhabited. The earth, from the place of the East in the Tevet <note>Winter</note> season, which is South-Western, is high up to the firmament. This sphere is a circle around the edge of the **Oceanus** sea on every side. It is very high all around from the South-Eastern edge to the South-Western edge. This height is opened with many windows in the East and many windows in the West. 

From the place of the East in the Tishrei <note>Autumn</note> season, which is South-Eastern of the Tevet season, and further the Middle-Eastern and the center of the Tammuz <note>Summer</note> season. And in the middle, according to Enoch <note>The text likely refers to the astronomical calculations found in the Book of Enoch</note>, between the 91 windows in the Southern horn and the 91 in the Northern horn, there is one window in the middle, and its name is **Nogah** <note>Brightness/Venus</note>. Beside this window, on the Northern side of the five windows in the horn, there is a window called **Talumah** <note>Mystery</note>, and through it the light goes out, as it is said: "And the hidden thing [Talumah] He brings to light." It is in the South-Eastern horn of the Tevet season, and its name is called **Balwah**. The window in the Northern horn of the Tammuz season is called **Na'aman**. The Sun begins to shine from the window of **Na'aman** and goes round and round, returning backward and descending from North to South, window after window. When it has passed all 90 windows, there is one window called **Cheder** <note>Chamber</note> in which the storm wind dwells.

<margin>Season of</margin> Tishrei <note>Autumn</note>: The sun begins to shine from the window of **Nogah** and goes and circles to the South-Eastern horn, passing 91 windows until it reaches the window of **Balga**. The season of Tevet <note>Winter</note> begins from the window of **Balga** and goes and circles, returning backward and ascending from South to North, window after window, until it reaches the window of **Talumah**. It then goes and circles, ascending to the North-Eastern horn, window by window, until it reaches the window of **Na'aman** and the window of **Cheder**. This is the window where the count of 91 ends. And the paths start from the South-Eastern horn, from the window of **Balga** to the window of **Talumah**. That window named **Talumah**, from the count of 91, is the window that goes from **Talumah** to the window of **Cheder**. There is a window named **Nogah**; behold, their number is 183. Just as there are 183 windows in the East, there are 183 corresponding windows in the West. These are the three entrances. The sun, which is the "Chamah" <note>The solar orb</note>, shines every day from one window and sets and enters in another entrance in the West, corresponding to the window in the East. Sometimes it passes and ascends through the thickness of the entrance of a window to be seen in the inhabited world; this is the dawn, as it is said: "And when the dawn arose," and it says "for the dawn has arisen." And until it comes and goes and is drawn into the seat of the entrance, there is darkness, as it is said: "And it was, the sun went down and it was thick darkness." Its dwelling is always in the West, as it is said: "To Him who rides upon the clouds [Arabot]." There is one window in the middle of the firmament which is between the South-East of the Tevet season and the North-East window called **Mazarim** of the Tammuz season. Its name is **Mazarim**, as it is said: "Out of the chamber comes the storm, and cold from the scattering winds [Mazarim]." The sun does not go out or enter through it except once in the "Great Cycle" <note>The 28-year solar cycle</note> in which the sun was created. Its sphere is in a station and has a great desire. It has four faces for four sides:
1. The first side's power is hot and moist.
2. The second side is hot and dry.
3. The third side is cold and dry.
4. The fourth side is cold and moist.
These correspond to the four seasons <note>Hebrew: *Tekufot*</note> of the year.

<margin>In the season of</margin> Nissan <note>Spring</note>, the side of the sun that is hot and moist is turned to give life below upon the earth, and the time will be hot and moist for three months. In the season of Tammuz <note>Summer</note>, the side of the sun that is hot and dry is turned to give life below upon the earth, and the time will be hot and dry for three months. In the season of Tishrei <note>Autumn</note>, the side of the sun that is cold and moist is turned for three months. In the season of Tevet <note>Winter</note>, the side of the sun that is cold and dry is turned for three months. If the sun did not rotate through these four turnings, the world could not stand. And there are seven arrangements of the **Pleiades** <note>Kimah</note>, and five "bands" <note>Hebrew: *Mosheknot*</note>, two of **Orion** <note>Kesil</note>, and this book... two stars of the sphere, six connectors from the two [sides] to the inhabited world toward the South side, and five are opened outside the inhabited world toward the North side...

It also explains the role of the "Teli" (celestial dragon) as the axis for the Zodiac and defines the elemental qualities (hot, cold, moist, dry) of the four annual seasons.</summary>

# The Book of the Great Raziel

->The Book of the Great Raziel<-

The luminaries and the constellations move from East to West and from West to East. It is He <note>referring to God or the celestial mechanism</note> who turns the five planets <note>the visible planets: Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn</note> backward, and He who causes them to stop and stand in a different place in their path, moving neither forward nor backward, and He who leads them in a straight path. It <note>the Teli</note> is made of fire and water; its appearance is hidden and invisible to the eye, except through the tradition of the ancient scribes. Through them, we come to know the **Teli** <term>Teli: In medieval Jewish cosmology, this is the "Celestial Dragon" or the axis that holds the spheres together; it is the invisible pole around which the heavens turn</term>, its power, its dominion, and its kingdom—as well as the fear it inspires, its benefits, and its harms. We also learn to recognize the faces of those born under it, the timing of its passage from sign to sign, its habit of reversing its path, its height, and its depth. The twelve signs of the Zodiac are attached to it: six on the southern side and six on the northern side. Know that the **Sphere** <note>Hebrew: *Galgal*</note> that rotates the constellations, the stars, and the luminaries is a globe set within the thickness of the firmament. The constellations are fixed and joined to it forever; they never move from their established place. This sphere is a circle around the firmament in the South and the North, from East to West. The **Teli** is stretched within it from end to end like a bolt <note>referencing Job 26:13</note>, and the **Teli** is positioned in the center.

---
**The Sphere**
(A circular diagram showing the structure of the cosmos)
**The Dragon-Axis**
**Earth**
**Water**
---

The sphere turns in this manner, and the **Wagon** <note>Hebrew: *Agalah*, likely referring to the Big Dipper/Ursa Major</note> is rotated from the northern side toward the **Teli**. On the northern side of the southern constellations, it rotates with the **Teli**. By the power of the **Teli**, the leader of the edge of the **Wagon**—which is attached to the ring of the **Teli**—is rotated. Thus, the sphere of fixed constellations rotates in the sphere from East to West, and from the North from West to East, from world unto world. The seven planets have small spheres, and the Sphere of the Zodiac carries the seven spheres of the seven planets: **Saturn**, **Jupiter**, **Mars**, **Sun**, **Venus**, **Mercury**, and **Moon** <note>original Hebrew: *Shabbetai, Tzedek, Ma'adim, Hammah, Nogah, Kokhav, Levanah*</note>, along with the stars and the twelve signs of the Zodiac, from East to West and West to East. Just as the wheel of a wagon drives the **Teli**, so is the **Teli** driven; the sphere of the constellations and the seven planets rotate through it. All of this is by the spirit of the Holy One, Blessed be He, and by His power, as it is said: "By His spirit the heavens were made fair; His hand pierced the fleeing serpent" <note>Job 26:13</note>—this "serpent" is the **Teli**. It is also written: "By the word of the Lord the heavens were made, and by the breath of His mouth all their host." Our Sages of blessed memory <note>Hebrew: *Hazal*</note> said that He called the names of the stars and counts them, as it is written: "He counts the number of the stars..." <note>Psalm 147:4</note>. This demonstrates the great power of our Lord to teach you the magnitude of the arrangement of the handiwork of the stars, the constellations, the **Teli**, the Sphere, and the **Wagon**. His greatness is seen in the rest of the world's creation, for there is no wisdom as broad, deep, and wondrous in the world as this wisdom. The "heart" of the **Teli** is the strength of the heavens, calculating the mysteries of the universe and the sphere of the constellations.

Regarding the **Teli**, there is no other wisdom <note>Hebrew letters *A.Kh. A.V.Y.H.*—likely a mystical cipher</note>. Its service is the investigation of the world and the sphere of the constellations. If the Sun <note>*Hammah*</note> is in a certain sign, then the cycle of them all is hot and dry like fire. The Prince/Angel is **Michael**. In him are **Taurus** (Earth), **Libra** (Air). His service is **Venus** <note>*Nogah*</note>. If they are in a certain position, the cycle is confused and moist; the wind brings down water from Venus and rain. The Prince is **Anael**. **Gemini** (Air), **Virgo** (Earth). Their service is **Mercury** <note>*Kokhav*</note>. If they are in these signs, it will be partly moist and partly dry, based on the elements of Earth and Air. The Prince is **Raphael**. **Cancer** (Water). Its service is the **Moon** <note>*Levanah*</note>. If the cycle or the sign is in it, all will be moist. The Prince is **Gabriel**. **Sagittarius** (Fire), **Aquarius** (Air). Their service is **Saturn** <note>*Shabbetai*</note>. If the sign or cycle is in them, half will be dry and calm, based on a foundation of rest, mixed with moist wind and flowing water. The Prince is **Cassiel**. **Capricorn** (Earth), **Pisces** (Water). Their service is **Jupiter** <note>*Tzedek*</note>. In this sign and cycle, half are dry and calm, and half flow with water. The Prince is **Zadkiel**. Also, **Aries** (Fire), **Scorpio** (Water). Their service is **Mars** <note>*Ma'adim*</note>. In this sign and cycle, they are dry and calm, and half flow with water. The Prince is **Samael**.

<margin>Almanac</margin> **Signs of the Sun:** If at sunrise the sun is clear and pure in its heat, it indicates the fiery nature of the air and its purity. If at sunrise the sun is clear and pure but with a flaming, glowing red fire, it indicates many great winds. If you see before the sun rises that there are green and reddish clouds, they indicate rain and showers. If sparks of the sun's flame flash before it rises, they indicate the cold of autumn and winter. If the sun shoots out from within the clouds as if from water, and when it shines it looks like a flame, they indicate winter and autumn. If the sparks of the sun shine with the sun's radiance while it is cloudy, they indicate winter, autumn, and rain. If at sunrise in the West <note>likely referring to the light reflecting or the sun's position at a specific time</note> the clouds are cleared from around it, it indicates the purity of the firmament and the air. If the sun sets and returns from the North wind, and the clouds are reddish on the North side and black on the South side, they indicate purity but also herald winter and autumn. If they rise with the wind at their back, they testify to rain showers. If the sun's rays are received toward evening and they look like red, clear, radiant clouds like the sun, those clouds indicate strong but brief winds. If those clouds are with winds on either side of the sun, it indicates much winter and autumn. If the firmament appears very black, like a cold darkness, they say that dryness will occur and the rivers, springs, and wells will dry up. If at sunrise there is a surrounding circle like a halo, it indicates winter and autumn. When the sun shines for about an hour and then weakens, scattering its "blood" and turning its golden sparks to green within the clouds, it indicates rain mixed with hail. When at its setting it is entirely <unclear>hidden/consumed</unclear>, it indicates rain together with wind. The wind begins first with the light of the world, and if the sun burns greatly...

<meta>This page continues the cosmological exploration of the celestial bodies, specifically detailing the physical and spiritual properties of the Sun and Moon, the angelic hosts that guide them, and the relationship between the planets and the Zodiac signs, concluding with the structure of the "Teli" or celestial axis.</meta>

# The Book of the Great Raziel

-> He shall minister in glory like a bridegroom coming out of his chamber <- <note>original: "יכהן פאר כחתן יוצא מחופתו" — a poetic allusion to Psalm 19:6 describing the Sun.</note>

<margin>During the days of the Sun <note>Summer</note></margin>
Its horns and its face look toward the earth, made of fire; its horns and its face look upward, made of hail. If not for the hail that covers it, the world would be consumed by fire, as it is said: "And nothing is hidden from its heat" <note>Psalm 19:7</note>. In the winter, it turns its face from above to below; if not for the fire that warms the face of the hail, the world would not be able to stand before the cold, as it is said: "Who can stand before His cold?" <note>Psalm 147:17</note>. And it is said: "In them He has set a tent for the sun" <note>Psalm 19:5</note>, and so on. There are 365 cords <note>Some versions say "windows"</note> for it, corresponding to the number of days in a solar year; therefore, the final letters of its names <note>In Hebrew</note> equal 365: *Shemesh* <note>Sun</note> and *Hammah* <note>Solar Heat</note>. Every day, the Sun and Moon cover their eyes from the Light Above and cast arrows into it. When it <note>the Sun</note> emerges, it is a giant, yet it is weakened by the sins of the creatures. It is placed in a sheath, and a pool of water from above, which is atop the firmament, passes beneath it to cool its fury with water; nevertheless, "nothing is hidden from its heat." Thirteen paths are written upon its heart; therefore, from the word "In the beginning" <note>Genesis 1:1</note> until "And they were finished" <note>Genesis 2:1</note>, the word "Light" <note>Or</note> appears thirteen times. Five angels lead them by day; therefore, there are five descriptions of the Sun: 1. It is like a bridegroom coming out; 2. It has strength; 3. Its rising; 4. Its circuit; 5. And nothing is hidden from its heat. Similarly, there are five mentions of "Light" in the first portion of Genesis. Thirteen angels lead them by night. Thus, there are 400 <note>This number likely refers to a total angelic calculation</note> angels between day and night. Correspondingly, there are eight mentions of "Light" for the day; therefore, the prayer "God, Master of all works" <note>El Adon, a famous Sabbath hymn</note> has eight words in its first phrase: 1. Great, 2. And good, 3. Fills, 4. The world, 5. Knowledge, 6. And understanding, 7. Surround, 8. Him. This pattern continues for each section, except for the alphabetical acrostic. In the prayer "God, Master," the entire alphabet corresponds to the 22 letters of the Torah. Therefore, "for the Sun He set a tent in them" is followed by "The Torah of the Lord is perfect" <note>Psalm 19:8</note>, showing the connection between day and night. The Sun travels through the four directions, which are two thousand <note>units</note>, corresponding to the Torah which preceded the world by two thousand years. In the morning, the Sun immerses in a river of fire <note>the *Rigyon*</note>, adding to its light as it goes and shines until the day is established. People prostrate to it, and it accepts the authority of flesh and blood <note>i.e., it is forced to serve humans despite their idolatry</note>. Then the Sun enters the firmament and immerses itself to purify itself and gain strength each time. **Galgaliel** is the prince appointed over the sphere <note>Galgal</note> of the Sun, and with him are 96 great and honored angels who race the sphere in the firmament 365,000 leagues every single day. The Sun shines even in filthy places and is not harmed by them; it immerses in the light from above, which no eye can see, as it is written: "For man shall not see Me and live" <note>Exodus 33:20</note>. So too stands the Moon, because the Throne <note>of Glory</note> is round behind it, shaped like a half-moon. Around the Divine Presence <note>Shekhinah</note> are clouds and water, as it is said: "Cloud and thick darkness are round about Him" <note>Psalm 97:2</note>. When the Moon turns its face—its neck downward and its face upward—there is rain. This is what the Sages said: "Whoever blesses the Moon <note>the monthly sanctification prayer</note> is as if he receives the face of the Divine Presence." They dance toward it like the *Chayot* <note>holy living creatures from Ezekiel's vision</note> running and returning, leaping beneath the Throne. **Ofaniel** is the prince appointed over the sphere of the Moon, and with him are 88,000 angels who race the sphere of the Moon 364,000 leagues every night. As long as the cycle stands, the Moon is in the East... <note>The text describes calculations for the 15th and 16th of the month</note>. The dwelling of the Moon is between cloud and thick darkness, shaped like two overturned bowls, one inside the other. At the birth of the New Moon, these two clouds turn their faces to the west wind, and the Moon emerges from between them like a shofar <note>a crescent</note>. On the first night, one measure; on the second night, a second measure; and so on until the middle of the month when it is fully revealed. Then these two clouds turn their faces toward the east wind, and the edge of the moon that emerged first begins to enter and is covered between them. On the first night, one measure; on the second, a second measure; until the end when it is entirely covered. How do we know it corresponds to the clouds? As it is said: "When I made the cloud its garment, and thick darkness its swaddling band" <note>Job 38:9</note>. How do we know it is entirely covered? As it is said: "Blow the shofar at the new moon, at the concealed time <note>Keseh</note> for our feast day" <note>Psalm 81:4</note>—*Keseh* refers to the day it is entirely covered. For one second of God's day, the Sun and Moon come together equally at the start of the night of the sixth day <note>Friday</note>, at the hour of Saturn, the time they were created. The flame of the Moon is secondary to the Sun; by day, 40 degrees pass through it and extinguish its light.

<margin>Rabbi Nehorai</margin>
Said: It is a royal decree—when Israel sins and they do not intercalate <note>adjust</note> the year properly, the flame of the Sun reaches the Moon at night, 40 degrees pass through it and extinguish it. But when Israel does His will and intercalates the year properly, the Holy One, Blessed be He, covers the Sun and sends His wrath upon the nations of the world, as it is said: "Thus says the Lord, learn not the way of the nations..." <note>Jeremiah 10:2</note>. The light of the Moon is sometimes revealed, but the light of the Sun is always revealed to all nations. Even though the Sun and Moon have fixed paths, the paths of the Moon are not like the paths of the Sun. The Sun's paths are fixed, but the Moon's are not; rather, they are internal, as it travels through the Zodiac signs sometimes quickly and sometimes slowly. How so? Sometimes it moves northward, making the path long; sometimes it moves higher, making the path short. Although there is no absolute proof for this, there is a hint: "He made the moon for seasons; the sun knows its coming" <note>Psalm 104:19</note>. And such is the order of their movement; the house of one is not like the house of the other:
The house of **Saturn** <note>Shabbetai</note> is Capricorn and Aquarius.
**Jupiter** <note>Tzedek</note> is Sagittarius and Pisces.
**Mars** <note>Ma'adim</note> is Aries and Scorpio.
**Venus** <note>Nogah</note> is Taurus and Libra.
**Mercury** <note>Kochav</note> is Gemini and Virgo.
The **Sun** <note>Hammah</note> <note>is Leo, though not explicitly named here</note> and the **Moon** <note>Levanah</note> is Cancer.
**Rehatiel** is the prince appointed over the constellations <term>Mazalot: The twelve signs of the Zodiac</term>, and with him are 72 great and honored angels. He causes the constellations to run in their courses from East to West and West to East. This is the path of ten <note>perhaps ten spheres</note> that the Holy One, Blessed be He, set for the stars and constellations to go at night from East to West and West to East. **Kochaviel** is the prince appointed over them, and with him are 365,000 myriads of angels who are great and honored, who race the stars from city to city and province to province in the firmament of the heavens. Everything is suspended from the **Teli** <term>Teli: The celestial dragon or cosmic axis</term> that the Holy One, Blessed be He, created. This firmament above us was smoothed; He created the Teli from head, fire, and water in the likeness of a great dragon, like a "slanting serpent" <note>Isaiah 27:1</note>. He gave it a head and a tail and placed it in the thickness of the firmament... and stretched it from end to end like a slanting serpent. Its center point is in the middle of its length, and its half-tilt is in a great ring. All the stars and constellations are attached to it—all the stars, the two luminaries, and the twelve signs of the Zodiac. He <note>the Teli</note> was appointed king over them all to lead them for both good and evil. He dims the light of the two luminaries and the stars; he is the one who leads and brings the path of the luminaries.

# The Book of the Great Raziel

...or if they roll in mud and mire and desire to become even filthier; and also when they seek to graze and are set free from their pasture ▢ from beneath the earth to the edges of their mouths; ▢ and also if they are found with their heads facing the north; and also if dogs dig in the earth, and likewise if a wolf ▢ approaches the goats or nears the birthing houses ▢ and also if oxen dip their lips into the reflection of the water in springs, though not out of a desire to drink; and also if geese go about calling out loudly for their fodder; and if spiderwebs fall of their own accord without a gust of wind—all this portends a winter of rain for many days. 

When the Moon is waning, it is called the "lesser light," and at that time, the stars do not rule in the firmament, except for ninety-eight stars, which is the count of the stars when the Moon complained against her Creator. ▢ Then the stars were made with a "venom" <note>original: "ארס" — likely referring to a potent celestial essence or influence</note> that strikes the resin tree and brings forth sparks. Therefore, the stars emerge ▢ with the Moon. 

From the beginning of the world, ▢ He refined the letters and the spheres of the heavens, their calculations, their tallies, and their numbers. He calculated the sum of the stars and constellations, the cycles of the seasons, and the rotations of the stars, of the *Tali* <note>term: Tali — the "Celestial Dragon" or axis that was believed to hold and move the heavenly spheres</note>, and of the constellations that were destined to be created. He laid out the entire calculation before Him in order to see the generations destined to be created from beginning to end, as it is written: "Who has wrought and done it, calling the generations from the beginning" <note>Isaiah 41:4</note>, even before the creation of the world ▢ so as to understand the deeds of every person ▢—their righteousness and their wickedness—and to decree upon each one according to the deeds they would do in the future, whether good or evil. He assigned the stars and constellations over them ▢ and assigned to each person their specific star and constellation that would be known at the time of their "sowing" <note>conception</note> and at the hour of their destiny, whether for good or for evil. 

All these things God made ▢ in His wisdom and His understanding even before ▢ the world was expanded, as it is said: "The Lord by wisdom founded the earth" <note>Proverbs 3:19</note>. Even though He assigned the star and constellation of every person ▢ before the world was created based on the deeds a person was destined ▢ to do, the Holy One, Blessed be He, did not give the stars and constellations permission to do harm or good at first until He saw that man ▢ as a creature was not stable without the "Evil Inclination," for it is written: "For the inclination of man's heart ▢ is evil from his youth" <note>Genesis 8:21</note>. Because of this, God is merciful and gracious, and He relents ▢ from evil; He prepared and established Repentance from the very start of creation, as it is written: "Before the mountains were brought forth..." <note>Psalm 90:2</note> until it says "You turn man back to dust" <note>Psalm 90:3</note>. This teaches that if one sins and returns in repentance, the Lord God will relent. This is why "there is no fixed destiny" <note>original: "אין מזל קבוע" — a variation on the Talmudic "Ein Mazal L'Israel," suggesting prayer and merit can change one's astrological fate</note>, for everything depends on what the person is at that moment, as it is written regarding Ishmael: ▢ "where he is" <note>Genesis 21:17 — midrashically interpreted to mean God judges a person based on their current state, not future sins</note>. 

Through the constellations of the luminaries, all the seasons are seen. Therefore, we say in the evening prayer: "He changes the times ▢ and alternates the seasons," but this is not said in the morning. When the rooster hastens to call in the night, the time will change and tomorrow there will be clouds, ▢ for the cloud enters its beak. Sometimes the Moon is in the night and sometimes it does not shine; this is the meaning of "He changes the times and alternates the seasons." With the appearance of three stars, it is night; ▢ this is what divides day ▢ and night. However, in the morning, even if ten important stars are seen, it is still considered day, as it is written: "He makes the dawn and the darkness... the Lord of Hosts is His name." 

The letter *Lamed* <note>ל</note> corresponds to the five luminaries. Just as the *Lamed* is higher than all other letters, so is the Holy One, Blessed be He, above all His creatures. He watches over everything from the beginning of the creation of the world with the utterance "Let there be light." This "Light" is the *Nehar Dinur* <note>term: Nehar Dinur — the "River of Fire" mentioned in Daniel 7:10, said to flow from beneath the Throne of Glory</note>, from which all the luminaries proceed. As we say in the tradition: Were it not for the "tail" of the Pleiades <note>Kimah</note> being in the River of Fire, no creature could endure the cold. Therefore, the Sun, Moon, and stars appear red. When the Sun travels by day, it travels through the River of Fire. In the place ▢ where the Sun travels by day, the River of Fire flows, and that path is called "Day," referred to as the "time of the day." In the place where the Sun travels by night, everything was water ▢ and it was like a *mulops* <note>unclear: possibly a corrupted Greek term for a mask or a dark passage</note> through which it travels ▢ and that place is called "Night." This is the meaning of "And God divided the light"—the River of Fire—"from the darkness"—the darkness of the waters and the thick clouds. This is why "God called the light Day"—meaning the River of Fire where the luminaries' regular motion is suspended—for if ▢ you do not say so, when would it be "and it was evening and it was morning"? Rather, the River of Fire moves just as the Sun does now; that is, the "light of day" is the River of Fire. ▢ And "darkness He called Night" refers to the darkness of the waters which are the path of the Sun. At dawn, the light of the River of Fire persists until midday, for the River of Fire rises in the East and West. 

On the fourth day, ▢ He brought forth from the River of Fire—like at midday when the River of Fire rises—and the Moon and stars ascended. ▢ Regarding the measure of the heavens according to their paths and their values, the Sages measured 340 <note>or 360</note> degrees, and twelve constellations are bound within it. Their names are: **Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius, Pisces.** Every constellation ▢ travels 30 degrees. Each degree is divided into *gastriot*, and every *gastria* into 60 *barkunot*, and every *barkun* into 60 *ulot*, and every *ulot* into 60 *orot*, and every *orot* into 60 *titot*, and every *titot* into 60 *rufusot*, and every *rufusot* into 60 *charifot*—as quick as the blink of an eye. Take this calculation in your hand until the eleventh and twelfth degrees, which are matters of precise calculation. 

The planets that descend and govern the world are named: **Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, the Sun, Venus, Mercury, and the Moon.** Each one has a portion in every one of these stars. Regarding the *Tali* <note>The Celestial Dragon</note> and the names of the constellations: the constellations and the *Tali* revolve and travel in the heavens as they do now. For in the *Tali*, twelve constellations are set; seven are the "bands" of the Pleiades and five "draw" the constellations. The *Tali* and the constellations revolve in the heavens and the earth. Seven and eight, they are twelve around the sphere, tied from within ▢ and open from without. Seven are from the right to the north, and five are from the north to the right. Corresponding to them, they are gathered ▢ six by day and six by night. The "Oamsim" <note>unclear</note> are at the end of the seven, and Scorpio is at the end of the five. From Gemini to Sagittarius, and from Scorpio to Cancer likewise. The "Wagon" <note>Ursa Major</note> is gathered toward Scorpio; the Wagon is in the north and Scorpio is in the south. ▢ It rises in a circle toward the south ▢ and sets leaning toward the north. In the north, the Wagon rises and turns the *Tali*, and the *Tali* turns the constellations, and the constellations turn the sphere, and the sphere returns in the secret sphere. The constellations are like passengers and the *Tali* is like the driver or leader, head to head. At the New Year, the *Tali* is at the end of the first six; it travels straight, and the tail travels in reverse. So it was from the beginning of the world: the River of Fire surrounded everything, and eighty days later, on the fourth day, a circuit was formed from the River of Fire ▢ and He created the Sun to shine by day upon the earth, and the River of Fire was placed near the constellations to block the cold of the Pleiades and the snow of the Moon and such things.

It provides a highly technical breakdown of celestial measurements and explains the "River of Fire" (Nehar Dinur) as the source of heat and light that balances the cosmic cold.</summary>

# The Book of the Great Raziel

...and He sends sparks of radiance within it like fire, and it is pursued by all of them. It tells of rain together with the wind. And if there is a pure brightness, it tells that the North wind will sit, and the essence of the heavens will be for purity; for the North wind purifies the light by the decree of the Creator—may His Name be blessed—because it blows upon the earth from a place higher and more exalted than all high and exalted places. Therefore, it purifies the thickness of the air, its heat, and its darkness, and the Sun shall shine without the darkness of cloud or fog, like the glow of light. 

If you see the Moon on the first day, and the upper horn <note>The "horns" of the moon refer to the tips of the crescent.</note> is hidden or concealed, it tells that there is a North wind. And if the lower horn is hidden, it tells that the Star-wind <note>original: "רוח הכוכב" — likely referring to a specific planetary wind or Mercury's influence</note> shall blow. And if the Moon is seen on the tenth day of its cycle, it tells of the nature of the air's radiance. And if in the days of the Moon’s smallness <note>The waning phase or the very early crescent.</note> its fog and clouds are very clear and pure, they tell that the winds blowing on that day will change. And if clouds are seen with the Moon, they tell of winter and the rainy season. 

And if the Moon is seen on the fourth day of its birth <note>The "birth" of the moon is the New Moon.</note> with its horns upright until the blowing of the wind from the western directions, they tell of a passing winter. And if you see that the Moon has something like cracks, from that place where the cracks appear, it tells that the wind will return from there. When the Moon is full and complete and "salted" <note>original: "ממולח" — likely a metaphor for a shimmering or speckled appearance</note> until the fourth day, and from the fourth day until the seventh, and from its "salting" until its midpoint, and from its midpoint until its fullness—it tells that when it is close to the Sun, the wind will be either in the fourth sign of the zodiac that goes with the Sun, or there will be a change in the winds for seven days; for thus they were clarified by the signs of the Moon. 

The Moon is worthy to be observed and understood, and to contemplate the signs: of rain three days before its birth, or three days after its birth, until its fullness is complete. And at the half-moon—being half the measure of its fullness—at all these appointed times the Moon is worthy for seeing the signs of rain, whether three days before or after. If the Moon is thin, clear, and pure, and nothing of darkness is seen in it, it tells of the nature and straightness of the air. But if it appears slightly, and around it is seen a circle of clouds and fog—this is the "dwelling of the Moon" <note>An atmospheric halo</note> which covers the Moon within it for the whole month—and it appears to every wind as if encompassing and moving the clouds upon it, it tells that a wind will blow from that side from which those clouds are moved. 

If the Moon appears saffron-colored <note>original: "מכורכם" — a deep orange or yellow</note> and thick, its name tells of winter and rain. And if around the Moon there is something like one circle of purity and understanding, with a little [unclear] <note>original: "עות יתץ"</note> it tells of the nature of the air and its purity. And if there are two or three [circles], they tell of winter, rain, and wind. If they are saffron-colored like <note>original: "כרונג" — likely a transliteration of "orange"</note> and they burst out from one side, clouds will come from that side. And if they are hidden <insert>another version: few</insert> and thick, or greenish and saffron, or black, and they all appear as if broken—the larger they are, so too will be the storm of the air and much change. 

If the "furnace" of the Sun <note>term: "כורות השמש" — the solar disk or its intense heat</note> creates around it something like a circle, or if it breaks quickly, it tells of the nature of the purity of the firmament. Know that on that day there will be rain. And if the Sun rises from within clouds, and in its rising they are like a path of fire, it will be winter and the rainy season, and the strength of the Sun will be reddish. The Moon tells of the wind. If in the morning the Sun appears great and large, it will be winter and rain. And if its spark is seen before its rising, it tells of rain. And if at its rising there are reddish clouds before it, it tells of rain that will come toward the South. 

And if the [birds] search themselves much, like those searching for eggs, and shake themselves much; and also the crows, if they are seen in abundance and cry out much; and also the flies, if they gather in abundance and bite much or chirp with their voice—it tells of rain. And know that if a *Tekufah* <note>term: Tekufah — one of the four seasons marked by the solstice or equinox</note> falls at the beginning of all seasons, there will be heat, and in the middle of its half, it will be average. 
**Venus** (Nogah) brings many rains; **Mercury** (Kokhav) is entirely hot; the **Moon** (Levanah) is entirely cold; **Saturn** (Shabbetai) in summer and winter is cold and brings much rain; **Jupiter** (Tzedek) is entirely cold, snow, and rain; **Mars** (Ma'adim) is heat, and rains will fall, and it will be entirely without cold.

->**The Arrangement of the Motion of the Ruling Constellations at the Start**<-

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| **Night 1:** Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon; then Saturn, Jupiter, Mars. | **Day 1:** Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon; Saturn, Jupiter, Mars; then as above. | **Night 3:** Venus, Mercury, Moon; Saturn, Jupiter, Mars; Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon. |
| **Day 2:** Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun; then Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon; Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun. | **Night 4:** Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun; then Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon. | **Night 5:** Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon; Saturn, Jupiter, Mars; Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon. |
| **Day 5:** Jupiter, Mars; Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon; Saturn, Jupiter, Mars; Sun, Venus. | **Night 6:** Saturn, Jupiter, Mars; Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon; Saturn, Jupiter, Mars. | **Day 6:** Venus, Mercury, Moon; Saturn, Jupiter, Mars; Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon; Saturn, Jupiter. |
| **Night 7:** Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon; Saturn, Jupiter, Mars; Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon. | **Day 7:** Saturn, Jupiter, Mars; Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon; Saturn, Jupiter, Mars; Sun, Venus. | And the order of the nights is found to be: **Moon, Mercury, Venus, Sun, Mars, Jupiter, Saturn.** |
