<margin>And I will further instruct you</margin> to understand if there will be winter or rain from the signs of the Moon on the third day of the New Moon from its birth: if the horns of the Moon are like charcoal, dark and obscured, this sign tells of showers of rain. Likewise, if the horns of the Moon are like this on the fourth day, it tells of rain. And if a halo is seen around the Moon, reddish like fire, this sign tells of winter and great rain. And in the days of its fullness, if anything like blackness is seen upon the Moon, this sign tells of winter and great rain. And in the days of its fullness, [it tells of] showers. And if there is around all its sides something like the color of a circle, or two or three circles, this tells of winter and great rain.

<margin>And further</margin> we shall learn to understand and know rain before it happens: if there will be winter and rain from the signs of the Sun. If the Sun rises reddish, it tells of showers of rain. And when it is ready to set and come into the West, if on the "left hinges" <note>The northern side of the sunset point.</note> there are black clouds close to it, it is a certainty that immediately there will be rain and showers. And when the Sun rises, if a cloud stands with the Sun upon the glow of its flaming sparks, this sign tells of rain and showers; and there is much more like this in the **Book of Horoscopes** <note>original: "ספר המולות" — Sefer HaMolot, a likely reference to an astrological source text</note>.

<margin>And further wisdom</margin> is placed there regarding winter and rain for many days: if the pigs or the goats have mated excessively with the females, and at the time they finish and cease the act, they make themselves [unclear] <note>original: "עור גרצ'ם" — possibly a phonetic corruption for "shaking their skin" or "rolling"</note> to act, or if they soil themselves in places of water or in the mud...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of celestial mechanics and meteorological signs, moving from the movement of the moon and winds to the physical structure of the firmament, the origin of the sun's heat, and the architectural "beams" that hold the heavenly waters.</meta>

# The Book of the Great Raziel

ו א 6

...like glass; it was moist, then they congealed and He spanned them, as it is written: *My right hand spanned the heavens* <note>original: "ימינו טפחה שמים" — Isaiah 48:13. The author interprets "spanned" (from the word for 'palm' or 'handbreadth') as a physical act of shaping and solidifying.</note>. When the firmament dried, it became the sky <note>original: "שחקים" — *shachakim*, often translated as 'clouds' or 'heights,' here referring to the ethereal layers of the sky.</note>. That is to say, when the firmament was moist, it was as strong as a cast mirror. This occurred when it dried: *He alone stretches out the heavens and treads on the waves of the sea* <note>Job 9:8</note>. This happened when it dried afterward and was stretched out *like a tent to dwell in* <note>Isaiah 40:22</note> for the joy of the divine flow. Everything is governed by the Spirit, as it is written: *By His Spirit the heavens are made fair* <note>Job 26:13</note>. From the firmament itself, He brought forth its strength: beams that protrude. Thus, the heavens are full of water, and the space between one beam and another is called an "upper chamber." Some of the waters are rested within them, and every single constellation has its own upper chamber above it. This is the meaning of: *Who lays the beams of His upper chambers in the waters* <note>Psalm 104:3</note>. 

You must conclude that the beams are part of the heavens themselves and are joined to them. For if the beams were simply placed upon the heavens, would it not contradict what is written: *My right hand spanned the heavens*? It is also written: *He made narrowed rests for the house round about, so that the beams should not be fastened into the walls of the house* <note>1 Kings 6:6</note>, meaning He did not pierce the wall. The first protrusion and the second one above the first are called "recesses" <note>original: "מגרעת" — *migra'at*, an architectural term for an offset or ledge in a wall.</note>, because the wall is "diminished" as its thickness is reduced to create a protruding ledge. However, the one at the top of the wall is called a "join" <note>original: "משפחת" — *mishpachat*. While usually meaning 'family,' the context here implies a structural border or joining point.</note>, because at the top of the wall there was a boundary place where the beam rested. But in a "recess," the entire wall decreased in thickness; therefore, at the bottom it was five cubits wide, above it six, and above that seven. Thus it is written: *The lowest story was five cubits wide, the middle was six cubits wide, and the third was seven cubits wide* <note>1 Kings 6:6</note>. But at the very top, there was only a place designated for the Law, and these are called joins <note>original: "משפחות" — *mishpachot*</note>. It is written: *All these were of costly stones... sawed with saws, within and without, even from the foundation unto the coping* <note>1 Kings 7:9</note>, meaning from the base to the handbreadth-ledges <note>original: "טפחות" — *tefachot*, here interpreted as the architectural cornices or tops of the walls.</note>. Since regarding the heavens it is written *My right hand spanned [tefacha] the heavens*, you must explain that the beams protrude upward into the heavens, and the engraving is behind the heavens. Those protrusions are like those in a wall; this is the meaning of *Who lays the beams of His upper chambers in the waters*. 

You can also understand that in the place where the heavens are joined, the Earth likewise has a boundary place to receive the heavens. The heavens have a "restraint" <note>original: "בלימה" — *belimah*, a reference to Job 26:7, "He hangs the earth upon nothing [belimah]."</note> that enters into the boundary. It is like a silver cup made with a place to receive the cover. This is what is written: *My hand laid the foundation of the earth, and My right hand spanned the heavens* <note>Isaiah 48:13</note>; they are attached to one another. Above the beams, there is something like a sky, made in the form of a dome, as it is written: *In them He has set a tent for the sun* <note>Psalm 19:4</note>. Above that is the River of Fire <note>original: "נהר דינור" — *Nehar Dinur*, a mystical river of fire mentioned in Daniel 7:10.</note>, like two shells. From there, a little of the River of Fire descends to each and every constellation to make the constellation boil with heat. This is what is written: *A river of fire issued and came forth from before Him; thousand thousands ministered unto Him* <note>Daniel 7:10</note>. Like the path of the River of Fire, the angels who serve Him lead it to every constellation. *And ten thousand times ten thousand stood before Him*—they stand before the River of Fire to guide it into every single aperture. But the first ones find the waters to be flowing, this way and that; these are the "thousand thousands." This is the meaning of: *And God set them in the firmament of the heaven*. Between those protruding beams, let there be "lights" <note>original: "מאורות" — *me'orot*</note>. It does not say "luminaries" <note>original: "אורות" — *orot*</note>, but "lights," meaning the *place* of the lights. For the sun is placed like glass, and the radiance goes out beyond the glass. Regarding that glass, He said: *Let there be lights in the heavens*. They are full of splendor and emit a glow, just as glass is full of splendor and emits it outward. 

This is what we say in the Sabbath liturgy: "He who opens every day the doors of the gates of the East." For the thickness of the firmament from the place where the sun enters in the morning is a journey of five hundred years. There are doors in the thickness of the firmament, and the firmament has windows in the place of the world's "threshold" <note>original: "צפי" — possibly meaning the lookout or surface.</note> that overlooks the Earth. When the sun enters the opening of the doors, that is the "pillar of dawn" <note>term: "עמוד השחר" — *amud ha-shachar*, the first light of daybreak.</note>. They are wide; therefore he said "the doors of the gates of the East." It does not "break through" there, because the sun has room. When it comes out to the world, there are narrow windows; therefore he said, "He breaks through the windows of the firmament." The sun does not move in its full strength by itself, for it would burn the entire world; rather, the Holy One, Blessed be He, brings it out gently. This is the meaning of "He brings the sun out of its place and the moon from its dwelling-place." The "dwelling-place" <note>original: "מכון שבתה" — *makhon shivtah*</note> is the dome of the heavens that the sun possesses. It is like a bowl or a palm-branch turned toward the moon; this is "and the moon from its dwelling-place." When the sun comes out in the morning or evening, its light spreads through the whole world. Therefore, "sun" is used in the feminine sense here: *Let there be lights* <note>In Hebrew, the verb "let there be" here can be read as feminine</note>. It does not say *Ma'orot* [masculine]. This is as in: *If the sun has risen [zarachah, feminine] upon him*. It implies it is fruitful and multiplies. But when the sun is at its full strength, it is spoken of in the masculine: *The sun had risen [yatza, masculine] upon the earth*. It goes forth like a groom. 

Likewise, "the sun in its strength" for six hours of the day. From the River of Fire that stands between the shells, He congealed the River of Fire like cheese and created the Sun. The appearance of the fire was darkened like milk that has already become cheese. Afterward, He separated the firmament above the shell of Fire, and below was the darkness of the waters. There was darkness, and He created the sun, moon, stars, and constellations, giving each one a dwelling according to its honor.

**And know** that the stars stand in the firmament both by day and by night, but the glare of the sun prevents them from being seen. It is like a person who holds a flame in the sun; it cannot be seen from a distance because it is clear. If you put glowing coals in the sun, they look like ash. Therefore, by day, the glare of the sun covers their light and they are not seen. But at night, when the sun is below the Ocean <note>original: "ים אונקיינוס" — *Yam Okeanos*, the Great Ocean surrounding the world in medieval geography.</note>, the stars are visible. 

If you ask: Why were the tiny, thin stars created if they do not provide light? It is because the upper waters are cold, and He created the heavenly host to warm them. In the morning, what appears as a star "leaving its place" is the radiance of the day arriving; it appears like the glow of a star shooting from its place like an arrow. But the star actually stays in its path and does not move: *not one is missing* <note>Isaiah 40:26</note>. In their movement, they praise Him. He prepared paths for them in the heavens, and in the place of the paths, He made the work of the firmament greenish-blue <note>original: "ירקרק" — *yerakrak*, can mean green or yellow; here likely referring to the color of the sky.</note> so that they might be better seen outside, to shine upon the earth. He placed them in the lower thickness of the firmament close to the earth. Thus, they shine upon the earth and not within the firmament. Through the stars and constellations, a person can know everything that will happen on earth, whether good or bad. 

Set it in your heart that the Holy One, Blessed be He, knows all. If a flesh-and-blood human can see things through the constellations, how much more so the King of Kings, the Holy One, Blessed be He, who knows everything? For He is the one who informs all flesh and blood. It goes without saying that He knows all; blessed be He, and may His mention be exalted forever and ever—certainly and naturally so.

<summary>This page describes the physical and mystical architecture of the heavens, comparing the firmament's creation to the congealing of glass or cheese and explaining how the sun and stars are positioned within "beams" and "windows" of the celestial spheres.</summary>
<keywords>firmament, architecture of heaven, River of Fire, Nehar Dinur, sun and moon, celestial windows, stars, constellations, Divine knowledge, cosmogony</keywords>

--- Page 54 ---
<meta>This page continues the exploration of the celestial architecture and the divine administration of the universe. While the previous page focused on the physical "beams" of the heavens and the waters they hold, this page shifts to the roles of the stars, the specific angels governing the months, and the mystical origins of the sun, moon, and stars from the River of Fire.</meta>

# The Great Book of Raziel

<margin>Therefore</margin> small stars were created; it is not that they provide no light, but rather they were made to warm the world with the light of their flame. For if you do not say so, why were they created? What need would the world have for them? However, He created everything for the benefit of the world. From the strength of their rotation, the stars run in their courses, as do the constellations <note>original: "מזלות" — *mazalot*, referring to the signs of the zodiac.</note> and the luminaries, running and rushing. A "Guard" <term>Officer/Sovereign</term> runs with them, and twenty-three noble princes are appointed to make the constellations and luminaries run. Therefore, he is called **Rahtiel**, from the language of "running" <note>original: "רהט" — *rahat*, an Aramaic and Rabbinic Hebrew root for running or rushing.</note>, as "running" is translated as *rahata*. 

Indeed, all the angels are named according to what they are appointed over. For example: **Raziel**, who delivered the *Book of Secrets* <note>original: "ספר הרזים" — *Sefer HaRazim*. Raziel means "Secret of God."</note> through which Adam the first man gained wisdom. There are also angels over the months:

*   **Shafiel** is the prince over the month of **Nissan** <note>March/April</note>, and with him are 36 kings.
*   **Raghiel** is the prince over **Iyar** <note>April/May</note>, and with him are 43 angels.
*   **Arinaor** is the prince over **Sivan** <note>May/June</note>, and with him are 65 angels.
*   **Ta’atz Bon** is the prince over **Tammuz** <note>June/July</note>, and with him are 28 angels.
*   **Tachar Gar** is the prince over **Av** <note>July/August</note>, and with him are 29 angels.
*   **Mural** is the prince over **Elul** <note>August/September</note>, and with him are 29 angels.
*   **Pachadron** is the prince over **Tishrei** <note>September/October</note>, and with him are 29 angels.
*   **Lirbag** is the prince over **Marcheshvan** <note>October/November</note>, and with him are 27 angels.
*   **Arganor** is the prince over **Kislev** <note>November/December</note>, and with him are 29 angels.
*   **Averchiel** is the prince over **Adar** <note>February/March</note>, and with him are 28 angels. 

Since the prince is called **Averchiel**, which is a language of "blessing" <note>original: "ברכה" — *berachah*. The name contains the root B-R-K.</note>, beneath him are angels with names of goodness, such as **Rumiel, Chashiel, Tafdiel, Shalmiel, Malkiel**, and all their like. The Holy One, blessed be He, elevated and warned Mordechai in the month of Adar.

And afterward, there are Favor and Grace and the "Book of Remembrances," and peace came to Mordechai, and he became second to the King. He gave blessings to the King of Glory: **Nuriel** is over the *Dinur* <term>River of Fire</term>; **Shamshiel** and **Yarchiel** are over the Sun and the Moon; **Kochviel** is over the stars; **Shofriel** is over the Books of Life—so named for the "words of beauty" <note>original: "אמרי שפר" — *imrei shefer*, from Genesis 49:21</note>; **Zochuchiel** is over the merit <term>purity/virtue</term> of Israel; and **Yehudael** is over the angels. 

All these are under the Most High. All the angels are named according to their dominion. When the Holy One, blessed be He, combined the letters and made the "Sphere of Speech," He saw all the actions He was to create. He called out and they came above, given names through wisdom. 

Because the sun shines and the stars were created from the **River of Fire** <note>original: "נהר דינור" — *Nahar Dinur*, a mystical river of fire mentioned in Daniel 7:10.</note>, it is located in the second firmament to lead the constellations to the completion of the cold constellations. He made pools of water before the sun to cool the sun's heat and to temper their coldness. On the fourth day, He brought forth the Sun from the River of Fire, and afterward the Moon, and afterward the stars. They began to move in the firmament, separated from the River of Fire in their rows. He throws a molten mass behind them; thus, the stars move toward one side. 

It is like a man who prepares cheese: first, he extracts the cheese from the milk, and from what remains of the milk, he cooks it until it becomes like cheese, yet it is not so white. Therefore, the Sun is white and shines more than the stars, and the Moon's light is diminished upon the waters. The River of Fire was created, and from it, the luminaries were created. What the Sages said—that the river comes from the sweat of the *Chayot* <term>Holy Living Creatures</term> and that they were created in two years—they are one and the same: they come from the sweat near the Throne of Glory, and it shall rest upon the heads of the wicked.

<margin>And in the firmament</margin> the second, there is a certain River of Fire from which the constellations are judged. For our Rabbis said: "Were it not for the 'Tail of the Pleiades' <note>original: "זנב כימה" — *Zanav Kimah*. The Pleiades constellation was thought to have a cooling effect that counteracted the heat of the sun or fire.</note> [tempering] the River of Fire, no one would be saved because of the cold." The constellations are in the second firmament like a belt. Regarding the sun's path: the River of Fire moves by day, and it is called "day" during the sun's movement. At night, it moves in darkness, for the world was water, and surrounding the water was a shell that enclosed the water. And surrounding that shell...

...the River of Fire moves in one shell. That which was above in the shell moved, and this occurred during the sun's movement by day. That which was below in the shell was in the place of the sun by night. Then it would move in the darkness of the waters—for the waters are below—and in the lower part of the shell, there were clouds, as is the way of water. But above, they are clear. It is written: *And the darkness He called Night* <note>Genesis 1:5</note>, meaning the place of darkness is called the place of Night when the sun moves there at night. 

Thus it was for every single constellation: it has an ascent on the side. For the firmament—He took from the edge of the firmament from His great fire, and from the moisture of His holy word and His holy spirit, and He mixed them further, and it became like...

It explains the creation of the luminaries from the "River of Fire" using a metaphor of cheese-making and describes the sun's cooling mechanism in the second firmament.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the celestial hierarchy and the elements. While the previous page listed the angelic princes of the months, this page explores the relationship between the four fundamental elements—earth, water, air (wind), and fire—and how they are ordered within the structure of the cosmos according to various biblical books.</meta>

# The Great Book of Raziel

<page-num>42</page-num>
<sig>2 2 6</sig>

The space beneath the clouds is full of wind <note>original: "רוח" — *ruach*, which means both "wind" and "spirit."</note>; therefore, every bird flies upward because the wind gives it the strength to fly. He "makes His messengers spirits" <note>Psalm 104:4</note> who are sent to the earth regarding the matters of the world. Since he mentioned the angels roaming the world and mentioned his ministers as a "flaming fire," He "founded the earth upon its foundations." The earth we are upon is covered by the deep <note>original: "תהום" — *tehom*, referring to the primordial depths.</note> as a garment; these are the seven depths that exist between the four lands. "Water stands upon the mountains"—this refers to the water beneath the earth, as it is written: "To Him who spread out the earth above the waters." The water is upon the mountains, and the mountains are suspended in the dust. And according to the secret meaning of Proverbs, "the heavens are height and the earth is depth." Who has ascended to the heavens, which are above? Who has gathered the wind, which is near the heavens? For wind is near the heavens because "by His breath <term>spirit/wind</term> the heavens are cleared," and the wind brings the clouds.

Who has bound the waters after the wind? "The wind blew, and the waters flowed." Rain comes by means of the wind that "establishes all the ends of the earth," so that the clouds may empty their waters upon the earth. At the beginning of Proverbs, the order is earth, heavens, and water—but not wind—because the earth was created first. Afterward, since he mentioned the foundation of the world, the earth and the heavens are tied to it, with water above and below. Then the foundation of the earth began; He hissed snow into the water and it became mud like a garden bed. This is "The Lord by wisdom <note>original: "חכמה" — *chokhmah*</note> founded the earth," and afterward "established the heavens by understanding" <note>original: "תבונה" — *tevunah*</note>, for He took the power of fire from the sparkling appearances and the pure waters and created the heavens with understanding. Afterward, "by His knowledge <note>original: "דעת" — *da'at*</note> the depths were broken up," meaning the depths beneath were split, and afterward "the skies drop down the dew" as a blessing. In every place where dew comes, there is no wind, which is why wind was mentioned here.

**And because** the earth is the foundation, in every place where a foundation is mentioned, the earth is prioritized over the heavens. But in Psalms, the order is wind, heavens, earth, and sea—from top to bottom—because the wind surrounds it, as it is written: "He made darkness His secret place; His pavilion round about Him were dark waters and thick clouds of the skies." The wind is there; this is "Whither shall I go from Your spirit <note>original: "מרוחך" — *meruchakha*</note>?" And he further says, "Whither shall I flee from Your presence?" He said: "If I ascend to heaven, You are there," and afterward "if I make my bed in the netherworld <note>original: "שאול" — *sheol*</note>," which is like dust at the end of the sea, "there Your hand shall lead me." Darkness refers to the blackness of the world; there, "the darkness hideth not from You." In Psalms, he establishes the order as earth, heavens, and height, because he mentioned "the foundations of the mountains shall tremble." In His weariness, He acted upon the earth first, and "the earth shook and trembled." Afterward, "He bowed the heavens and came down"—that is above. Afterward, "He was seen upon the wings of the wind"—that is surrounding the height. Afterward, "darkness of water and thick clouds of the skies"—that is surrounding Him. Thus, it is counted from bottom to top. 

In Job, the order is earth, water, heavens, and wind: "He hangeth the earth upon nothing," and it is written "To Him who spread out the earth above the waters." Therefore his brother said, "He described a circle upon the face of the waters" by means of the heavens over the ocean. Therefore, "the pillars of heaven tremble," and afterward "by His spirit the heavens are cleared." Furthermore, from bottom to top: earth, and then "He bindeth up the waters in His thick clouds," and then the heavens which are above the clouds, and then the wind which is near them. And in Job, it speaks of water, heavens, earth, and wind in another matter concerning rain: first the water of the <unclear>Hachifa</unclear> region in the ground, then the rain descends, and the water is upon the firmament and descends to the earth. The wind is exalted in the descent of the rain, for "He draweth up the drops of water" which the skies pour down from a full or prepared stream; these skies are from the gates of water. The water descends in its entirety, and afterward "its light is emptied upon the wings of the earth." The wind blows: "out of the chamber cometh the storm." 

In another part of Job, the order is earth, water, heavens, and no wind, because he mentioned the foundations of the ancient earth, which is the base, and afterward the sea which was around it, and afterward the heavens which are connected to the sea. "I was there when the earth was founded," and "who laid the cornerstone?" What is around it? The sea. "He shut up the sea with doors." There is a brother there... the wings of the earth. Afterward, you are informed of the "statutes of heaven" which are tied to the foundations, having mentioned the foundations of the earth and water. For Him who spread out the earth and afterward created the heavens, he did not mention wind because it is not visible to the eye. Furthermore, the wind was not created to the extent that one could say the Torah preceded it. Therefore he says, "before the earth existed," and "when there were no depths," and "when He prepared the heavens, I was there." 

In Jeremiah, the order is earth and heavens: "He made the earth by His power, He established the world by His wisdom, and by His understanding He stretched out the heavens. At the sound of His giving a multitude of waters in the heavens..." He is not speaking of the creation of the world here, but rather responding to it. He said, "Learn not the way of the nations, and be not dismayed at the signs of heaven," for they were practicing idolatry and looking to silver and gold. They are like "scarecrows in a cucumber patch," as it is written in Zechariah: "the Teraphim <note>original: "תרפים" — household idols used for divination.</note> have spoken vanity." The prophet was showing that the idols do not speak. He restrains the dew and rain except according to His word, in order to refute the Teraphim. This is what it says in Zechariah: "Ask of the Lord rain in the time of the latter rain; the Lord shall make lightning streaks <note>original: "חזיזים" — *chazizim*.</note> and give them showers of rain, to every one grass in the field." That is to say, one should ask from the Holy One, Blessed be He; but "the Teraphim have spoken vanity." Similarly here, "He makes the earth by His power" speaks of those deeds as it is written concerning Egypt, Judah, and Edom. He says, "Be not afraid of them, for they cannot do evil, neither is it in them to do good." "There is none like You, O Lord... the Lord is the true God; at His wrath the earth shall tremble, and the nations shall not be able to abide His indignation." 

What will He do to them? They shall say: "The gods that have not made the heavens and the earth, even they shall perish from the earth and from under these heavens," by the withholding of rain that He shall bring upon them. And He "makes the earth by His power" over the mountains, as it is written: "Who by His strength setteth fast the mountains." Regarding the living, as it is written: from those mountains they shall perish. "He established the world by His wisdom" refers to the inhabited world <note>original: "תבל" — *tevel*.</note>, the place of many people and confusion <note>original: "בלבול" — *bilbul*, a play on words with *Tevel* and *Babel*.</note>. The Holy One, Blessed be He, shall execute His vengeance with His wisdom, as it is written in Jeremiah: "Hear the counsel of the Lord that He hath taken against Babylon, and His purposes that He hath purposed against the land of the Chaldeans: Surely the least of the flock shall draw them out." The young lions shall gather against them for war. Regarding Nebuchadnezzar, it is written in Isaiah: "You said in your heart, I will ascend into heaven." It was impossible for him to do so; rather, he was establishing fortresses upon the mountains, as it is written: "great cities fortified up to heaven." "By His understanding He stretched out the heavens" from above "at the sound of His giving a multitude of waters in the heavens." When they would say there will be rain, the Holy One, Blessed be He, would change it and not answer according to the natural order of the world. 

The reason it says "stretched out the heavens" and does not say "created the heavens" is because those stargazers of theirs cannot see except when the heavens are full of stars, like at the beginning or end of the month. Then it is appropriate to say "stretched," like a thing stretched out so that you can see. They see all these stars because the heavens are clear for His sake. Therefore it is written, "At the sound of His giving a multitude of waters," for there is no thunder except on a day when it is not cloudy. "For all the gods of the nations are idols," like this world which they act upon through the constellations <note>original: "מזלות" — *mazalot*.</note>. But the Lord made the heavens with power; He made the constellations, therefore they are faithful. And by the word of the Lord the heavens were made. "He made the earth by His power," yet they abandon Him; therefore "they shall perish from the earth." And there it is written: "Wherefore should the nations say, where is now their God?"

<summary>This page analyzes the biblical order of the elements (earth, water, air, and fire) across different books like Psalms, Proverbs, Job, and Jeremiah to explain the mechanics of creation and weather. It emphasizes that while nations worship the stars and idols, God alone controls the rain and the celestial bodies.</summary>

<keywords>creation, elements, wind, rain, depths, Tehom, wisdom, understanding, stars, constellations, Mazalot, Teraphim, Jeremiah, Job, Nebuchadnezzar</keywords>

--- Page 56 ---
<meta>This page continues the cosmological exploration from the previous page, moving from the movement of birds and the wind to the specific creation and division of the element of Water, as well as the alignment of the four elements with the signs of the Zodiac.</meta>

<header>The Great Book of Raziel</header>

# The Great Book of Raziel

**Water**—Mem [the letter M] is for water, and He tied a knot for it, etc. <note>original: "מים מ' במים וקשר לו קשר" — A reference to the Sefer Yetzirah (Book of Formation), where the letter Mem is associated with the element of water.</note> In the *Book of Formation*, the Holy One, blessed be He, brought forth spirit <term>ruach: wind or spirit</term> from His holy spirit, and He engraved and carved the spirit through his light. This spirit which He breathed into creation formed the four winds of the world, and a spirit within each one. This is like a craftsman making glass vessels: he takes a hollow iron tool that is pierced from end to end, takes glass from the furnace, and places one end of the tool in the person's mouth. The end of the tool in his mouth is blown through with the breath of his mouth; the breath passes through the hollow tool and reaches the glass attached to the end of the hollow tool. The breath of his mouth that he blew into the molten glass creates a vessel—be it large or small, long or short, wide or many-sided—according to his strength. How much more so for the Great, Mighty, and Awesome God! When He brought forth His spirit into the expansion of the world's void, He brought forth water from His immeasurable moisture, and He carved and engraved the Throne of Glory and all the heavenly host. The Holy One, blessed be He, did not wish to make them from the great fire, lest they seem equal to Him, for He is "a consuming fire." So He took water, fire, and spirit and created His ministers. He divided the water into three parts: from the first part He congealed the water and boiled it with fire and smoke, trembling and dust, and the Earth was made. From the second part He made all the heavenly host and the upper heavens. The third part became the Firmament—the expanse above us to separate the waters from the waters. Half was above and half was below. The waters that He took below were happy, saying, "How fortunate are we that we are close to our Creator, and are happy and supported beneath the Throne of Glory!" They overflowed with great joy, giving praise, thanks, and glory, crowning the Creator of the world with a crown and praise, as it is said: "More than the voices of many waters..." <note>Psalm 93:4</note>. In the account of Creation <note>Genesis 1</note>, you find the word "water" eleven times, and in the account of the descendants of Noah, you find "water" eleven times. This is to say that in the eleventh generation there was Noah and the Flood. The Holy One, blessed be He, knew that the waters would return to the world. And in the section regarding the suspected wayward woman <note>the Sotah, Numbers 5</note>, the word "water" appears eleven times because the generation of the Flood sinned with a woman <term>eshet ish: a married woman</term> like a wayward wife. The Holy One, blessed be He, decreed upon the water that it should come from the upper waters. He decreed to bring water upon Egypt eleven times. In the word for "water" <note>Hebrew: מים — mayim</note>, there are two instances of the letter Mem; thus there are twenty-two Mems <note>perhaps referring to the 22 letters of the Hebrew alphabet being the foundation of creation</note>. Therefore, there are twenty-two Mems in the verse: "And the Lord said to Moses, 'Say to Aaron: Take your staff and stretch out your hand over the waters of Egypt, over their rivers, over their canals, and over their pools...'" <note>Exodus 7:19</note>. This is to say that with everything, it was given, for "there is nothing new under the sun." Therefore, in the song of salvation <note>at the Red Sea</note>, from the beginning of the song until "Moses led Israel," the word "water" <note>or the letter Mem</note> is found eleven times. Also in Joshua, "water" appears eleven times: eight times regarding the "waters of the Jordan" and three times regarding the "water" that He stipulated would divide the sea before the children of Israel. Likewise, "and the sea returned to its strength <term>le-eitano</term> at daybreak"—to the condition <term>li-tenao</term> that He had stipulated with the sea during the six days of Creation. The upper waters are suspended by the command of the Holy One, blessed be He. It is not said "between the waters that are *on* the Firmament," but rather "from *above* the Firmament," meaning they are suspended by His word.

**The stream** <note>original: פלג — Peleg</note> of God is full of water. This teaches that God divided the waters, placing half in the Firmament and half in the Ocean. The Firmament is like a pool, and above the pool is a dome. Because of the pool, the dome responds, and it sweats thick drops which descend into the salt water. The upper waters are clearer than anything, and the upper waters are greater than the lower waters by thirty measures. This is the meaning of "between waters and waters"—thirty waters. This is why "A Song of Ascents" <note>Psalm 120-134</note> consists of thirty levels, for there were thirty levels above and below. Why are the upper ones called more than the lower? Because the windows of the brilliance of the water were included in the fire and created the angels. When it burns in the eyes of the angels, the upper waters are male and the lower waters are female. Between the upper and lower waters there are only three finger-breadths, and the circumference of the Firmament separates them. Therefore, Isaiah began: "Who has measured the waters in the hollow of his hand?" <note>Isaiah 40:12</note>. To the Name <term>God</term>, water is "Heaven and Earth," as found in the *Seder Olam*. These are the upper waters, for the firmaments have a type of cup, a receptacle for water, as it says: "Who has measured the waters in the hollow of his hand"—either by hand or by the sole of the foot. And afterwards "Heavens," and afterwards "Earth," and afterwards the "Spirit <term>Ruach</term> of the Living Creatures," for the world is spirit, and the spirit hovers over the surface of the waters above and below, and they are suspended in a stormy wind. But in Genesis, the order is: Heavens, Earth, Spirit, Water. In the order of the Zodiac signs, the names of the Fire signs are **T-A-K** <note>Taleh/Aries, Aryeh/Leo, Keshet/Sagittarius</note>; the Earth signs are **Sh-B-G** <note>Shor/Taurus, Betulah/Virgo, Gidi/Capricorn</note>; the Wind/Air signs are **T-M-R** <note>Teomim/Gemini, Moznayim/Libra, Dli/Aquarius</note>; and the Water signs are **S-A-R** <note>Sartan/Cancer, Akrav/Scorpio, Dagim/Pisces</note>. Why does Fire come first? Was not Earth first according to the School of Hillel? Rather, the Heavens are Fire, and the Divine Presence <term>Shekhinah</term> is in the heavens; therefore the Fire signs (**T-A-K**) come first, and Aries <term>Taleh</term> is the head of the Zodiac. Therefore, Fire came first: "In the beginning God created the Heavens"—the Fire signs. After them come the Earth signs (**Sh-B-G**), for fire burns earth and wood, making them dust. After them are the Wind signs (**T-M-R**), for wind dries the earth: "And God caused a wind to pass over the earth." The earth receives the wind so that it does not destroy the seeds. After them come the Water signs (**S-A-R**): "He causes His wind to blow, and the waters flow," and the ships of the sea go. After water comes fire, for Cancer <term>Sartan</term> is water, and after it is the fire of Leo <term>Aryeh</term>, which is fire, because water extinguishes fire. And after Leo, which is fire, comes Virgo <term>Betulah</term>, which is earth, for fire burns earth. And after the earth of the sign of Virgo comes Libra <term>Moznayim</term>, which is wind/air, for the earth and the wind are great—they break the wind. And after Libra, the wind, comes Scorpio <term>Akrav</term>, which is water, for the wind breaks the waves of the sea and blows the water and clouds. But the main point is in Ecclesiastes: "The earth stands forever." He mentions the generation of the earth—a generation goes and a generation comes, and the dust returns to what it was. And "generation to generation" refers to the Resurrection of the Dead, and the spirit returns. And afterwards he says of the earth: "The sun rises," for he did not need to mention the heavens, as they are only recognized through the sun. And after heavens comes the wind: "The wind goes round and round," literally the wind, for the wind goes to the four corners because it goes with the sun. After wind comes water: "All the rivers go to the sea." Because in Ecclesiastes he aligned them correctly, it is written: "Words of truth." But David aligned them in a different order: "By the word of the Lord the heavens were made, and by the breath <term>spirit/wind</term> of His mouth all their host; He gathers the waters of the sea as a heap; let all the earth fear the Lord." This is to say that through the spirit, the heavens were beautified; therefore the order is Spirit, Earth, Water. And after spirit comes sea, for the spirit gathers the waves and guards them. After them comes earth, because of the verse: "Will you not fear Me? says the Lord, who placed the sand as a bound for the sea." And David aligned them another way: Heavens, Spirit, Earth—ordering them from top to bottom. He "makes light like a flame," and "light dwells with Him" on high. He "stretches out the heavens like a curtain"—these are the upper heavens. He "makes the clouds His chariot"—these are the musicians beneath the heavens. And "He walks on the wings of the wind"—for all the air...

<summary>This page explains the mystical creation and division of water using the metaphor of a glassblower and explores the numerical significance of "water" in the Bible. It also details the relationship between the four elements—Fire, Earth, Air, and Water—and their corresponding groups of Zodiac signs.</summary>

--- Page 57 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on the elemental division of the cosmos. Having established the nature of water and the winds, the text now ascends into the celestial spheres, specifically focusing on the third heaven, its angelic inhabitants, and the mystical names of God that govern existence.</meta>

[Page 24]

# The Great Book of Raziel

Manna rained down upon them to eat, for there are angels in the *Shechakim* <note>The third of the seven heavens; the name literally means "clouds" or "skies."</note> who grind manna for the righteous. This is the meaning of the verse "And His strength is in the *Shechakim*"—that which provided you with aid. And "strength" refers to the third heaven <note>The text associates "strength" (Oz) with the number three and this specific layer of heaven.</note>. Why are the *Shechakim* distinguished from all other lofty firmaments? Because the might of the Holy One, blessed be He, is within them. His strength is His majesty, His beauty, His Divine Presence (*Shekhinah*), and His Torah. 

Jerusalem and the Holy Temple are also called His "strength," as it is written: "Ascribe strength to God; His majesty is over Israel, and His strength is in the *Shechakim*." His majesty is as written: "He rides the heavens to help you, and in His majesty [He rides] the skies (*Shechakim*)." The *Shekhinah* is there, as it is written: "And He commanded the *Shechakim* from above." There is no "command" except that of the *Shekhinah*, as it is written: "And the Lord God commanded." Thus, the Holy One, blessed be He, commanded the *Shechakim* to provide types of food and sustenance for Israel, as it says: "And He rained manna upon them to eat." His Torah is there, as it is written: "And strength is in the *Shechakim*," and "strength" refers to nothing but the Torah, as it is said: "The Lord will give strength to His people; the Lord will bless His people with peace." Jerusalem and the Holy Temple are there, built of precious stones, pearls, sapphire, and every kind of blessing and costly stone, as it is said: "And the *Shechakim* shall drip with righteousness." "Righteousness" refers to nothing but Jerusalem and the Holy Temple, as it is written: "Righteousness lodged within her, but now murderers." Jerusalem and the Holy Temple stand suspended by chains of fire within the *Shechakim*. During the day, they shine within the orb of the sun, and the angels of the hosts—camps and classes of them—are within it. A ladder is set up in the *Shechakim*, and its top reaches to the *Zevul* <note>The fourth heaven, the "Habitation."</note>. Holy angels—and some say noble princes—are appointed over it and over the gates of the *Shechakim*. At the gates of the North are six princes; at the gates of the South are six princes; at the gates of the East are six princes; and at the West are six princes. Badu'el, the prince of the host, is appointed over him. 

And why is it called *Shechakim*? Because there they grind (*shochakim*) manna for the righteous, as well as dew and rain, to bring joy to Israel in this world and the world to come. Furthermore, do not read it as *Shechakim* (skies), but as *Sechakim* (those who play/rejoice), for they rejoice with every kind of song, praise, and melody before the Divine Presence in the Temple on high, as it is said: "Splendor and majesty are before Him." For this is the Temple in the *Shechakim*, whose radiance covers all the chambers of the firmament from the heights, as it is said: "His glory covered the heavens." Another explanation: *Shechakim* teaches that groups of camps stand in the Temple in the *Shechakim* to sing before Him every day. Each camp consists of 918 <note>original: תתרג"ח</note> great ministering angels. The word *Shechakim* corresponds numerically to 958 <note>original: תתר"נח</note>, for these 958 camps surround the Divine Presence in the *Shechakim* and exalt the name of the Holy One, blessed be He, with every kind of praise and melody from morning until evening. They say before Him: "Holy, Holy, Holy," and from morning until evening they say: "Blessed be the glory of the Lord from His place." Why? Because the Divine Presence is above them, exalted in their secret dwelling, as it is written: "Truly You are a God who hides Himself."

<margin>The Firmament</margin> The third firmament is full of treasures of mist, and from it winds go forth. Within it are the gifts of thunder and lightning. From it they emerge, and three princes sit upon their thrones. The appearance of these thrones is like fire, and the fire gleams like gold. They rule over all the angels of fire. They are within the fire of their majesty; their voice is the fire of the sound of thunder; their eyes are like the conduits of the sun. They rule over the wheels of flame and heat. They have wings for flying. The neighing of their mouths is like horses; their appearance is like torches. They speak and tremble, roar and fly, soaring at every sound of the wind and flying to every corner.

<margin>These</margin> are the names of the ministering princes in the third dwelling (*Ma'on*): The first is Yakhniel, and 619 <note>original: שש י"ט</note> angels minister before him. They are appointed over every matter of fire, to kindle and to extinguish. The name of the second is Rahtiel, and 63 angels minister before him. He rules over everything involving chariots of fire and horses of fire, to make them run or stumble. The name of the third is Dalkiel, and 19 angels minister before him. They rule over the flames of fire to light the "House" and to immerse the *Shechakim* in it. In the *Shechakim*, every instrument sings before the Holy One, blessed be He. Sandalphon <note>A legendary archangel of immense height who weaves crowns for God from the prayers of the faithful.</note> is appointed over him; he ties crowns for his Maker. Concerning the "Work of the Chariot" <note>Ma'aseh Merkavah: the study of the divine throne-chariot in Jewish mysticism.</note>, it is said that Sandalphon ties *Tefillin* <note>Phylacteries; small leather boxes containing Torah verses worn during prayer.</note> upon the head of the Rock of Worlds, the Lord God of Israel. And in those *Tefillin* is written: "Who is like Your people, Israel?"

<margin>And these</margin> are also the names of the angels who minister in the third level with Dalkiel, who stands there ruling over the flames of fire: Norah, Aviel, Malkiyah, Chuliel, Churiel, Chaviel, Shalchiel, Tzagriel, Kambiel, Akriel, Sasniel, Gezviel, Tumiel, Ammiel, Talmanof, Tachniaf, Arfiel, Agviel, Mashriel, Ammannan.

<margin>And these</margin> are the names of the angels who minister in all seven dwellings: In the first dwelling, Aniel; in the second, Tzadqiel; in the third, Hasdiel; in the fourth, Dorniel; in the fifth, Gabriel; in the sixth, Barkiel; and in the seventh, Michael.
<margin>And these</margin> are the angels who minister in the sea and all that is within it: Kuthiel, Tuthiel, Tavriel, Nakit, Va’ah.
<margin>And these</margin> rule over the rain: Arvot, Malkiel, Marmarot, Tartiel, Sakniel, Laqbatiel, Taniel, Gadiel, Ramiel, Yasaiel, Matriel, Litiel.

<margin>This is the Explicit Name</margin> It is the beginning of all names and the start of the names of the upper and lower beings. Its secret is "male and female," found in the head of Adam and Eve. It is the four-letter name: Y-H-W-H. This is the honored and awesome Name. It informs us of the reality of the Essence called the "First Cause" of all that exists—He whose existence is necessary by virtue of His own being. From the truth of His necessary existence, every being exists through His wisdom and His will, according to what the wisdom of His blessed will decreed. From this name is also derived the twelve-letter name—meaning, twelve words—and this is their permutation:

-> YHWH, YHHW, YWHH, HWYH, HWYH, HHYW, WHHY, WYHY, WYHH, WHHW, HWHW, HHWY <-

<margin>These</margin> names are required according to their permutations from the Unique Name. The root of their knowledge and their indication is clarified from the names written with me. And this is the forty-two letter name:

<sig>Abg</sig> <note>The first three letters of the famous 42-letter name "Ana B'Koach."</note>

It lists the angelic governors of the various dwellings, the sea, and the rain, and concludes with a kabbalistic analysis of the Tetragrammaton and its permutations.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the cosmological exposition, moving from the specific functions of the third heaven (Shechakim) to the broader elemental mechanics of the universe—focusing on the balance between fire, water, and wind, and the angelic hierarchy that maintains them.</meta>

# The Great Book of Raziel

Our God is in the heavens; whatever the Lord desired, He performed in heaven and on earth, in the seas and all the depths <note>original: "Tehomot"</note>. Their origins are all equal. Consider in your heart that in one place it is written that the heavens were first, as it says: "Whatever the Lord desired, He performed in heaven and on earth, in the seas and all the depths." Their origins are equal, yet heaven is listed first, balanced against "Our God is in the heavens." Therefore, "whatever the Lord desired, He performed in heaven." And what is written there—"At the sound of His giving a multitude of waters in the heavens"—means water precedes His wind <note>The text is analyzing the sequence of creation and natural phenomena to determine the hierarchy of the elements.</note>. For on a day when the heavens are clear, when thunder comes, water comes without wind. This is what is written: "By His understanding He stretched out the heavens." He stretched them like a kind of sky. How the clouds appear on a day of the rainbow, like in the summer months! 

In the Book of Job, it is written that earth, heaven, wind, and water are all subject to the Divine decree that the Holy One, blessed be He, ordains. "God understands its way, and He knows its place"—meaning, in which place He shall send the rain and how much. For "He looks to the ends of the earth," meaning He does not need to search, for He looks beneath all the heavens "to establish a weight for the wind." This is like a bladder <note>original: "Shalpuchit," likely referring to a bellows or a water-skin.</note> that travels to the sea; its length is a span and its width is a span <note>term: "Zeret" (Span). An ancient unit of measurement based on the distance between the thumb and little finger.</note>. An angel stands and fills it with water, and the Holy One immediately prepares the saturation. When the wind blows, it is divided into drops. If you fill a bladder with feathers and sit upon it, they disperse. So too, the waters are established by measure for every single land, as it is written: "Who has measured the waters in the hollow of His hand?" <note>original: "Be-sha'alo."</note> 

A "hollow" <note>term: "Sha'al." A measurement of volume equivalent to a handful.</note> is a vessel, as is written concerning the false prophets: "And will you profane Me among My people for handfuls <note>sha'ale</note> of barley and for pieces of bread?" The "hollow" is a measure used for measuring barley, as in "six measures of barley." And in the Book of Kings, regarding the King of Aram: "if the dust of Samaria shall suffice for handfuls <note>sha'alim</note> for all the people that follow me"—as if to say, "I will fill the city with dust until all the people at my feet have no doubt of the measure." 

"And meted out heaven with a span" <note>Isaiah 40:12</note>. Even the high mountains reaching toward the heavens were meted out with a span. "My hand laid the foundation of the earth, and My right hand spread out the heavens." And "heavens" can refer to mountains, as it is written in Amos: "Though they dig into Sheol <note>The underworld or the grave.</note>, from there shall My hand take them; though they climb up to heaven, from there will I bring them down." This is interpreted as: "if they hide from my eyes at the bottom of the sea"—for the seabed is called Sheol—and Mount Carmel is called "heaven." In the Book of Kings, in the song, it is written: "the foundations of heaven moved," and in Psalms: "the foundations of the earth moved." 

"Though you set your nest among the stars"—these stars are strong rocks, as it is written: "And if among the stars..." And Amaziah strengthened himself and slew his people, going to the Valley of Salt and smiting ten thousand of the children of Seir. And another ten thousand, left alive, the children of Judah carried away and brought to the top of the rock <note>Sela</note>, and they were all dashed to pieces. 

In every place where the "foundation of the earth" is written, earth is mentioned first and then heaven. But what is written in Isaiah—"And I have put My words in your mouth, and I have covered you in the shadow of My hand, that I may plant the heavens, and lay the foundations of the earth, and say unto Zion: 'You are My people'"—why is the language of "planting" used for the heavens? Are they not already planted and standing? Rather, "heavens" here refers to high mountains that shall be resettled. For Sennacherib <note>An Assyrian king who besieged Jerusalem in the 8th century BCE.</note> captured all the fortified cities of Judah, as it is written: "In the fourteenth year of King Hezekiah, Sennacherib king of Assyria came up against all the fortified cities of Judah and took them." All Israel fled from the cities; therefore it is said to Zion, "You are My people," because they did not flee. Concerning those in the cities of Judah who fled, it is written in Kings: "Israel was carried away out of their own land to Assyria unto this day." But in Chronicles, we hear that Hezekiah brought them back: "And Hezekiah sent to all Israel and Judah... that they should come to the house of the Lord at Jerusalem, to keep the Passover." This is also written in Isaiah: "Say unto the cities of Judah: 'Behold your God!'" Thus Jerusalem brings tidings to other places that they should come. Therefore, another verse says: "How beautiful upon the mountains are the feet of the messenger of good tidings"—referring to the King Messiah. Another verse says: "Get you up into the high mountain, O Zion, that brings good tidings." This herald says to the cities of Judah: "Behold the King in His glory!"

The Holy One, blessed be He, brought up the Upper Waters to sustain the creatures on earth, who could not live for even a moment otherwise because of the power of the Great Fire that is upon the highest heavens. But because of the great height, and the abundance of the Upper Waters, and the thickness of the firmament that separates the heavens, the creatures are able to survive the celestial fire. The Upper Waters cool the world from the fire above, and the fire above warms the world from the cold of the Upper Waters. 

The waters are suspended in light and are distant from the firmament by a journey of five hundred years. From these waters, He shall purify His people, as it is written: "And I will sprinkle clean water upon you, and you shall be clean." Through the steam of atonement, water sparkles from them; from them comes rain, like a person who places a vessel in a bathhouse—it fills with water because of the heat. The heat is even greater because of the heat of the sun. The Great Fires are male, and when the Depths <note>Tehomot</note> descend below, they long for them. Both are hidden from the Deep, as it is written: "Deep calls unto deep," for the deeps are female. Just as the female longs for the male, so the earth and the deep long for the descent of rain. This is why rain is called the "husband of the earth" <note>original: "ba'ala de-ar'a"</note>. 

Therefore, all purification is through immersion in water—"or you shall pass it through fire and it shall be clean." For the waters emerged from the goodness of His Holy Speech, and the fire from the power of the heat of His Speech. From both of them, the totality of the heavens was made. Therefore, we bless over water: "That everything exists by His word" <note>The "Shehakol" blessing said before drinking water.</note> and His power. The waters are suspended by the command of the Holy One, blessed be He; blessed be He and blessed be His name forever.

---

<margin>
Over the firmament is appointed **Hadarniel**. It is said of Hadarniel that he is taller than his companions by a journey of sixty thousand *parsa* <note>term: "Parsa" (Parasang). An ancient Persian unit of distance, roughly 3.5 to 4 miles.</note>. Every single word that leaves his mouth is accompanied by twelve thousand bolts of white lightning. The commander of the host is **Barkiel**, who is appointed over him. There are angels in the firmament who declare His power and how He conducts the world, as it is said: "And the firmament declares His handiwork."
</margin>

<margin>
**Nun**: This [letter] refers to the Holy One, blessed be He, who has **Fifty Gates of Wisdom**. All of them were given to Moses except for one, corresponding to the forty days [of the mountain] and the days from the first day of Passover until Shavuot <note>The 50 days of the Counting of the Omer.</note>; on the fiftieth day, the Torah was given. The "straightened Nun" <note>The final form of the letter Nun (ן).</note>—it is said in the *Alphabet of Rabbi Akiva* <note>A midrashic work on the mystical meanings of the Hebrew letters.</note> that this is the Holy One, the Faithful One (*Ne'eman*), who sits faithfully and stands faithfully, who decrees and fulfills. The Holy One sits and the angels stand, like those who have no joints in their legs <note>original: "kofztin"</note>, as it says: "Seraphim stood above Him." 

By our great merit, He bound the lands together. The earth is suspended upon a storm and tied to the "Curtains" <note>original: "Vilonin," the first layer of heaven.</note>. The Curtains are suspended by the storm and tied to the "Firmament" (*Raki'a*). The Firmament is suspended by the storm and tied to the "Habitation" (*Machon*). The Habitation is suspended by the storm and tied to the "Clouds" (*Aravot*). The Clouds are suspended by the storm and tied to the **Arm of the Holy One, blessed be He**. He pours down from the skies, as it is said: "The skies <note>Shechakim</note> pour down righteousness." And it is written: "He commanded the skies from above, and opened the doors of heaven," and rained upon them.
</margin>

<summary>This page describes the mystical balance between the celestial elements of fire and water, explaining how the "Upper Waters" protect the world from the heat of the divine fire. It also details the angelic governance of the firmament by Hadarniel and the cosmological structure where the heavens and earth are suspended by the word and arm of God.</summary>

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on the elemental mechanics of the universe and the divine decrees, moving into the mystical significance of the Divine Names, specifically the 72-letter name and the Tetragrammaton, and their relationship to the Sefirot and the cosmic cycles.</meta>

# The Great Book of Raziel

Their encirclements and their hosts are all included within one another in a unique unity, joined to a single oneness through the secret of the 72 awesome [Names], known as the Explicit Name <note>original: "Shem HaMeforash." This refers to the 72-fold name of God derived from Exodus 14:19-21.</note>. All of these are holy and pure powers, acting according to the laws in every matter where they are mentioned by a person who has received the tradition <note>original: "Mekubal," referring to an initiate or Kabbalist.</note> regarding them, their powers, and their operations. This includes the secret of the form and the examination, and the angel that emerges from every shape and letter of their name in their specific measure and language. Through each name of the Sefirot <note>term: "Sefirot" (The ten divine emanations or attributes through which the Infinite reveals and governs the universe).</note> belonging to Him, a person may understand the names, their power, and their associated angel. From the name of every letter in the name of the Sefirah, everything descends and cascades; one may then know the power of every word, angel, celestial sphere, star, and every supernal matter. 

Then every wise person shall gain insight and know awesome things, and every awesome and exalted secret that was hidden and concealed shall be revealed to them. They will understand the secret of the permutation of letters, their hidden meanings, their musical accents, their shapes, and all their movements. This will be known to every wise person, and from this, the understanding one will know how to join the supernal names and letters and how to combine the letters of the Honored and Awesome Name <note>Refers to the Tetragrammaton, Y-H-V-H.</note>. Every name has already been written, explained, and clarified according to its power, its substitution, its reversal, its shape, and its numerical value, along with the name of their power in their measure to perform every deed and action. This is explained and depicted only for use in holiness, purity, and ethical conduct; then one will be considered complete, like one of the supernal entourage. Upon this, one should set an understanding heart and complete intention to comprehend all these awesome things.

The explanation of the Awesome and Exalted Name—the Name of Unity, the four-letter Name <note>The Tetragrammaton: Y-H-V-H.</note>—is awesome. Its shape is explained as Y-H <note>The first two letters of the Name, often associated with the hidden, higher realms.</note>, and through its awesome power, V-H <note>The latter two letters, associated with the revealed world.</note> are explained in their secret. Every single name is explained in its concealed and revealed aspects, its reversal and combination, and its letters and shape. Everything follows a known measure to hint at its power and its very exalted and elevated place. Each one follows the power and shape that comes to every path. From here, you shall understand:

->The Whole Matter in its Clarity:<-

You shall know that the entire power of the Honored and Awesome Name lies in its hidden secret. This is the explanation for you of the four awesome letters, which point to the secret of the Great Truth, joining and clinging to those who say it. In brief, the secret of the Sabbatical cycle <note>term: "Shmitah" (A seven-year cosmic cycle in Kabbalistic chronology).</note> and its purpose is for a "Small Light" to emerge from them within the hidden secrets. For "Let there be light" <note>original: "Yehi Or." The text breaks "Yehi" into Y-H, part of the Divine Name.</note> is the source of the positive commandment, as it is written: "I am the Lord your God," and the negative commandment, as it is written: "You shall have no other gods." We heard "I am" and "You shall not have" directly from the Mouth of Power <note>A traditional idiom for God's revelation at Sinai.</note>. Regarding this, if the Daughter is Y-H-H, then the Mother is to the Daughter, and the Daughter is to the Mother <note>This is a highly cryptic reference to the relationships between the letters of the Name and the Sefirot of Binah (Mother) and Malkhut (Daughter).</note>. Then, if you understand the secret of the letter *He* in its form, the names will be called "I will be" <note>original: "Ehyeh."</note> for 188, 155, and Amnon; he shall be cut off, and for Israel.

<columns>2</columns>

The first *He* of the Name and the last *He* are two hands for holiness. One is to serve His hands, and it shall be for a single knot. The shape of the *He* is [composed of] the letters *Yod* and *Dalet*. "Hand" <note>original: "Yad" (Hand) equals 14 in gematria, composed of Yod=10 and Dalet=4.</note> is in three: the one *Dalet* with the word, up to its tip, its tail, and its body. This is a hint to three Sefirot. The *He* corresponds to five Sefirot, totaling 17 <note>original: "Gematria of 'Tov' (Good)."</note>. For the tip of the *Yod* is not counted toward the number of the final *Yod* of Y-H-V. This hints at the three final [Sefirot]. Thus, the ten Sefirot are hinted at in the word Y-H.

<column-break/>

And "Peace" <note>original: "Shalom."</note>—for He is Peace, and so is His shape. For the Name of the Soul <note>original: "Neshama."</note> is thus in its secret. The secret of the matter and its purpose is the joining of Y-H, hinting at three supernal matters, as it is said: *Yod*, *Dalet*, its tip, and its tail. And the *He* for the five Sefirot, and another *Yod* for the other three supernal spiritual ones, in the secret of Ten. This is an exalted and hidden secret. At the end, the [letter] *Aleph* is hidden, for Y-H-V-D are attached yet not attached, because of the Sabbatical cycle of Isaac. The *Yod* and *Vav* are always attached, and the *Dalet* and *Vav* are attached to the *He* for the world of the Jubilee <note>term: "Yovel" (The 50th year cycle, associated with the Sefirah of Binah).</note>.

->Time 107<-

"The Sabbatical cycle" <note>original: "Ha-Shmitah."</note> in numerical value <note>term: "Gematria."</note> is 369. With its letters and the word itself, it is 376, which is the same value as "Peace" <note>original: "Shalom."</note>. The meaning of 376 is "Turn away from me" <note>original: "Sha'u minni."</note>, and this refers to Esau the Wicked, about whom Jacob said, "Where?" <note>original: "Ayneh."</note> and they came in peace to his father's house. And may I have peace instead of "Esau," which is "Shua" <note>meaning "cry for help" or "noble."</note>, just like "Canaanite." And if you place the number of "My brother" <note>original: "Achi" = 19.</note> next to "Esau" <note>376</note>, it equals 395, the value of "Soul" <note>original: "Neshama."</note>, about which he said, "Deliver me from the hand of my brother, from the hand of Esau." The word "from the hand" appears twice, which in gematria equals 28. "Deliver me" hints at the "End" <note>original: "Ketz."</note> with its letters. It is Isaac <note>original: "Yitzchak."</note>, half the number of 375 with the word. He prayed during troubles that they should be a wonder for me <note>wordplay on "Pele" (Wonder) and "Philo."</note>, to give relief between them. We shall merit Him who dwells in the heavens; Isaac shall laugh with them, and His kingdom shall be revealed over us. Through the mercy in Isaac, with the addition of the letter *Yod*, he becomes "Crown" <note>original: "Keter," the highest Sefirah.</note> as is known. "He placed a royal crown upon her head and made her queen instead of Vashti."

->Esau my son is a perfect ruler:<-

This [is included] in that. Now it is Y-H-V, for the *He* is below, and when it rises, it is called Y-H-V-H. The *He* will be exchanged for *Aleph-Bet* in the aforementioned four-letter Name: *Aleph* for the upper ones and *He* for the lower ones, attached to one another, one in place of the other. Now and in the future. "Now" is as it is written: "From between the two Cherubim He shall speak." And for the future: "Yours is the day," even though it says "The God of Abraham, the God of Isaac, and the God of Jacob," Isaac is in the middle. In the future, the *He* will rise to the place of Y-H, and it is said in the supernal secret: "The Great, Mighty, and Awesome God," to join Peace. And when the Tenth <note>Refers to the tenth Sefirah, Malkhut.</note> builds the palace, a full Name will be said over a full world, for the sake of the unity of Truth and Peace. At the end, the *Vav* hints at the two Cherubim, and the *Zayin* <note>Numerical value 7.</note> hints at Beauty <note>original: "Tiferet."</note>; therefore, they are always attached. The Name Y-H is to be joined to the upper ones and formed in the lower ones at the end of the Sabbatical cycle. "And she shall return to her father's house as in her youth," as it is said: "Yours is the day, Yours also is the night," in the secret of "Let there be light." For this is the secret of the Name Y-H-V-H throughout everything. At the beginning, "Let there be light," and in the end, Y-H-V-H will be in its place. Then the four-letter Name will be full and complete, and the letters will be joined to the Name and ascend to the place of Emanation <note>term: "Atzilut" (The highest of the four worlds in Kabbalah).</note> in the name of the supernal, awesome, and hidden Y-H-V, which is called in its secret "Peace," as it is written: "And He called it: The Lord is Peace." And He is called "God" <note>original: "El."</note> during the Sabbatical cycle, and they exchange because of His furnace. This is a sublime secret in all of them: five ascending and five descending, found in one unity in the middle. It is very awesome: two ascending and two descending, found in the middle of the "Nothingness" <note>original: "Kelum," possibly a play on "Bli-mah."</note> for the One.

# And Wisdom, where shall it be found?

"From nothingness <note>original: "Me-Ayin." A play on the Sefirah of Keter, which is called 'Ayin' (Nothingness).</note> it shall be found." From where shall my light come to bequeath to those who love me "Substance" <note>original: "Yesh."</note>? It is from Him in His secret, for from there it separates and becomes. How mighty are their heads and all their host, and all their circles, and thus it is with them, the secret of the Sacrifice <note>original: "Korban."</note>, that a man should offer from the animals...

It emphasizes the unity of the Sefirot and the ultimate realization of "Peace" as a divine state where the Name becomes "full" and "complete."</summary>

<meta>This page continues the exploration of divine names and their practical and mystical applications, specifically transitioning from the general theory of letters to the specific derivations of the 42-letter and 72-letter names used in priestly service and the miracle of the Red Sea.</meta>

# The Great Book of Raziel

->A-B-G Y-T-Z; Q-R-E Sh-T-N; N-G-D Y-K-Sh; B-T-R Tz-T-G; Ch-K-B T-N-E; Y-G-L P-Z-Q; Sh-Q-V-Tz-Y-T<-
<note>This is the 42-letter name of God, often associated with the prayer 'Ana B'Koach.' It is presented here as a series of seven triplets.</note>

This name is holy and sanctified, and it is known through its permutations. It is derived from the first verse in the Torah, from the first letter *Bet* of "In the beginning" <note>original: *Bereshit*</note> until the *Bet* of the word "void" <note>original: *Bohu*. See Genesis 1:1-2.</note>. 

->And it is known that the entire Torah in its entirety consists of the names of the Holy One, Blessed be He; this is a Torah-based wonder, known and understood by the practitioners of Kabbalah.<-

->V-H-V Y-L-Y S-Y-T A-L-M M-H-Sh L-L-H A-K-A K-H-T H-Z-Y A-L-D L-A-V H-H-E Y-Z-L M-B-H H-R-Y H-Q-M L-A-V<-
->K-L-Y L-V-V P-H-L N-L-K Y-Y-Tz M-L-H Ch-H-V N-T-H H-A-A Y-R-T Sh-A-H R-Y-Y A-V-M L-K-B V-Sh-R Y-H-V L-H-Ch<-
->K-V-Q M-N-D A-N-Y Ch-E-M R-H-E Y-Y-Y H-H-H M-Y-K V-V-L Y-L-H S-A-L E-R-Y E-Sh-L M-Y-H V-H-V D-N-Y H-Ch-Sh E-M-M<-
->N-N-A N-Y-T M-B-H P-V-Y N-M-M Y-Y-L H-R-Ch M-Tz-R V-M-B Y-H-H E-N-V M-Ch-Y D-M-B M-N-Q A-Y-E Ch-B-V R-A-H<-
->Y-B-M H-Y-Y M-V-M<-
<note>This grid represents the 72 Names of God, each consisting of three letters. They are derived from Exodus 14:19-21, where each of the three verses contains exactly 72 letters.</note>

Know that all these names which are permutated here in the explanation of the secrets of these 72 letters are those from which the life of the "Tree of Life" <note>original: *Etz Chayim*</note> emerges for the prophets.

**God**, You have taught me from my youth, and until now I declare Your wonders. The Lord <note>original: *Y-H-V-H*</note>, the honored and awesome Name, is explicitly declared through these 72 names which emerged from the mouth of the High Priest <note>original: *Kohen Gadol*</note> in sanctity and purity, as it was properly received by him. This name emerges from the three well-known verses <note>Exodus 14:19-21, describing the parting of the Red Sea.</note> that emerge from the [Divine] Attribute. They all emerge through the way of grace, with a beginning and an end for the three letters in each name. These matters are very awesome and hidden, for they are the powers of the Supernal Realm <note>term: "Supernal Realm" (The higher spiritual dimensions or the world of Atzilut).</note> and awesome, exalted letters. Moses divided them into three very awesome verses according to a sublime and hidden secret as it was made known to him. Through them, the wise ones knew every sublime and hidden secret, containing the secret of the upper heavens and the earth, the tradition of the letters. 

They are divided into three groups, and their sign is: "I have set your twenty-four names" <note>Meaning 24 names per verse (24x3=72).</note>. But in the "Awesome Explanation," they are divided into ten awesome parts—seven in each part—and through them, they likened the entire secret of the "Supernal Exodus from Egypt." These are their matters: the secret of the Angel of God, the secret of the Clouds of Glory, the secret of the power of the sea and its roaring, and the secret of the "Speech" of Moses over the sea. They also contain the secret of the camp of Egypt and the camp of Israel, light and darkness, day and night, and the secret of the East Wind. All of these are awesome, supernal matters hinted at through the power of the secret of the ten Sefirot <note>term: "Sefirot" (The ten channels of divine creative vitality).</note>.

This is their explanation through a sublime and awesome secret, according to the secret of the thirty-two paths <note>original: *L-B Netivot*</note> which are explained in their deep mystery in their place. You will find that the "Angel of God" refers to the awesome supernal powers... encircling and surrounding Victory <note>term: "Netzach" (The seventh Sefirah, meaning endurance or victory).</note> and Splendor <note>term: "Hod" (The eighth Sefirah, meaning majesty or glory).</note> for "Old Israel" <note>A mystical name for the collective soul of the people or a specific spiritual configuration.</note>. The two pillars depart and rise, one from one power and one from another, until the place of their emanation: the cloud for light and the darkness for fire. Old Israel is surrounded by the Right hand, with Boaz <note>A reference to the pillars of the Temple, representing Strength.</note> within them. And the Night, which includes them all, shines from the light and darkens from the fire. By this, the "External Camp of Egypt" is separated from the pure powers attached to it. The emanation of the Night shall shine to give light to Israel on the right and on the left, and they give permission to one another. He called the dry land "Earth" and also the "Source of Water." And behold, it was very good. 

Moses commanded the Exalted One in His Victory <note>Netzach</note>, who struck them into seven streams of the ancient wilderness and the hidden holy power in every single being. All of these are the secrets of the awesome wonders of the power of the names that all emerge from the power of Understanding <note>term: "Binah" (The third Sefirah, associated with the Divine Mother and the source of differentiation).</note>, which brought them out to the light of the paths. The Clouds of Glory, the Sabbatical release, and every angel and supernal power belong to Him; they were all clarified in the secret of the "Supernal Egypt," from which come supernal and awesome powers—refined, hidden, clear, and pure. He restrained them and joined them, form within form and power within power, and included them in the secret of its circuits and its judgments, called the "Great Supernal Sanhedrin." Attached and included within it are 72 awesome supernal spirits, divided into three parts of 24 names each. Each part is a power unto itself. Furthermore, they are divided into ten parts, seven in each part, each one a sublime power unto itself. From each part come awesome and strong operations from the power of the joining of the names, their form, and their power, as explained in their places to the one who understands.

<margin>Furthermore</margin> they are divided into 72 matters from the words of the supernal angels and in the languages of the 72 names; every word and name is a sublime and awesome power unto itself. <margin>Furthermore</margin> they are divided into 303 <note>The text notes an alternative reading of 210.</note> refined forms—awesome, supernal, and hidden markings—from which come supernal powers, hosts upon hosts, ten classes of forms, letters, and supernal names. These are awesome, strong matters and supernal judgments, all called collectively the "Explicit Name" <note>original: *Shem HaMeforash*</note>. 

Everything is included and attached to the most honored Name among them, and in every power of theirs is "Y-H-V," from the names *Adonai*, *El*, *Elohim Chayim* (Living God), *Tzevaot* (Hosts), and all the titles seen and called in the Holy Tongue. All these mentioned powers are included in the name *Adonai*, which is emanated from the hidden and concealed Name—the "All" that is seen in its letters but not pronounced <note>Referring to the Tetragrammaton, Y-H-V-H, which is written but read as 'Adonai'.</note>. Thought, idea, knowledge, calculation, power, or imagination cannot grasp it—only the four awesome letters that are not pronounced, containing the secret of Unity through the secret of the ten Sefirot. From its connection to the unique Unity comes the absolute Unity. From them, everything mentioned is emanated; Blessed be He and Blessed be His Name. Since everything is emanated from the secret of the four elements <note>referring here to the four letters of the Name as spiritual elements.</note>—awesome, hidden, and concealed supernal letters containing the power of Unity—from them come the ten supernal Sefirot with all their operations and ways.
